\title{Publication-Coordinate Specifications for Generated Formal-Mathematics Artifacts}
\titlerunning{Publication-Coordinate Specifications}
\author{Haobo Ma\inst{1} \and Wenlin Zhang\inst{2}}

\institute{AELF PTE LTD. \email{auric@aelf.io} \and
National University of Singapore \email{e1327962@u.nus.edu}}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
This presentation-only paper gives a six-coordinate publication interface for
generated formal-mathematics artifacts.  The worked \emph{newmath}/\emph{automath}
instance is not a reviewer-executable artifact submission: its unconditional
public claim is the printed Horn calculation with no positive coordinate.  The
finite instance rows used in that projection--manifest rows, token rows, scanner
rows, digest rows, transcript rows, and ledger/frame rows--are displayed at the
start of Section~1.  Scanner success promotes only the byte-indexed
decoded-source witness defined there: the input is raw bytes, decoding is fixed,
and every accepted decoded token carries its unique raw-byte interval.  Bounded
replay record-gate adequacy is conditional on archive, digest, scanner, run,
frame, and script-conformance premises.
\end{abstract}

\section{Presentation route}
The six coordinate names form the finite typed alphabet
\[
\Coord=\{\mathsf{raw},\mathsf{qrgs},\mathsf{qsrc},\mathsf{qart},
\mathsf{qext},\mathsf{qven}\},\qquad
\Bool=\{\mathsf{false},\mathsf{true}\}.
\]
The associated Horn atoms are supplied by the fixed name map
\[
\begin{array}{lll}
\coordAtom(\mathsf{raw})=\qlex,&
\coordAtom(\mathsf{qrgs})=\qrgs,&
\coordAtom(\mathsf{qsrc})=\qsrc,\\
\coordAtom(\mathsf{qart})=\qart,&
\coordAtom(\mathsf{qext})=\qext,&
\coordAtom(\mathsf{qven})=\qven.
\end{array}
\]
For any Horn-closed evidence set \(E\), the declared publication vector is the
Boolean map
\[
V_E:\Coord\to\Bool,\qquad
V_E(c)=\mathsf{true}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \coordAtom(c)\in E .
\]
Equivalently, a displayed coordinate set \(S\subseteq\Coord\) denotes the
Boolean vector \(\chi_S\in\Bool^\Coord\) with \(\chi_S(c)=\mathsf{true}\)
exactly for \(c\in S\) and \(\chi_S(c)=\mathsf{false}\) otherwise.  Thus
\(\{\mathsf{raw}\}\) denotes the vector
\((\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})\) in the coordinate order below.  When the same
calculation is displayed by Horn atoms, \(\{\qlex\}\) is the atom-level image
\(\coordAtom[\{\mathsf{raw}\}]\), not a separate Boolean value and not a scanner
row predicate.
Displayed tuples use the coordinate order
\[
(\mathsf{raw},\mathsf{qrgs},\mathsf{qsrc},\mathsf{qart},\mathsf{qext},
\mathsf{qven})
\]
and are often printed by their corresponding Horn atoms
\[
(\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven).
\]
Equivalently, in the displayed coordinate order,
\[
V_E=(V_E(\mathsf{raw}),V_E(\mathsf{qrgs}),V_E(\mathsf{qsrc}),
V_E(\mathsf{qart}),V_E(\mathsf{qext}),V_E(\mathsf{qven}))
\in\Bool^6,
\]
or, in the plain-text notation used by the finite records,
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}l@{}}
\texttt{V\_E = (V\_E(raw), V\_E(qrgs), V\_E(qsrc),}\\
\texttt{V\_E(qart), V\_E(qext), V\_E(qven)) in B}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Here \(\Bool=\{\mathsf{false},\mathsf{true}\}\) and each component is evaluated
by the fixed name map above.
These six Boolean fields record raw
begin-token witnessing, bounded replay record-gate adequacy, fresh source
rebuild, artifact semantics, external archive equivalence, and venue acceptance.
The second coordinate keeps the legacy atom name \(\qrgs\) for compatibility with
the printed Stage--A certificate rows; throughout this paper its public reading
is record-gate adequacy, not a record-gate soundness theorem.

\label{tab:minimal-finite-horn-system}
\scriptsize
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.10\linewidth}Y L{0.13\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
Rule name & Body & Head \\ \midrule

\addlinespace

\addlinespace

\digestok(M)\wedge\noselfdigest(M)\wedge
\immutablelocator(A)\wedge\transcriptsok(A)\wedge\\

\end{array}\)

\begin{definition}[Decidable replay premises for Table~\ref{tab:minimal-finite-horn-system}]
\label{def:decidable-replay-premises-minimal}
For a finite archive record \(A\) and finite manifest \(M\), the replay premises
in Table~\ref{tab:minimal-finite-horn-system} are the following finite
decidable predicates.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \(\archivepresent(A)\): \(A\) is a finite archive record with locator
\(\operatorname{loc}(A)\) and digest \(H(A)\).
\item \(\ordinaryfilebyteeq(A,M)\): for every manifest row \((p,h)\in M\), the
current ordinary file bytes \(\operatorname{bytes}_{\mathrm{current}}(p)\) and
archived bytes \(\operatorname{bytes}_{\mathrm{archive}}(A,p)\) exist, are
ordinary non-symlink file bytes, and are equal.
\item \(\digestok(M)\): for every row \((p,h)\in M\),
\(\operatorname{SHA256}(\operatorname{bytes}_{\mathrm{current}}(p))=h\).
\item \(\noselfdigest(M)\): no path containing the digest table itself occurs in
the domain of \(M\).
\item \(\immutablelocator(A)\): \(\operatorname{loc}(A)\) is content-addressed,
for example by a Software Heritage identifier or by an archive DOI together with
a cryptographic digest of the exact archive bytes.
\item \(\transcriptsok(A)\): the finite checker transcripts named by the archive
exist as ordinary archived files, have the expected exit-status and output rows,
and their digest rows match \(M\).
\item \(\scriptsconform(A)\): the submitted script-conformance certificates
state, branch by branch, that the scripts read exactly the files in \(M\), use
the byte/digest conventions above, and emit precisely the atoms consumed by the
rule table.
\end{enumerate}
All seven predicates are finite table lookups, byte equalities, digest
equalities, or explicit certificate-row checks.  They do not assert source
rebuild, artifact semantics, external archive equivalence, or venue acceptance;
those stronger coordinates require the separate certificates named in
Rsrc--Rven.
In the compact table the short bodies abbreviate the exact certificate
predicates as follows:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\RsrcCert=\freshsourcecertificate,&
\RartCert=\artifactsemanticscertificate,\\
\RextCert=\externalarchivecertificate,&
\RvenCert=\venueacceptancecertificate.
\end{array}
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Least-closure calculation for the minimal table]
\label{prop:minimal-horn-least-closure}
Let \(\HornMin\) be Table~\ref{tab:minimal-finite-horn-system}, with coordinate
set \(Q=\{\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\}\) and no input coordinate atom.
Let \(A_{\emptyset}\) contain none of the rule bodies,
let \(A_{\mathrm{scan}}=A_{\emptyset}\cup
\{\scannerSuccess(B,\mathrm{Rows},\mathrm{Prefixes})\}\), and let
\[
\begin{aligned}
A_{\mathrm{replay}}=A_{\mathrm{scan}}\cup\{&
\archivepresent(A),\ordinaryfilebyteeq(A,M),\\
&\digestok(M),\noselfdigest(M),\immutablelocator(A),\\
&\transcriptsok(A),\scriptsconform(A)\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Assume the four promotion certificates
\(\RsrcCert,\RartCert,\RextCert,\RvenCert\) are absent from all three bases and
are not concluded by any rule in \(\HornMin\).  Then the least closures have
coordinate vectors
\[
A_{\emptyset}\mapsto(0,0,0,0,0,0),\qquad
A_{\mathrm{scan}}\mapsto(1,0,0,0,0,0),\qquad
A_{\mathrm{replay}}\mapsto(1,1,0,0,0,0)
\]
in the order \((\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Forward chaining starts from a base disjoint from \(Q\).  Rule R0 has body
\(\mathsf{false}\), so it never adds a coordinate.  In \(A_{\emptyset}\), no
remaining rule body is present; the least fixed point has empty \(Q\)-projection.
In \(A_{\mathrm{scan}}\), Rscan fires and adds \(\qlex\).  Rreplay is still
blocked by the seven missing replay predicates, and Rsrc--Rven are blocked by
their absent certificates, so no other coordinate can enter.  In
\(A_{\mathrm{replay}}\), Rscan again adds \(\qlex\), and every premise of
Rreplay is now present, so \(\qrgs\) is added.  The four certificate bodies for
Rsrc--Rven remain absent and no rule derives them.  Since Table~\ref{tab:minimal-finite-horn-system}
has no other coordinate-headed rule, the displayed vectors are exactly the
least-closure projections.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Byte-indexed scanner convention.}
The word ``raw'' in the first coordinate names the scanner input and the reported
offsets, not an unstructured byte-substring parser.  For a finite byte string
\(B\), the scanner is defined only when \(B\) decodes as \texttt{utf-8-sig}.
Write \(\delta(B)\in\{0,3\}\) for the consumed BOM length, put
\(X=B[\delta(B):]\), and let
\[
\operatorname{dec}_B:X\mapsto T=X.\operatorname{decode}(\texttt{utf-8})
\]
be the resulting decoded source string.  For each decoded character position
\(r\) in \(T\), define the raw-byte offset map
\[
\beta_B(r)=\delta(B)+\bigl|\operatorname{utf8}(T[0:r])\bigr|.
\]
Then \(\beta_B\) is strictly increasing on decoded character positions and is
therefore injective; every decoded interval \([r,s)\) has the unique raw-byte
interval \([\beta_B(r),\beta_B(s))\).  In particular the ASCII prefix
\auditcode{\\begin\{} has interval length seven, independent of any earlier
multibyte characters.  All scanner rows in Table~\ref{tab:current-instance-coordinate-rows}
are byte-indexed decoded-source rows in this sense.

\[
\boxed{\text{typed records}\Rightarrow\text{finite Horn rules}\Rightarrow
\bigl(\varnothing,\{\qlex\},\{\qlex,\qrgs\}\bigr)}
\]

\paragraph{Operative PDF record schemas and worked finite instance.}
This paragraph is the self-contained finite slice used by the operative PDF.
The row schemas are ordinary finite records: every required field is a key in a
finite row, every example below is an example value of that key, and no row is
promoted to a coordinate except through the printed Horn rules.

The same worked finite instance is calculated in the typed coordinate alphabet,
not by importing source semantics.  Let \(E_{\mathrm{pdf}}\) be the evidence
printed in the PDF base rows, \(E_{\mathrm{scan}}\) the scanner rows satisfying
the scanner lemma below, and \(E_{\mathrm{archive}},E_{\mathrm{digest}},
E_{\mathrm{transcript}},E_{\mathrm{script}}\) the four archive-side finite row
families required for replay.  The Actual closure calculation for the finite
instance is
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
E_{\mathrm{pdf}}&\mapsto&\varnothing,\\
E_{\mathrm{pdf}}\cup E_{\mathrm{scan}}&\mapsto&\{\mathsf{raw}\},\\
E_{\mathrm{pdf}}\cup E_{\mathrm{scan}}\cup E_{\mathrm{archive}}\cup
E_{\mathrm{digest}}\cup E_{\mathrm{transcript}}\cup E_{\mathrm{script}}
&\mapsto&\{\mathsf{raw},\mathsf{qrgs}\}.
\end{array}
\]
Under \(\coordAtom\), these three coordinate sets are exactly the Horn-atom
projections \(\varnothing\), \(\{\qlex\}\), and \(\{\qlex,\qrgs\}\).

\begin{lemma}[RawScanSoundness]
\label{lem:operative-raw-scan-soundness}
Lemma RawScanSoundness.  For every byte string \(B\), if
\(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) holds, then each accepted scanner row is generated from
the \texttt{utf-8-sig} decoded source by the literal begin-token pattern
\auditcode{\\begin\{e\}} for one of
\(\texttt{definition},\texttt{lemma},\texttt{proposition},\texttt{theorem},
\texttt{corollary}\), with no blank between \auditcode{\\begin} and the left
brace.  If the decoded match has interval \([r,s)\), the reported raw interval
is uniquely \([\beta_B(r),\beta_B(s))\).  Therefore a derived raw coordinate
\(\qlex\) means only byte-indexed decoded begin-token witnessing.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The scanner first fixes a single decoded source \(T\) from \(B\) using
\texttt{utf-8-sig}.  The map
\(\beta_B(r)=\delta(B)+|\operatorname{utf8}(T[0:r])|\) is strictly increasing
because every decoded UTF--8 character contributes a positive number of bytes.
Thus every decoded interval has one raw byte interval.  The accepted token
grammar is the displayed literal \auditcode{\\begin\{e\}} grammar, so the ASCII
prefix interval has length seven and cannot be confused with
\auditcode{\\begin \{e\}}, starred environments, macro-generated begins, or
included-source tokens.  Rule \(R1\) promotes only the finite scanner-success
atom; it does not add \TeX{} execution, source-completeness, or proof-checking
content.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite reference-checker soundness]
\label{lem:operative-reference-checker-soundness}
For the paper-local reference checker for a finite Horn table \(H\), base
\(A\), and coordinate set \(Q\),
\[
\operatorname{checker}(H,A,Q)=S\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\cap Q=S .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The checker maintains a finite set \(C\), initially \(A\), and repeatedly adds
the head of every rule whose finite body is included in \(C\).  Monotonicity and
finiteness imply termination at the least fixed point of the immediate
consequence operator for \(H\).  The checker returns exactly the elements of
that fixed point that lie in \(Q\), which is the displayed intersection.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[ClosedWorldExactness]
\label{lem:operative-closed-world-exactness}
Lemma ClosedWorldExactness.  Let \(H\) be the printed Horn table, let
\(Q=\{\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\}\), and let \(A\) be a finite base.
Assume \(A\cap Q=\varnothing\), the only \(Q\)-headed rules are
\(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\), no rule derives the four promotion premises
\(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\), and those four premises are
absent from \(A\).  Then \(\operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\cap Q\) is exactly the set
of heads among \(R1\) and \(R3\) whose printed bodies are contained in
\(\operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Forward-chain stages start with no coordinate atom.  By induction on the stage,
the four upgrade premises remain absent: they are absent initially and have no
deriving rule.  Hence \(R5,R6,R7b,R8\) cannot fire at any stage.  Since the
closed-world hypothesis lists all \(Q\)-headed rules, the only remaining ways to
enter \(Q\) are \(R1\) and \(R3\), proving both inclusion directions at the
least fixed point.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[ReplayClosure]
\label{thm:operative-replay-closure}
Theorem ReplayClosure.  If a finite replay record \(Z\) satisfies
\(\operatorname{ReplayCertOK}(Z)\), \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\), \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\),
\(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\), \(\RecordGateOK\), \(\CertDAGOK\),
\(\DigestOKstageA\), \(\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}\), and
\(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\), and if the replay vector is computed as
\[
\ReplayVector_i(Z)\Longleftrightarrow
q_i\in\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}
(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\setminus Q),
\]
then
\[
\ReplayVector(Z)=(\mathsf{true},\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})
\]
in the order
\((\mathsf{raw},\mathsf{qrgs},\mathsf{qsrc},\mathsf{qart},\mathsf{qext},
\mathsf{qven})\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The \(\ScanOK\) and replay-certificate premises put the five \(R1\) premises
and the ten \(R3\) premises in the framed replay base, so \(R1\) derives
\(\qlex\) and \(R3\) derives \(\qrgs\).  The frame \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) removes
initial coordinate facts, forbids hidden \(Q\)-headed rules, keeps the four
non-allowed promotion premises absent, and prevents identifying
\(\LocatorOKstageA\) with \texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}.  Applying
Lemma~\ref{lem:operative-closed-world-exactness} gives no further coordinates.
Mapping the two derived atoms back through \(\coordAtom\) gives exactly the
displayed Boolean vector.
\end{proof}

outer \(\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}\mapsto\)
\auditcode{a847f7656298ecf219b8082ed7b0b46d5a27a6fb61dd4b2510557c841cfbdfb2}.
Accepted digest rows contribute \(\texttt{finalDigest}\) and
\(\texttt{DigestOKstage\_a}\), never a coordinate atom.\\
\addlinespace

\(\texttt{R1\_inventory\_closure\_to\_qinv}\) with premises

\par\smallskip
Forward chaining these rows through \(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\) gives the branch
projections
\[
A_{\mathrm{rec}}\mapsto(0,0,0,0,0,0),\quad
A_{\mathrm{scan}}\mapsto(1,0,0,0,0,0),\quad
A^{+}\mapsto(1,1,0,0,0,0),
\]
in the coordinate order
\((\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)\).
\end{table}

\paragraph{Operative finite core.}
Table~\ref{tab:minimal-finite-horn-system} is the abstract six-coordinate Horn
system used to review the vector calculation.  Table~\ref{tab:current-instance-coordinate-rows}
is the complete finite row slice used for the current-instance projection.  Its
compiled \stageAprose{} article instance refines Rscan and Rreplay into the
following \(Q\)-headed Horn rules:
\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.07\linewidth}Y@{}}
R1 & \(\qlex\leftarrow\) \texttt{mainTex}, \texttt{invJson}, \texttt{invMd},
\texttt{finalDigest}, \texttt{scanOK}\\
R3 & \(\qrgs\leftarrow\) \texttt{stageAHornSchema}, \texttt{stageAManifest},
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate}, \texttt{stageAReplayReport}, \texttt{finalDigest},
\texttt{RecordGateOK}, \texttt{CertDAGOK}, \texttt{DigestOKstage\_a},
\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}, \texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}\\
R5--R8 & \(\qsrc\leftarrow\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\);
\(\qart\leftarrow\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\);
\(\qext\leftarrow\texttt{locatorOKstageA},\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\);
\(\qven\leftarrow\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\)
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
No other displayed rule concludes an atom of
\(Q=\{\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\}\), and no rule derives the four
upgrade premises for \(R5,R6,R7b,R8\).  Let \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\) contain the
printed source, inventory, digest, Stage--A manifest/certificate/report,
\(\RecordGateOK\), \(\CertDAGOK\), \(\DigestOKstageA\), and
\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}, but not \texttt{scanOK} or
\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}.  Put \(A_{\mathrm{scan}}=A_{\mathrm{rec}}\cup
\{\texttt{scanOK}\}\) and \(A^{+}=A_{\mathrm{scan}}\cup
\{\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\}\), with all three bases disjoint from \(Q\).

\begin{proposition}[Printed Horn closure calculation]
\label{prop:printed-horn-calculation-main}
For the least finite Horn closure of the rules above,
\[
\operatorname{Cl}(A_{\mathrm{rec}})\cap Q=\varnothing,\quad
\operatorname{Cl}(A_{\mathrm{scan}})\cap Q=\{\qlex\},\quad
\operatorname{Cl}(A^{+})\cap Q=\{\qlex,\qrgs\}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Rule \(R1\) is blocked exactly until \texttt{scanOK} is adjoined; \(R3\) is
blocked exactly until \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a} is adjoined.  The other four
\(Q\)-headed rules require upgrade premises absent from the three bases, and no
rule derives those premises.  Since no coordinate atom is initially present,
the three displayed intersections follow by forward chaining.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Replay predicate and frame.}
A later archive record \(Z\) can instantiate the replay branch only if the
finite predicate \(\operatorname{ReplayCertOK}(Z)\) holds.  In the notation of
Definition~\ref{def:decidable-replay-premises-minimal}, this means that the
archive object \(A\) and manifest \(M\) extracted from \(Z\) satisfy
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\archivepresent(A)\wedge\ordinaryfilebyteeq(A,M)\\
&{}\wedge\digestok(M)\wedge\noselfdigest(M)\wedge\immutablelocator(A)\\
&{}\wedge\transcriptsok(A)\wedge\scriptsconform(A).
\end{aligned}
\]
Equivalently: every required path has unique ordinary-file bytes, the current
and archived bytes agree row by row, every printed SHA--256 digest row is exact,
the digest table has no self-row, the locator supplies only
\(\LocatorOKstageA\), scanner and run transcript rows are accepted, and
branch-by-branch conformance rows are supplied for the Stage--A and certificate
checkers.  Its closed-world frame \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) additionally requires
\(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\cap Q=\varnothing\) before closure, no hidden
\(Q\)-concluding rule, absence of the four non-allowed upgrade premises, and no
identification of \(\LocatorOKstageA\) with
\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}.  These are finite membership and digest
checks, not consequences of merely printing a path.

\paragraph{Three coordinate statements.}

\begin{proposition}[Coordinate statement I: PDF base]
\label{prop:coordinate-statement-pdf-base}
Assume the following finite hypotheses.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The coordinate alphabet is \(Q=\{\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\}\),
in the displayed order.
\item The rule system is exactly the printed Horn system above: the only
\(Q\)-concluding rules are \(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\).
\item The base is exactly \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\), it is disjoint from \(Q\), and
it omits \texttt{scanOK}, \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}, and the four upgrade
premises
\(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\).
\item No displayed rule derives any of those four upgrade premises.
\end{enumerate}
Then the PDF-base coordinate vector is
\[
(\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)=(0,0,0,0,0,0).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
This is the first branch of
Proposition~\ref{prop:printed-horn-calculation-main}:
\(\operatorname{Cl}(A_{\mathrm{rec}})\cap Q=\varnothing\).  The displayed
hypotheses are the closed-world conditions used in that finite calculation.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Coordinate statement II: scanner-success branch]
\label{prop:coordinate-statement-scanner}
Assume the following finite hypotheses.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The four hypotheses of
Proposition~\ref{prop:coordinate-statement-pdf-base} hold for the same printed
Horn system and coordinate order.
\item A byte string \(B\), inventory byte strings \(I,J\), and digest table
\(D\) satisfy the explicit scanner premise \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\), contributing
the formal atom \texttt{scanOK}.
\item The scanner branch base is
\(A_{\mathrm{scan}}=A_{\mathrm{rec}}\cup\{\texttt{scanOK}\}\), remains disjoint
from \(Q\), and still omits \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}.
\end{enumerate}
Then the scanner-success coordinate vector is
\[
(\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)=(1,0,0,0,0,0).
\]
Equivalently, the coordinate set is \(\{\mathsf{raw}\}\subseteq\Coord\), whose
Boolean vector is
\[
(\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false}).
\]
The positive coordinate \(\qlex=\rawFirstBeginTokenSubset\) has only the
byte-indexed decoded-source meaning fixed by the scanner convention above: each
accepted begin-token prefix is a decoded-source substring with its unique
\(\beta_B\)-induced raw-byte interval.  It is not active \TeX{}, included-source
coverage, theorem-inventory coverage, or proof-checked mathematics.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The scanner premise contributes the formal atom \(\texttt{scanOK}\).  In the
printed Horn system the only rule concluding the raw-coordinate atom is the
typed promotion
\[
\qlex\leftarrow\texttt{scanOK}.
\]
Hence \(\qlex\in\operatorname{Cl}(A_{\mathrm{scan}})\).  This is a promotion
from a scanner-success atom to the coordinate atom; it is not the reflexive
identity of any scanner-row witness predicate.  By the first hypothesis, the
only rules with \(Q\)-heads are \(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\).  Rule \(R3\) is blocked
because \(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\notin A_{\mathrm{scan}}\); rules
\(R5,R6,R7b,R8\) are blocked because their four upgrade premises are absent and
no displayed rule derives them.  Since \(A_{\mathrm{scan}}\cap Q=\varnothing\),
no coordinate enters except by one of those rules.  Therefore
\[
\operatorname{Cl}(A_{\mathrm{scan}})\cap Q=\{\qlex\}.
\]
Using the coordinate map \(\coordAtom(\mathsf{raw})=\qlex\) and the characteristic
vector convention for subsets of \(\Coord\), this atom-level set is exactly the
coordinate set \(\{\mathsf{raw}\}\) and the Boolean vector
\((\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})\).  The meaning of the positive raw coordinate is
only the byte-indexed scanner convention stated above, under the
\(\operatorname{dec}_B,\beta_B\) map.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Coordinate statement III: framed replay branch]
\label{prop:coordinate-statement-framed-replay}
Let \(Z\) be a finite candidate replay record.  Assume the following finite
hypotheses.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The printed Horn system and coordinate order are those of
Proposition~\ref{prop:coordinate-statement-pdf-base}.
\item The scanner premise \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) holds and contributes
\texttt{scanOK}.
\item \(Z\) satisfies \(\operatorname{ReplayCertOK}(Z)\) and the framed archive
conditions used below: \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\), \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\), the checker/run
acceptance premises, \(\RecordGateOK\), \(\CertDAGOK\), \(\DigestOKstageA\),
\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}, and \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}.
\item The frame condition is closed-world for coordinates:
\(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\cap Q=\varnothing\) initially; no hidden
\(Q\)-concluding rule is available; the non-allowed promotion premises for
\(\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\) are absent; and
\(\LocatorOKstageA\) is not identified with
\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}.
\item \(\ReplayVector(Z)\) is evaluated only after applying the displayed Horn
closure to the framed replay base; raw coordinate atoms in \(\Facts(Z)\) are not
used as vector entries.
\end{enumerate}
Then the framed replay coordinate vector is exactly
\[
(\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)=(1,1,0,0,0,0).
\]
Extra non-coordinate archive facts are harmless only under \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\); the
argument does not replace \(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\) by \(A^{+}\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let
\[
\begin{aligned}
C_0&=\ReplayBase(Z),\\
C_{n+1}&=C_n\cup
\{h:\text{some displayed rule }h\leftarrow P
\text{ has }P\subseteq C_n\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
By the frame hypothesis, \(C_0\cap Q=\varnothing\).  The replay-certificate
hypotheses place the five \(R1\) premises, including \(\texttt{scanOK}\), in
\(C_0\), so \(R1\) adds \(\qlex\) at the first expansion stage.  They also place
the ten \(R3\) premises, including \(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\), in \(C_0\), so
\(R3\) adds \(\qrgs\).  Thus
\(\{\qlex,\qrgs\}\subseteq\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(\ReplayBase(Z))\).

It remains to prove that no other coordinate enters; this is the part supplied
by \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\), not by replacing \(\ReplayBase(Z)\) with \(A^{+}\).  We
show by induction on \(n\) that
\[
C_n\cap\{\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\}=\varnothing
\]
and that the four non-allowed promotion premises
\(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\) remain absent.  The claim holds
at \(n=0\) by the closed-world clauses of \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\).  In the induction
step, F2 says the only rules whose heads are in \(Q\) are \(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\).
Rules \(R1\) and \(R3\) can add only \(\qlex\) and \(\qrgs\).  Rules
\(R5,R6,R7b,R8\) require exactly the four absent promotion premises, with
\(R7b\) requiring \(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\) in addition to any
locator atom.  No displayed rule derives those promotion premises, and the
frame forbids treating non-coordinate support atoms as \(\qsrc,\qart,\qext\), or
\(\qven\).  Hence none of the four negative coordinates can be added at stage
\(n+1\).  At the finite fixed point the \(Q\)-projection is exactly
\(\{\qlex,\qrgs\}\), which is the displayed Boolean vector.
\end{proof}

\section{Finite checker route}
The object studied here is the publication routing of generated and
machine-maintained formal-mathematics artifacts, not the success rate or
mathematical creativity of the tools that produced them.  Proof assistants check
obligations; artifact and manuscript systems also need to decide which local
objects may support which publication claims.  Our pipeline therefore records
every accepted increment through six roles: source object, formal interface,
finite evidence row, advisory action, deterministic gate, and human promotion
boundary.  A theorem wrapper, review note, log, digest, or support file becomes
load-bearing only at the coordinate named by its finite gate.

This PDF-only article has no operative executable-artifact layer.  It is the route
paper: it prints the finite predicates, the coordinate calculation, and a
mathematical archive predicate.  It should therefore be read as a compact
specification/position contribution, not as a mathematical-software artifact
submission and not as a checker-package claim.  A byte-bound support
layer would require an immutable archive locator, the archive bytes, an
executable checker package or checked command transcript, and separate
branch-by-branch conformance proofs for any script treated as executable
evidence, in addition to the archive-format, ordinary-file, byte-equality, and
environment clauses printed below.  None of those operative artifact ingredients
is supplied here.  Path names, command lines, generated wrappers, ledgers,
digest rows, and script names are therefore evidence only through the printed
finite relations, not through unproved source-code behavior.

\paragraph{Non-instantiation convention.}
Throughout the paper, words such as ``case'', ``surface'', ``Stage--A'', and
``worked instance'' name finite mathematical records and roles inside the
specification.  They do not assert that the current submission contains a
reviewable artifact, immutable archive bytes, an executable checker
bundle, or a script-conformance proof.  Theorem~\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}
is consequently non-operative for the submitted PDF object: it states exactly
what a later archive-bearing submission would have to satisfy before a replay
claim could be cited.

\paragraph{Audit-string typography.}
Every path, command fragment, digest, code identifier, and literal token pattern that is part of a
byte-level audit row is typeset as an atomic monospaced audit string, not as
ordinary prose.  The paper uses \verb|\auditpath|, \verb|\artifactpath|, and
\verb|\auditcode| for such strings; these wrappers disable discretionary
URL/path breaks inside the token.  The prose name \stageAprose{} is only a
human-readable label.  The byte string \stageApath{} is a different object:
its underscore is literal, and the string is never to be read as ``stage a'' or
as a hyphenated prose phrase.

The normative token is the uninterrupted monospaced string shown by the source,
with no inserted blank, hyphen, slash, line-break character, or typographic
replacement character.  Thus \auditpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json},
\auditpath{str.splitlines()}, and \auditcode{forward_chain} must be read
literally; wrapping in adjacent prose or tables is only page layout and does not
insert bytes or change identifier characters.

\paragraph{Terminology.}
The paper uses the following four scope terms only in this finite-record sense.
\emph{Public surface} means the finite interface a reviewer is asked to read
as the paper's publication claim, here the printed Horn closure proposition: no
positive coordinate for the PDF-only base relation, one raw first-begin-token subset coordinate
only after the explicit scanner-success premise, and the replay coordinate only
under the archive and script-conformance premises.  \emph{Foreground} means membership in that
public surface; material outside it is ledger or artifact support, even when it
is mathematically useful.  A \emph{support coordinate} is an element of
\(\Coord\), evaluated by a Boolean map \(V_E:\Coord\to\Bool\), together with its
required finite input rows.  In displays, the coordinate order is the typed
order \((\mathsf{raw},\mathsf{qrgs},\mathsf{qsrc},\mathsf{qart},\mathsf{qext},
\mathsf{qven})\), printed through the Horn atoms
\((\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)\).  \emph{Current-byte} means the exact
bytes listed in the manifest and
digest table at the moment the replay script is run; later edits require a new
digest/replay tuple before the term applies again.  These terms do not add
evidence.  They are abbreviations for the displayed finite records, closure
rules, and coordinate projections.  Throughout the main text the first public
coordinate is \(\rawFirstBeginTokenSubset\), the raw first-begin-token subset
coordinate.  Legacy artifact spellings are confined to support-file
compatibility notes; the public mathematical meaning is byte-indexed
decoded-source witnessing under the fixed \(\texttt{utf-8-sig}\) decoding and
\(\beta\)-offset convention, not theorem-inventory coverage and not
displayed-\TeX{} semantics.

\caption{Public coordinate names used in theorem statements.}
\label{tab:notation-public-names}
\footnotesize

\toprule

\midrule

Raw first-begin-token subset coordinate: the finite
\(\rawBeginTokenSubset\) scanner over current \auditpath{main.tex} bytes and the
declared five literal \auditcode{\\begin\{...\}} tokens.  Its soundness
claim is only that accepted records come from matching decoded-source substrings
whose ASCII begin-token prefixes have the unique raw-byte intervals induced by
the scanner's decoding map.\\
\addlinespace
\(\qrgs\) &
Bounded fixed-replay record-gate adequacy coordinate for the \stageAprose{} Horn
schema, manifest atoms, certificate, replay report, digest rows,
\auditcode{qrgsReplayUpgrade}, and named
script-conformance premise.\\
\addlinespace

\addlinespace

In an archive-bearing submission, the exact ordinary-file byte strings named by

\paragraph{Presentation-route invariant.}
The unconditional object submitted as this article is a finite checker-boundary
specification: it prints the Horn rules, typed record predicates, digest
discipline, TCB assumptions, and archive contract.  Its unconditional PDF-level
claim is the printed finite specification and the Horn calculation
\[
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A_{\mathrm{rec}})\cap Q=\varnothing,
\]
equivalently
\[
(\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)
=(\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false}).
\]
It supplies only the paper-local reference checker for the printed Horn
calculation in Definition~\ref{def:finite-horn-reference-checker}.  It does not
assert a reviewer-executable archive replay or certificate-checker package,
because no DOI-bearing supplement, Software Heritage identifier,
venue-controlled supplement identifier, equivalent immutable locator, archive
byte set, full executable checker package, or script-conformance proof is
asserted in the PDF-only object.  Theorem~\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}
therefore has only a non-operative sufficient-condition reading in this
submission: if some byte object \(Z\) satisfies \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\) and the
external conformance premises \(\ScriptOKstageA\) and
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\), then the conditional replay relation adjoins
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) to obtain
\[
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A^+)\cap Q=\{\qlex,\qrgs\},
\]
equivalently the conditional vector
\[
(\mathsf{true},\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false}).
\]
The remaining scope boundaries are centralized in
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}; in particular, locator and byte-equality rows are
\(\LocatorOKstageA\) submission-boundary facts and do not by themselves promote a
stronger coordinate.

\paragraph{Byte-indexed decoded-source \(\rawBeginTokenSubset\) scanner for \(\qlex\).}
The \(\rawFirstBeginTokenSubset\) scanner is the following finite
\(\rawBeginTokenSubset\) function
of the submitted
\path{main.tex} bytes.  On input byte string \(b\), it first decodes \(b\) as
\texttt{utf-8-sig}; undecodable bytes are rejected.  Let
\(\delta(b)\in\{0,3\}\) be the length of the consumed UTF--8 BOM, so
\(\delta(b)=3\) exactly when \(b\) begins with bytes \texttt{EF BB BF}.  Let
\(x=b[\delta(b):]\) and let \(t=x.\operatorname{decode}(\texttt{utf-8})\).  For every
decoded character position \(r\) in \(t\), define
\[
\beta_b(r)=\delta(b)+\bigl|\operatorname{utf8}(t[0:r])\bigr|.
\]
Because UTF--8 encodes every decoded character by a positive number of bytes,
\(\beta_b\) is strictly increasing and hence injective.  Thus a decoded
character interval \([r,s)\) corresponds to the unique half-open byte interval
\([\beta_b(r),\beta_b(s))\) in the original byte string \(b\), with the BOM
included only through the additive offset.  This is the scanner's
interval-preservation convention: all substring claims are first claims about
decoded-source intervals, and their raw-byte offsets are exactly the image of
those intervals under \(\beta_b\).  Newlines are not normalized by this map:
decoded \texttt{\textbackslash r}, \texttt{\textbackslash n}, and
\texttt{\textbackslash r\textbackslash n} occupy exactly the byte intervals
determined by their decoded characters before line splitting.  The scanner then
applies Python \path{str.splitlines()} to \(t\) and processes the resulting
finite line list in order, carrying for each decoded line its start character
offset in \(t\).  If a match begins at column \(c\) on a line whose start offset
is \(r_0\), the ASCII prefix \auditcode{\\begin\{} has byte interval
\([\beta_b(r_0+c),\beta_b(r_0+c+7))\); because the prefix is ASCII, this
interval has length seven and is unique even when earlier characters are
multibyte.
For each line it records at most the first match of the following copyable
decoded-line Python definition over the UTF--8-sig decoded source:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}l@{}}
\auditcode{BeginPat_src = (}\\
\auditcode{    r"\\begin\{"}\\
\auditcode{    r"(definition|lemma|proposition|theorem|corollary)"}\\
\auditcode{    r"\}(?:\[([^\]]*)\])?"}\\
\auditcode{)}\\
\auditcode{BeginPat = re.compile(BeginPat_src)}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The source string is the displayed concatenation in order; no newline, blank,
hyphen, indentation byte, or other hidden byte is inserted into
\texttt{BeginPat\_src}.  The cursor semantics are part of the specification:
after a record is emitted, the scanner skips to the line after the matched end
line, so begin tokens inside the recorded body are not reconsidered as separate
top-level records.  Equivalently, the executable loop is
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}l@{}}
\auditcode{i = 0}\\
\auditcode{while i < len(lines):}\\
\auditcode{    m = BeginPat.search(lines[i])}\\
\auditcode{    if not m: i += 1; continue}\\
\auditcode{    e = m.group(1)}\\
\auditcode{    j = least k >= i with EndPat(e) in lines[k], else len(lines)-1}\\
\auditcode{    emit record for lines[i:j+1] using the first label in that slice}\\
\auditcode{    i = j + 1}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
No blank may occur between \path{\textbackslash begin} and the left brace, and
the only accepted environment names are the five displayed alternatives.  For a
recorded begin token with name \(e\), the scanner searches from that line forward
for the first line containing the literal end pattern
\(\texttt{\textbackslash\textbackslash end\textbackslash\{}e\texttt{\textbackslash\}}\).
The environment body is the source slice from the begin line through that first
end line, or through end of file if no matching end line is found.  The label
field is the first match in that slice of
\path+\\label\{([^}]*)\}+,
or is absent if there is no such match.  This gives a deterministic record list
and hence a count vector by environment name.  Formally,
\[
\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)
\]
holds for submitted \path{main.tex} bytes \(B\), inventory JSON bytes \(I\),
inventory Markdown bytes \(J\), and digest table \(D\) exactly when \(B,I,J\)
are ordinary digest-bound rows in \(D\), \(B\) decodes as above, every recorded
environment has a label, and no two records have the same label.  Define three
finite label sets: \(\LabelsJSON(I)\), parsed from the inventory JSON;
\(\LabelsMD(J)\), parsed from the inventory Markdown; and
\(\LabelsStatic(D)\), parsed from the digest-bound static source-label scan row
named by \(D\).  \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) additionally requires exact agreement
\[
\LabelsJSON(I)=\LabelsMD(J)=\LabelsStatic(D)
\]
and then requires
\[
\DetectedLabels(B)\subseteq\LabelsJSON(I),
\]
where \(\DetectedLabels(B)\) is the label set emitted by the cursor algorithm
above.  There is no priority order, fallback convention, or union of the three
sources.  The predicate is finite because all four byte strings are finite and
the regular-expression scan, label extraction, set-uniqueness test, equality
tests, and containment test are bounded by their lengths.  Thus \(\qlex\)
accepts only through this byte-indexed decoded-source
\(\ScanOK\), equivalently through the named \(\rawBeginTokenSubset\)
predicate, not merely through the names \texttt{invJson}, \texttt{invMd}, and
\texttt{finalDigest}.  The positive coordinate name is deliberately
\(\rawFirstBeginTokenSubset\): it witnesses only matching first decoded
begin-token substrings, with the unique raw-byte interval for the ASCII token
prefix induced by \(\beta_b\),
not theorem-inventory coverage.  The scanner never strips comments, expands macros,
follows \path{\textbackslash input}, evaluates conditionals, balances nested
environments, or validates proof text.  Its exact guarantee is therefore a
byte-indexed decoded begin-token substring subset check: every accepted record
is generated by an accepted decoded source substring matching the grammar above,
and the ASCII prefix \auditcode{\\begin\{} has the determinate raw-byte interval
given by \(\beta_B\).  No theorem in this paper
identifies that substring set with displayed \TeX{} content.  Matching
substrings in comments or inactive branches are deliberate false positives
relative to \TeX{} execution, and macro-generated, included, nested, or
second-same-line begin tokens are deliberate false negatives relative to
\TeX{} execution.  Therefore
\path{\textbackslash begin\{lemma\}\textbackslash label\{lem:x\}\textbackslash end\{lemma\}}
is a positive one-record example; the same decoded substring after a percent sign
or inside an inactive conditional branch is still counted.  By contrast
\path{\textbackslash begin \{lemma\}}, \path{\textbackslash begin\{remark\}},
\path{\textbackslash begin\{lemma*\}}, macro-generated begin tokens,
\path{\textbackslash input}-included begin tokens, begin tokens nested inside the
recorded body, and a second begin token later on the same source line are not
additional accepted begin records under this scanner grammar.

\begin{lemma}[Scanner regular expression is fixed]
\label{lem:scanner-regex-compiles}
The exact scanner source used by \(\ScanOK\) is the displayed concatenation
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}l@{}}
\auditcode{BeginPat_src = (}\\
\auditcode{    r"\\begin\{"}\\
\auditcode{    r"(definition|lemma|proposition|theorem|corollary)"}\\
\auditcode{    r"\}(?:\[([^\]]*)\])?"}\\
\auditcode{)}\\
\auditcode{BeginPat = re.compile(BeginPat_src)}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Here \texttt{BeginPat\_src} is the concatenation, in the displayed order, of the
three raw Python string literals above; no newline, blank, hyphen, indentation
byte, or other hidden byte is inserted into \texttt{BeginPat\_src}.  Under
Python's \texttt{re} grammar it compiles, and its optional bracket-argument
clause is the non-capturing group \auditcode{(?:\[([^\]]*)\])?} followed by a
single capturing group for the bracket contents.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The raw-string prefix makes backslashes ordinary regular-expression bytes, the
brace escapes match literal braces, the middle literal is the finite
environment-name alternative, and \auditcode{(?:} is Python's non-capturing-group
opener.  Because the source string is exactly the displayed three-literal
concatenation, the displayed bytes form the well-formed \texttt{re.compile} input
used by \(\ScanOK\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[RawScanSoundness]
\label{thm:raw-begin-token-witness-soundness}
For every byte string \(B\), inventory byte strings \(I,J\), and digest table
\(D\), if \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) and \(R\) is any record emitted by the
\(\rawBeginTokenSubset\) scan of the decoded \path{main.tex} byte string \(B\),
then there is a contiguous decoded source slice \(s_R\) of the UTF--8-sig decoded string, beginning at the exact decoded token pattern
\[
\auditcode{\\begin\{e\}}
\quad
e\in\{\texttt{definition},\texttt{lemma},\texttt{proposition},
\texttt{theorem},\texttt{corollary}\},
\]
with no blank between \texttt{\textbackslash begin} and \texttt{\{}, and the ASCII characters in that begin-token prefix correspond to a unique half-open byte interval of \(B\).  The token may be
possibly followed by one bracketed title argument, such that \(R\)'s environment
name, optional title, body slice, and first label field are exactly the fields
obtained from \(s_R\) by the scanner described above.  Consequently
\(\rawFirstBeginTokenSubset\) is sound only as a first byte-indexed
decoded-source-substring subset witness: every accepted record has a matching
decoded source substring, and its ASCII-token prefix has the unique raw-byte
interval induced by \(\beta_B\).
Later coordinate claims that cite this scanner result must cite this lemma and
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}, not the certificate-record theorem.
Substrings in comments or inactive branches may be accepted, while included,
nested, macro-generated, and second-same-line begin tokens may be missed.  This
lemma makes no claim that \(s_R\) is active \TeX{} input, displayed theorem
content, an included file record, a balanced environment, a theorem-inventory
row, or proof-checked mathematics.

For the associated Boolean coordinate, let
\(\scannerSuccess(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram)\) mean that \(\RawProgram\) is the
displayed scanner program, the existing \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) contract holds for
some bound \(I,J,D\), and \(\RawRows\) is exactly the finite list of rows emitted
by that run.  Let
\[
\RawEvidence(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram)=
\{\texttt{scanOK}\}\cup
\{\,\rawBeginTokenWitness(r):r\in\RawRows\,\}
\]
when \(\scannerSuccess(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram)\) holds, and leave it undefined
otherwise.  Let
\(\operatorname{Cl}\) be the one-step coordinate closure generated by the typed
rule
\[
\qlex\leftarrow \texttt{scanOK}
\]
on this scanner evidence, and define
\[
\piRaw(S)=
\begin{cases}
\mathsf{true},&\qlex\in S,\\
\mathsf{false},&\qlex\notin S.
\end{cases}
\]
Then the scanner-success implication is the typed Boolean projection statement
\[
\scannerSuccess(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram)\Longrightarrow
\piRaw\bigl(\operatorname{Cl}(\RawEvidence(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram))\bigr)
=\mathsf{true}.
\]
Here \(\qlex\) is the raw public-coordinate atom and
\(\rawBeginTokenWitness(r)\) is an evidence predicate on a scanner row; the two
names are not identified.  The proof-critical rule is the implication
\(\texttt{scanOK}\Rightarrow\qlex\), equivalently the Horn rule
\(\qlex\leftarrow\texttt{scanOK}\), and never a tautology such as
\(\rawBeginTokenWitness(r)=\rawBeginTokenWitness(r)\).  The corresponding
six-coordinate vector is obtained only after applying the
\(S\subseteq\Coord\mapsto\chi_S\) convention from Section~1.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By definition of \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\), the bytes \(B\) decode as
\texttt{utf-8-sig} and the scanner processes the finite list returned by
\auditpath{str.splitlines()}.  A record is emitted only when the fixed compiled
regular expression of Lemma~\ref{lem:scanner-regex-compiles} has a match on a
processed line.  Such a match is, by the displayed regular expression, exactly a
literal decoded \auditcode{\\begin\{} token, one of the five listed environment
names, a literal right brace, and possibly one bracketed title argument.  The
scanner then takes the source slice from that begin line through the first later
line containing the corresponding literal end pattern, or through end of file if
no such line exists, and assigns the first label match in that slice.  These are
precisely the fields of \(R\).  The construction uses no \TeX{} expansion,
comment stripping, conditional evaluation, include traversal, nesting balance,
inventory semantics, or proof checker.  Therefore the only soundness conclusion
available from the emitted record is existence of the stated decoded source
substring, the corresponding \(\beta_B\)-induced raw-byte interval for the ASCII
begin token, and scanner-derived fields.
For the Boolean-coordinate part,
\(\scannerSuccess(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram)\) includes the existing
\(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) contract and makes
\(\RawEvidence(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram)\) defined with the typed atom
\(\texttt{scanOK}\) in it.  The displayed closure rule therefore adds the
coordinate atom \(\qlex\) to
\(\operatorname{Cl}(\RawEvidence(B,\RawRows,\RawProgram))\), and the definition
of \(\piRaw\) returns \(\mathsf{true}\).  The witness predicates
\(\rawBeginTokenWitness(r)\) justify the byte-indexed decoded-source records
above, but no witness predicate is used as the coordinate name or as the Boolean
value.  Thus scanner success supplies an evidence atom, the Horn rule derives
the coordinate atom, and the characteristic-vector convention turns the
coordinate set \(\{\mathsf{raw}\}\) into the Boolean vector
\((\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})\).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Public coordinate surface.}
For this PDF-only article the public coordinate surface has one foreground
printed calculation: Proposition~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}, together with the
certificate-record and archive-contract theorems below.  When the non-PDF
archive contract of Section~\ref{sec:replay-artifact-boundary} is supplied, the
conditional byte-bound replay reading is available only under the same explicit
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) and certificate-script conformance premises.  That
coordinate surface has \(\{\qlex,\qrgs\}\) only under those premises and under
the explicit \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) scanner premise, with \(\qlex\) carrying only
the byte-indexed decoded-source meaning of Lemma~\ref{thm:raw-begin-token-witness-soundness}
and Table~\ref{tab:scope-register};
the unconditional base coordinate set is empty.  The four upgrade coordinates and the support status of
certificate-record material are recorded in Table~\ref{tab:scope-register};
Richardson, Zeckendorf, orbit, height-transfer, and cover material is routed to
the non-load-bearing supplement.

\paragraph{Reusable route schema.}
The reusable design pattern is not the surrounding project history.  It is the
following finite route from a proposed public assertion to a bounded publication
claim:
\begin{equation}
\tag{RouteSchema}
\begin{array}{c}
\text{proposed public assertion }c\\
\downarrow\\
\text{claim-kind set }K\text{ and declared coordinate vector }Q_c\\
\downarrow\\
\text{finite evidence surface }E=(E_{\mathrm{atom}},E_{\mathrm{path}})\\
\downarrow\\
\text{gate rows }R\text{ with }\PointerOK_{\CertTuple}(u,E)\text{ evidence pointers}\\
\downarrow\\
\text{human or route boundary }H_{\mathrm{route}}\text{ and command boundary }B\\
\downarrow\\
\text{accepted finite predicate }\Acc_{\Gamma}(c,C)\text{ and public claim}
\end{array}
\end{equation}
Each arrow is a finite projection: it either records an explicit coordinate or
rejects a stronger reading that lacks the required coordinate.  The schema is
therefore portable across artifact-producing projects, while every instantiated
claim remains relative to its own manifest \(\ReviewManifest\), submitted
surface \(\mathit{Surf}\), gate dispatch table \(\Dispatch\), and certificate
record \(C_{\mathrm{rec}}=(K,\ell,E,H_{\mathrm{route}},R,D_{\mathrm{row}},B_{\mathrm{row}})\).  It
does not say that a generated artifact is mathematically correct merely because a
record exists; it says which public reading the record is allowed to support.

\begin{table}
\caption{Finite schema predicates used by the route and certificate interfaces.}
\label{tab:route-schema-predicates}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.28\linewidth}Y@{}}
\toprule
Predicate & Finite domain and acceptance condition \\
\midrule
\(\operatorname{ReqEntryFields}(\Sigma)\) & The finite schema field set required of every ledger entry:
\(\{\texttt{id},\texttt{name},\texttt{kind},\texttt{level},\texttt{evidence},
\texttt{passes}\}\), plus any schema-declared key in
\(\Sigma.\operatorname{entryRequired}\).  Acceptance is set inclusion in the
parsed row domain; no default value is inserted.\\
\addlinespace
\(\operatorname{ReqPassFields}(\Sigma)\) & The finite schema field set required of every pass row:
\(\{\texttt{gate},\texttt{decision},\texttt{level},\texttt{pointer}\}\), plus any
schema-declared key in \(\Sigma.\operatorname{passRequired}\).  Acceptance is exact
presence of these keys before evaluating the pointer predicate.\\
\addlinespace
\(\operatorname{KindDomain}(\Sigma)\) & The finite set printed in
\(\Sigma.\operatorname{kinds}\), restricted here to the route kinds
\(\textsf{lexical}\), \(\textsf{replay}\), \(\textsf{source}\),
\(\textsf{artifact}\), \(\textsf{archive}\), \(\textsf{venue}\), and
\(\textsf{discovery}\).  A row accepts only when its nonempty kind set is a subset
of this finite domain.\\
\addlinespace
\(\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(\Snapshot)\) & The finite set of snapshot-boundary
strings in the parsed table \(\Snapshot\).  A string is accepted only if it is an exact
member of \(\Snapshot\)'s key set and the corresponding row is marked eligible for the
current submission surface; filesystem existence is not queried.\\
\addlinespace
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_2(e)\) & The path-verified boundary:
entry \(e\) has no command transcript treated as evidence, every path evidence
string is accepted by \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_{\Digest}\), and every pass row points
through \(\PointerOK_{\CertTuple}\).  It accepts finite records only; it does not run a
command.\\
\addlinespace
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_3(e)\) & The command-run boundary:
entry \(e\) has a finite command string, working-directory token, exit-status
field, transcript pointer, and digest-bound output pointer, all accepted by
\(\PointerOK_{\CertTuple}\) or \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_{\Digest}\).  It records a run row but
still requires a separate \(\ScriptOK\) premise before script conformance becomes
a theorem premise.\\
\addlinespace
\(\Acc_{\Gamma}(c,C)\) & The finite acceptance predicate for claim \(c\) with
certificate record \(C\): every kind of \(c\) is in
\(\operatorname{KindDomain}(\Sigma)\), the pass rows of \(C\) are in bijection with
\(\bigcup_{k\in\operatorname{Kind}(c)}\Gamma(k)\), every decision is accepted by
\(\Sigma.\operatorname{passDecisions}\), and every pointer satisfies
\(\PointerOK_{\CertTuple}\).  It is false if a required row is missing, duplicated, or
outside the dispatch table.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

\begin{figure}
\centering
\small
\begin{tabularx}{0.96\linewidth}{@{}L{0.23\linewidth}Y@{}}
\toprule
Schema coordinate & Portable meaning \\ \midrule
Public coordinate & The exact assertion being offered to the reader, such as a
decoded begin-token subset check, replayed finite closure, fresh source build,
artifact-semantic validation, archive identity, or venue state.\\
Evidence surface & A finite typed list of non-path atoms and digest-bound paths;
arbitrary files under the working directory are not promoted by existence.\\
Gate row & A deterministic pass/fail row whose pointer satisfies
\(\PointerOK\) against the typed evidence, manifest rows, table labels, or
submitted snapshot rows.\\
Boundary row & A human-promotion, route, command-run, archive, or venue boundary
that states which stronger readings are still absent.\\
Checker result & A finite accept/reject vector for the declared coordinates,
not a semantic theorem about unlisted artifacts or live services.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\caption{General publication-coordinate schema used by this paper.}
\label{fig:general-route-schema}
\end{figure}

As a non-self-referential instance, consider a separate paper whose appendix is a
program-generated table of \(500\) small graph obstructions.  A conservative
publication-coordinate certificate could assert only
\[
(\text{appendixTableLexicalCoverage},\text{tableDigestReplay})=(\mathsf{true},
\mathsf{true})
\]
and leave
\(\text{graphIsomorphismMinimality}\), \(\text{freshGeneratorRun}\), and
\(\text{archiveUpload}\) false.  Its evidence surface would name the appendix
\TeX{} file, the generator output CSV, a digest table for those two paths, and a
gate row saying that every displayed obstruction row has a matching CSV row under
the recorded SHA--256 values.  That certificate would let the article say
``the displayed table is byte-linked to the submitted generator output''; it
would not let the article say that the obstruction list is complete, minimal, or
freshly regenerated unless new finite rows for those stronger coordinates were
added.  This graph-table example is not an input to the current replay theorem;
it illustrates the schema in Figure~\ref{fig:general-route-schema} outside the
present package.
