\begin{definition}[Finite Horn reference checker]
\label{def:finite-horn-reference-checker}
For a finite Horn system \(H\), finite base \(A\), and finite coordinate set
\(Q\), define \(\operatorname{Check}_{H,Q}(A)\) as follows.  First reject if
\(A\cap Q\ne\varnothing\).  Otherwise set
\[
C_0=A,\qquad
C_{n+1}=C_n\cup
\{h:\text{some rule }h\leftarrow P\text{ in }H\text{ has }P\subseteq C_n\}.
\]
Because \(H\) and \(A\) are finite, this monotone iteration reaches a first
fixed point \(C_N=C_{N+1}\).  The checker returns
\[
\operatorname{Check}_{H,Q}(A)=C_N\cap Q
\]
in the displayed coordinate order.  The paper-local reference implementation
\artifactcmd{python}{certificates/stage_a_horn_closure_checker.py} hard-codes
exactly the printed \(H_{\mathrm{Horn}}\), \(Q\), and the three branch bases
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}},A^{\mathrm{scan}},A^{+}\).  Running it on all three branches
returns the JSON projections
\[
\texttt{A\_rec}:\varnothing,\qquad
\texttt{A\_scan}:\{\texttt{q\_raw}\},\qquad
\texttt{A\_plus}:\{\texttt{q\_raw},\texttt{qrgs}\}.
\]
Here \texttt{q\_raw} is the checker spelling of the public coordinate
\(\qlex\), whose meaning is the raw first-begin-token subset witness described
above.
This checker is a small reference witness for the printed finite calculation.
It does not parse \TeX{}, prove \(\ScanOK\), prove script conformance, or
instantiate the archive contract.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Reference-checker soundness]
\label{lem:finite-horn-reference-checker-soundness}
For any finite \(H,A,Q\), if
\(\operatorname{Check}_{H,Q}(A)=S\), then
\[
\operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\cap Q=S.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The checker iteration is exactly the standard least fixed-point construction
for finite Horn closure.  Monotonicity gives \(C_n\subseteq C_{n+1}\) for every
\(n\), and finiteness implies termination at some \(C_N\).  By construction,
every atom added to \(C_{n+1}\) is justified by a rule whose premises are already
in \(C_n\), so \(C_N\subseteq \operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\).  Conversely, \(C_N\)
is closed under every rule of \(H\), so the least closed superset
\(\operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\) is contained in \(C_N\).  Hence
\(C_N=\operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\), and projecting both sides to \(Q\) gives the
returned set \(S\).  The initial \(A\cap Q=\varnothing\) rejection is the
closed-world coordinate frame used by the proposition below; it prevents a
positive coordinate from being smuggled in as an input atom.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Closed-world Horn exclusion]
\label{lem:closed-world-horn-exclusion}
Let \(H\) be a finite Horn system, \(A\) a finite base, \(U\) a set of queried
atoms, and \(M\) a set of missing premise atoms.  Suppose
\[
A\cap U=\varnothing,\qquad A\cap M=\varnothing,
\]
no rule of \(H\) has head in \(M\), and every rule of \(H\) whose head lies in
\(U\) has at least one premise in \(M\).  Then
\[
\operatorname{Cl}_{H}(A)\cap U=\varnothing .
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let \(C_0=A\) and let \(C_{n+1}\) be the usual one-step Horn expansion of
\(C_n\).  We prove \(C_n\cap M=C_n\cap U=\varnothing\) for all \(n\).  This is
true at \(n=0\).  If it is true at \(n\), then no atom of \(M\) can be newly
added, because no rule has head in \(M\).  Also no atom of \(U\) can be newly
added: every \(U\)-headed rule needs a premise in \(M\), and no such premise is
present in \(C_n\).  Thus the invariant holds at \(n+1\).  At the finite fixed
point \(C_N=\operatorname{Cl}_H(A)\), the queried atoms in \(U\) are still
absent.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Printed Horn closure calculation]
\label{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}
Let
\[
Q=\{\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\}
\]
be the six-coordinate support alphabet.  This proposition separates three finite
objects.  The only unconditional PDF-level object is the base branch
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\): it consists only of the digest-bound file atoms, finite
checker atoms, and human-scoped support atoms displayed below.  It does not
contain the scanner-success atom \(\texttt{scanOK}\), any script-conformance
atom, or any archive-instantiation atom.  The scanner branch is a different
formal object obtained only by adjoining \(\texttt{scanOK}\), the atom supplied
by the external premise \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\).  Write
\[
A^{\mathrm{scan}}=A_{\mathrm{rec}}\cup\{\texttt{scanOK}\};
\qquad
A_{\mathrm{rec}}\cap\{\texttt{scanOK}\}=\varnothing.
\]
The script-conformance atom \(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\) is not a member of
\(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\); it is an external branch-by-branch premise which may be
adjoined, after the scanner premise, to form the replay branch
\[
A^{+}=A^{\mathrm{scan}}\cup\{\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\};
\qquad
A^{\mathrm{scan}}\cap\{\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\}=\varnothing.
\]
In all three branches the input side is closed-world on public coordinates:
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\cap Q=A^{\mathrm{scan}}\cap Q=A^{+}\cap Q=\varnothing\).
Thus every positive coordinate below must be produced by one of the displayed
Horn rules, not by a pre-existing coordinate fact.
The equality between the artifact bytes
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_horn_schema.json},
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json}, and these displayed objects is
not inferred from the PDF; it is a separate digest-bound parser/compilation
claim that belongs to the archive-instantiated reading, not to the PDF-only
base relation.
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{0.94\linewidth}{@{}L{0.12\linewidth}Y@{}}
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}=\) &
\texttt{pubAbs}, \texttt{suppMain}, \texttt{invJson}, \texttt{invMd},
\texttt{finalDigest}, \texttt{mainTex},
\texttt{stageAHornSchema}, \texttt{stageAManifest},
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate}, \texttt{stageAReplayReport},
\texttt{RecordGateOK}, \texttt{CertDAGOK}, \texttt{DigestOKstage\_a},
\texttt{srcInterface}, \texttt{caseArtifacts}, \texttt{venueReadiness},
\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}.
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
For this theorem, the names are formal atoms and do not import any unprinted
support ledger, source semantics, artifact semantics, or source-code
implementation conformance.  Let \(H_{\mathrm{Horn}}\) be the following finite
Horn system:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{0.94\linewidth}{@{}L{0.10\linewidth}Y@{}}
R0 & \(\texttt{singleRouteSurface}\leftarrow
\texttt{pubAbs},\texttt{suppMain}\).\\
R1 & \(\qlex\leftarrow
\texttt{mainTex},\texttt{invJson},\texttt{invMd},\texttt{finalDigest},\texttt{scanOK}\).\\
R2 & \(\texttt{localInventoryClosed}\leftarrow\qlex\).\\
R3 & \(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}
\qrgs\leftarrow
\texttt{stageAHornSchema},\texttt{stageAManifest},\\
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate},\texttt{stageAReplayReport},\\
\texttt{finalDigest},\texttt{RecordGateOK},\texttt{CertDAGOK},\\
\texttt{DigestOKstage\_a},\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a},\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}.
\end{array}\)\\
R4 & \(\texttt{boundedReplayRecordGateAdequacy}\leftarrow\qrgs\).\\
R5 & \(\texttt{qsrc}\leftarrow\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\).\\
R6 & \(\texttt{qart}\leftarrow\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\).\\
R7a & \(\texttt{locatorOKstageA}\leftarrow
\texttt{stableLocator},\texttt{archiveByteEquality}\).\\
R7b & \(\texttt{qext}\leftarrow
\texttt{locatorOKstageA},\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\).\\
R8 & \(\texttt{qven}\leftarrow\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\).\\
R9 & \(\texttt{boundedSourceInterface}\leftarrow\texttt{srcInterface}\).\\
R10 & \(\texttt{boundedArtifactRows}\leftarrow\texttt{caseArtifacts}\).\\
R11 & \(\texttt{datedVenueReadiness}\leftarrow\texttt{venueReadiness}\).\\
R12 & \(\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}
\texttt{routeSurfaceQuotient}\leftarrow
\texttt{pubAbs},\texttt{suppMain},\\
\texttt{stageAHornSchema},\texttt{stageAHornCertificate},\\
\texttt{stageAReplayReport},\texttt{invJson},\texttt{invMd},\\
\texttt{finalDigest}.
\end{array}\)
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
No other Horn rule has a conclusion in \(Q\).  Equivalently, the reference
checker of Definition~\ref{def:finite-horn-reference-checker}, which is sound by
Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-horn-reference-checker-soundness}, returns exactly the
three \(Q\)-projections displayed below; the checker is run on precisely the
displayed bases and rule table and rejects any base with \(A\cap Q\ne\varnothing\).
Thus forward chaining gives the
unconditional PDF-level closure
\[
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A_{\mathrm{rec}})\cap Q
=\varnothing.
\]
If the explicit scanner premise \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) holds and contributes the
formal atom \(\texttt{scanOK}\), forward chaining gives
\[
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A^{\mathrm{scan}})\cap Q
=\{\qlex\}.
\]
Only after also adjoining the external branch-by-branch conformance premise,
\[
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A^{+})\cap Q
=\{\qlex,\qrgs\}.
\]
The latter two displays are conditional closure statements over enlarged formal
bases.  They are not unconditional assertions that the submitted PDF has proved
scanner success, script conformance, or archive replay.  Equivalently, before
the scanner premise is adjoined the unconditional PDF-level digest/file-record
relation has coordinate vector
\[
\qlex=\qrgs=\qsrc=\qart=\qext=\qven=\mathsf{false},
\]
and that branch contains no legacy scanner-coordinate derivation.  The scanner branch
\(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\) has \(\qlex=\mathsf{true}\) only in the raw-substring sense of
Lemma~\ref{thm:raw-begin-token-witness-soundness} and
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}, with all other coordinates
false.  The replay-conditional relation over \(A^{+}\) has coordinate vector
\[
\qlex=\qrgs=\mathsf{true},\qquad
\qsrc=\qart=\qext=\qven=\mathsf{false}.
\]
Here \(\rawFirstBeginTokenSubset\) is read only through the raw begin-token
witness scanner above, Lemma~\ref{thm:raw-begin-token-witness-soundness}, and
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}.  Thus \(\qrgs\) is a replay-conditional bounded
fixed-replay record-gate-adequacy coordinate, not an unconditional coordinate
of the digest/file-record closure.  The branch-labelled coordinate sets are:
empty for the unconditional PDF-level branch \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\), \(\{\qlex\}\)
only for the scanner branch \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\), and \(\{\qlex,\qrgs\}\) only
for the replay branch \(A^{+}\).  Thus any positive \(\qlex\) display in this
proposition is scanner-conditional and is not a PDF-only conclusion about
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\).  A later archive-replay citation of the
positive \(\qrgs\) coordinate would additionally require \(\ArchiveOKA\),
\(\ScriptOKstageA\), \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\), immutable archive bytes, and
a stable locator.  The certificate-record
specification is support for this interface.  Richardson obstruction,
Zeckendorf transport, orbit/height material, and auxiliary-cover stress tests
are non-load-bearing supplement material unless a later record adds their own
positive coordinate and gate rows.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
This proposition is the displayed mathematical layer: \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\),
\(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\), \(A^{+}\), and \(H_{\mathrm{Horn}}\) are the finite sets and finite Horn
system printed in the statement.  Since the three input bases have empty
intersection with \(Q\), coordinate membership can enter only through a displayed
rule conclusion.  Forward chaining is monotone, so it is enough
to inspect the displayed rules whose conclusions lie in \(Q\), namely
\(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\).

First consider \(\qlex\).  Rule \(R1\) is the only displayed rule concluding
\(\qlex\).  It does not apply over \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\), because one of its five
premises is \(\textsf{scanOK}\), and that atom is not in
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\).  If \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) holds, adjoining the formal atom
\(\textsf{scanOK}\) forms \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\), where all five premises of
\(R1\) are present; hence
\(\qlex\in\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A^{\mathrm{scan}})\).

Next consider \(\qrgs\).  Rule \(R3\) is the only displayed rule concluding
\(\qrgs\).  It does not apply over either \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\) or
\(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\), because one of its ten premises is the explicit TCB atom
\(\textsf{ScriptOKstage\_a}\), and that atom is not file-derived, not
checker-emitted, and not in \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\).  After adjoining that single
external script premise, rule \(R3\) applies over \(A^{+}\) because its ten
premises, namely the four Stage--A file atoms, \(\textsf{finalDigest}\), the
finite predicates \(\textsf{RecordGateOK}\), \(\textsf{CertDAGOK}\), and
\(\textsf{DigestOKstage\_a}\), the explicit TCB premise
\(\textsf{ScriptOKstage\_a}\), and \(\textsf{qrgsReplayUpgrade}\), are in
\(A^{+}\); hence \(\qrgs\in\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A^{+})\).

For the four negative coordinates, the only displayed rules concluding
\(\qsrc\), \(\qart\), \(\qext\), and \(\qven\) are \(R5\), \(R6\), the two-step
\(R7a\)--\(R7b\) path, and \(R8\).  Their respective missing promotion premises
are \(\textsf{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\textsf{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\textsf{externalArchiveEquivalence}\) together with the replay-identity atom
\(\textsf{locatorOKstageA}\), where \(\textsf{locatorOKstageA}\) itself requires
\(\textsf{stableLocator}\) and \(\textsf{archiveByteEquality}\), and
\(\textsf{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\).  None is a base atom in
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\), \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\), or \(A^{+}\), and no displayed rule
derives any of them.  Applying
Lemma~\ref{lem:closed-world-horn-exclusion} with
\[
U=\{\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\},\qquad
M=\{\textsf{freshFormalSourceUpgrade},
\textsf{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade},
\textsf{externalArchiveEquivalence},
\textsf{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\}
\]
therefore proves that those four support coordinates are absent from the least
Horn closures of all three bases.  The other rules derive only auxiliary atoms,
not elements of \(Q\).
This is a calculation in the closed printed Horn frame only: no unprinted source
semantics, path existence, or coordinate atom is admitted as an additional
premise.

Equivalently, define the finite forward-chaining stages
\[
C_0(A)=A,\qquad
C_{n+1}(A)=C_n(A)\cup
\{h:\text{some displayed rule }h\leftarrow P\text{ has }P\subseteq C_n(A)\}.
\]
We prove the exact vector by induction on \(n\), simultaneously for the three
bases.  At stage \(0\), the intersections with \(Q\) are empty by the
closed-world input hypothesis, and the four promotion atoms
\(\textsf{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\textsf{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\textsf{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\textsf{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\) are absent.  The induction step
uses the rule table.  Rules \(R0,R2,R4,R7a,R9,R10,R11,R12\) add only auxiliary
non-coordinate atoms and none of the four missing promotion atoms.  Rule \(R1\)
can add \(\qlex\) exactly for bases containing all five \(R1\) premises, hence
for \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\) and \(A^{+}\) but not for \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\).  Rule
\(R3\) can add \(\qrgs\) exactly for bases containing all ten \(R3\) premises;
the only branch with the missing tenth premise
\(\textsf{ScriptOKstage\_a}\) is \(A^{+}\).  Rules \(R5,R6,R7b,R8\) still cannot
add \(\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\), because their promotion premises were absent at
stage \(n\) and no rule has added them at stage \(n+1\).  Thus the claimed
intersection with \(Q\) is invariant after the first applicable coordinate rule
fires, and the finite least fixed point has exactly the same \(Q\)-projection.
Exhausting all six \(Q\)-concluding routes gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A_{\mathrm{rec}})\cap Q&=\varnothing,\\
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A^{\mathrm{scan}})\cap Q&=\{\qlex\},\\
\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(A^{+})\cap Q&=\{\qlex,\qrgs\}.
\end{aligned}
\]

This proves the finite coordinate calculation and records precisely where
\(\qlex\) and \(\qrgs\) enter.  In the unconditional PDF-level branch neither
enters: \(R1\) is blocked by the absent \(\texttt{scanOK}\) atom and \(R3\) is
blocked by the absent \(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\) atom.  The atom \(\qlex\)
enters only in the scanner branch, as the decoded begin-token witness coordinate
through the explicit \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) premise; \(\qrgs\) enters only in the
replay branch through the additional \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise.  The support
and upgrade consequences of each coordinate are those in
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}.  No supplement obstruction or stress-test branch
is used to derive the branch-labelled coordinate calculation.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Public scope.}
Proposition~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy} is a typed interface
calculation, with the independent reference-checker soundness stated in
Lemma~\ref{lem:finite-horn-reference-checker-soundness}.
It permits proposal and repair artifacts to be retained while deterministic
gates and human promotion records control the public coordinates listed in
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}.

\section{Certificate-record and archive contracts}
\label{sec:replay-artifact-boundary}
The two remaining foreground theorems are compact contracts.  They name the
finite relation and the archive premise; the long row tables, digest ledgers,
branch-by-branch script obligations, and stress-test mathematics are retained
only in the supplement source after this file's first \verb|\end{document}|.


\begin{definition}[Certificate-record acceptance schema]
\label{def:certificate-record-acceptance-schema}
Let
\[
\begin{aligned}
\CertTuple=(&\MainBytes,\Schema,\Ledger,\InterfaceMap,\ClaimInventory,\\
&\ReviewManifest,\CaseManifest,\Dispatch,\Digest,\Snapshot)
\end{aligned}
\]
be a finite already-parsed tuple consisting, respectively, of the submitted
\path{main.tex} byte string, a certificate schema, seven-row current ledger,
submission-interface map, primary-claim inventory, review-bundle manifest,
case-row manifest, dispatch table, digest table, and finite snapshot-boundary
table.  These component names are part of the notation contract: the
six-coordinate public alphabet remains \(Q\), and no component of the parsed
certificate tuple is denoted by \(Q\), \(L\), or \(S\).  Evidence strings are
parsed by the following finite typed
grammar:
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
u&::=&a\mid p\mid m\mid d\mid s,\\
a&\in&E_{\mathrm{atom}}\quad\text{(closed non-path atoms)},\\
p&\in&E_{\mathrm{path}}\quad\text{(relative path strings)},\\
m&\in&\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(\ReviewManifest),\\
d&\in&\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{\MainBytes,\Digest},\\
s&\in&\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(\Snapshot).
\end{array}
\]
The path production is admissible only when \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_{\Digest}(p)\)
holds; no other grammar branch uses filesystem existence.  Let
\(\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{\MainBytes,\Digest}\) be the finite set of table and formal
label atoms emitted by the deterministic scan of the digest-bound submitted
\path{main.tex} byte string \(\MainBytes\), with no use of the rendered PDF surface.
The admissible-pointer predicate is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\PointerOK_{\CertTuple}(u,E)\Longleftrightarrow{}&
u\in E_{\mathrm{atom}}\\
&{}\vee (u\in E_{\mathrm{path}}\wedge \operatorname{BoundPath}_{\Digest}(u))\\
&{}\vee u\in\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(\ReviewManifest)\\
&{}\vee u\in\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{\MainBytes,\Digest}\\
&{}\vee u\in\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(\Snapshot).
\end{aligned}
\]
There is no untyped shortcut \(u\in E\) and no filesystem-existence shortcut.
Write \(\operatorname{ReqMan}(\Schema)\) for the finite schema-prescribed
manifest keys, \(\operatorname{ReqPath}_{\Schema}(k)\) for the required relative
path at key \(k\), \(\operatorname{LedgerRows}(\Ledger)\) for the parsed ledger rows,
\(\operatorname{PassRows}(e)\) for the parsed pass rows of \(e\),
\(\operatorname{Kind}(e)\) for its finite claim-kind set,
\(\operatorname{RowsIface}(\InterfaceMap)\) for the parsed submission-interface rows,
\(\operatorname{RowsCase}(\CaseManifest)\) for the parsed case-row-manifest records,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{CaseNames}_{0}=\{&
\textsf{NewmathIntakeIsolation},
\textsf{UpperFibersOverlapBlock},
\textsf{FakeExtensionBlock},\\
&\textsf{Rule110LimitationGate},
\textsf{Rule110DynamicSemanticValidation}\},
\end{aligned}
\]
\(\operatorname{EligRows}(\CaseManifest)\subseteq
\operatorname{RowsCase}(\CaseManifest)\) for the rows whose case name is in the
first four names of \(\operatorname{CaseNames}_{0}\) and whose verifier result is
\textsf{accepted}, \(\operatorname{HistTags}(\CaseManifest)\) for the finite set
of historical-boundary tags printed by those eligible rows, and
\(\operatorname{SnapPaths}(\CaseManifest)\) for the finite set of snapshot paths
printed by those eligible rows.  The fifth row of \(\CaseManifest\), the Rule110
dynamic semantic validation negative control, is parsed into
\(\operatorname{RowsCase}(\CaseManifest)\) but is not in
\(\operatorname{EligRows}(\CaseManifest)\) and contributes no member to
\(\operatorname{HistTags}(\CaseManifest)\) or
\(\operatorname{SnapPaths}(\CaseManifest)\).  Write
\(\operatorname{Prim}(\ClaimInventory)\) for the primary-claim rows.  Define the named Boolean
predicates \(\mathrm{M}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{M}_3\),
\(\mathrm{C}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{C}_5\),
\(\mathrm{L}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{L}_2\), \(\mathrm{E}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{E}_{10}\),
\(\mathrm{R}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{R}_4\), \(\mathrm{I}_1,\mathrm{I}_2\), and
\(\mathrm{P}_1,\ldots,\mathrm{P}_5\) by the following displayed finite
clauses.  The certificate predicate is exactly their conjunction:
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(\CertTuple)\\
&\quad:\Longleftrightarrow
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{3}\mathrm{M}_i
 \wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{5}\mathrm{C}_i
\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{L}_i
\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{10}\mathrm{E}_i
\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{4}\mathrm{R}_i
\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{I}_i
\wedge\bigwedge_{i=1}^{5}\mathrm{P}_i .
\end{aligned}
\]
\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.08\linewidth}Y@{}}
\toprule
Clause & Predicate \\ \midrule
\text{M1} & \(\operatorname{dom}(\ReviewManifest)=\operatorname{ReqMan}(\Schema)\).\\
\text{M2} & For every \(k\in\operatorname{ReqMan}(\Schema)\),
\(\ReviewManifest[k].\operatorname{path}=\operatorname{ReqPath}_{\Schema}(k)\).\\
\text{M3} & For every \(k\in\operatorname{ReqMan}(\Schema)\),
\(\operatorname{BoundPath}_{\Digest}(\ReviewManifest[k].\operatorname{path})\).\\[1mm]
\text{C1} & \(\{\operatorname{name}(c):c\in\operatorname{RowsCase}(\CaseManifest)\}
=\operatorname{CaseNames}_{0}\), with
\textsf{NewmathIntakeIsolation}, \textsf{UpperFibersOverlapBlock},
\textsf{FakeExtensionBlock}, and \textsf{Rule110LimitationGate} marked
\textsf{accepted}, and \textsf{Rule110DynamicSemanticValidation} marked
\textsf{rejected}.\\
\text{C2} & Every \(c\in\operatorname{RowsCase}(\CaseManifest)\) has the fields
\(\operatorname{name}(c)\), \(\operatorname{skeleton}(c)\),
\(\operatorname{verifierResult}(c)\), \(\operatorname{boundaryKind}(c)\),
\(\operatorname{snapshotList}(c)\), and a finite digest map for the listed
snapshot paths.\\
\text{C3} & \(\operatorname{EligRows}(\CaseManifest)=
\{c\in\operatorname{RowsCase}(\CaseManifest):\operatorname{verifierResult}(c)=\textsf{accepted}\}\);
each eligible row has boundary kind \(\textsf{historical}\) or
\(\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified}\), and every listed snapshot path is a POSIX
relative path below \texttt{review\_bundle/case\_snapshots/} with no empty,
\texttt{.}, \texttt{..}, absolute, or backslash segment.\\
\text{C4} & \(\Snapshot=\operatorname{SnapPaths}(\CaseManifest)\), and for every
\(p\in\Snapshot\), the digest printed for \(p\) by its eligible case row equals
\(\Digest[p]\).  No rejected case row contributes a snapshot path to \(\Snapshot\).\\
\text{C5} & The historical-boundary atom set available to
\(\PointerOK_{\CertTuple}\) is exactly
\(\{\texttt{historical-boundary:}t:t\in\operatorname{HistTags}(\CaseManifest)\}\); no case
name, skeleton name, rejected row, or path prefix is also a boundary atom.\\[1mm]
\text{L1} & \(|\operatorname{LedgerRows}(\Ledger)|=7\).\\
\text{L2} & \(\{\operatorname{name}(e):e\in\operatorname{LedgerRows}(\Ledger)\}
=\{\texttt{a1},\ldots,\texttt{a7}\}\).\\
\text{E1} & For every \(e\),
\(\operatorname{ReqEntryFields}(\Schema)\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(e)\).\\
\text{E2} & For every \(e\), \(\operatorname{Kind}(e)\ne\varnothing\).\\
\text{E3} & For every \(e\),
\(\operatorname{Kind}(e)\subseteq\operatorname{KindDomain}(\Schema)\).\\
\text{E4} & \(\Schema.\operatorname{evidenceLevels}
=\{\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified},\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\}\), and for
every \(e\), \(\operatorname{level}(e)\in\Schema.\operatorname{evidenceLevels}\).\\
\text{E5} & For every \(e\), \(\operatorname{EvidenceSurface}(e)=
(E_{\mathrm{atom}}(e),E_{\mathrm{path}}(e))\), finite, nonempty, and typed by
the grammar above.\\
\text{E6} & For every \(e\), \(\operatorname{PassRows}(e)\) is a nonempty finite
list.\\
\text{E7} & For every \(e\), the map
\(r\mapsto\operatorname{gate}(r)\) is a bijection from
\(\operatorname{PassRows}(e)\) to \(\bigcup_{k\in\operatorname{Kind}(e)}\Dispatch(k)\).\\
\text{E8} & For every \(e\), \(\operatorname{DiscoveryField}(e)\) is present iff
\(\textsf{discovery}\in\operatorname{Kind}(e)\).\\
\text{E9} & For every \(e\), if
\(\operatorname{level}(e)=\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified}\), then
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_2(e)\).\\
\text{E10} & For every \(e\), if
\(\operatorname{level}(e)=\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\), then
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_3(e)\).\\[1mm]
\text{R1} & For every \(e\) and every \(r\in\operatorname{PassRows}(e)\),
\(\operatorname{ReqPassFields}(\Schema)\subseteq\operatorname{dom}(r)\).\\
\text{R2} & For every \(e\) and every \(r\in\operatorname{PassRows}(e)\),
\(\operatorname{level}(r)=\operatorname{level}(e)\).\\

\(\operatorname{decision}(r)\in\Schema.\operatorname{passDecisions}\).\\
\text{R4} & For every \(e\) and every \(r\in\operatorname{PassRows}(e)\),
\(\PointerOK_{\CertTuple}(\operatorname{pointer}(r),\operatorname{EvidenceSurface}(e))\).\\[1mm]

\text{I2} & \(\{\operatorname{id}(x):x\in\operatorname{RowsIface}(\InterfaceMap)\}
=\{\texttt{sim1},\texttt{sim2},\texttt{sim3},\texttt{sim4}\}\).\\[1mm]

\(\operatorname{DeclaredCoordinate}(c)\in\{\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\}\).\\
\text{P2} & \(|\operatorname{Prim}(\ClaimInventory)|\ge 12\).\\
\text{P3} & For every \(c\in\operatorname{Prim}(\ClaimInventory)\),
\(\operatorname{claim}(c)\ne\varnothing\) and
\(\operatorname{status}(c)\ne\varnothing\).\\
\text{P4} & For every \(c\in\operatorname{Prim}(\ClaimInventory)\),
\(\neg\operatorname{NonLoadBearing}(c)\) implies
\(\operatorname{certificate}(c)\ne\varnothing\).\\
\text{P5} & For every \(c\in\operatorname{Prim}(\ClaimInventory)\),
\(\neg\operatorname{NonLoadBearing}(c)\) implies
\(\operatorname{evidenceLevel}(c)\ne\varnothing\).\\

\end{center}
All quantifiers over \(e\) range over \(\operatorname{LedgerRows}(\Ledger)\).  Thus
\(\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}\) is a mathematical finite relation over the
stated byte string \(\MainBytes\) and parsed records only; it
does not assert JSON byte decoding, root normalization, SHA--256 equality, or
Python-script conformance.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Exact gate-row bijection]
\label{lem:exact-gate-row-bijection}
For every parsed ledger row \(e\), the certificate-record schema accepts the gate
part of \(e\) if and only if the row's displayed gate records are in bijection
with the gate set assigned to \(e\) by \(\Dispatch\): every assigned gate occurs
exactly once, no unassigned gate occurs, and no duplicate gate row is resolved by
priority or last-writer convention.  This is a finite schema lemma only; a
supplied executable checker may use it only under the separate
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) conformance premise of
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The gate clauses are finite cardinality and membership checks against the set
\(\Dispatch(e)\).  If the row passes them, each required gate key has one row and no
other gate key is present, giving the bijection.  Conversely, such a bijection
satisfies the membership, uniqueness, and no-extra-row clauses by inspection.
There is no conflict-resolution rule in the relation, so duplicates and stale
rows reject rather than being merged.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Typed-pointer closure]
\label{lem:typed-pointer-closure-compact}
For every parsed ledger row \(e\), every accepted pass-row evidence pointer is in
one of the five displayed finite classes of \(\PointerOK\): a promoted non-path
atom, a digest-bound path, a manifest-boundary pointer, a digest-table-boundary
pointer, or an eligible snapshot-boundary path.  No other string, filesystem
entry, prefix match, wildcard, local file existence fact, or script output can be
used as evidence in \(\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}\).
This is likewise a finite schema lemma: it classifies accepted pointer strings
inside the printed relation and does not by itself promote any archive path,
script output, or public coordinate atom.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The productions for non-path atoms and path strings are disjoint, and the path
branch is additionally guarded by \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_{\Digest}\).  The remaining
three branches range over finite boundary sets computed from
\(\ReviewManifest\), \(\Digest\), and eligible snapshot rows.  These five
alternatives exhaust the displayed
\(\PointerOK\) disjunction, so an accepted pointer belongs to one of them; a
string outside the disjunction is rejected before it can support a pass row.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Certificate-checker conformance contract]
\label{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}
Definition~\ref{def:certificate-record-acceptance-schema} is the schema: it
defines \(\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(\CertTuple)\)
as the finite conjunction of clauses M1--M3, C1--C5, L1--E10, R1--R4,
I1--I2, and P1--P5.  Let
\(V_{\mathrm{cert}}\) be a supplied certificate-record checker for parsed tuples.
This theorem is a named conditional contract, not an independent verification of
any particular program.  Assume the branch-by-branch conformance statement
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}(V_{\mathrm{cert}},\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec})\):
its parser is deterministic on the supplied byte strings, it rejects duplicate
or missing required fields exactly as the schema prescribes, it computes
\(\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}\), \(\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{\MainBytes,\Digest}\),
\(\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}\), \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_{\Digest}\), and
\(\Dispatch\)'s gate sets exactly, it implements E7 as the finite gate-row bijection
of Lemma~\ref{lem:exact-gate-row-bijection}, it implements R4 as the five-way
typed-pointer disjunction of Lemma~\ref{lem:typed-pointer-closure-compact}, and
its final return value is the conjunction of the displayed finite clauses with
no extra acceptance branch.  Then, for every parsed finite tuple,
\[
V_{\mathrm{cert}}(\CertTuple)=\mathsf{accept}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(\CertTuple).
\]

The executable-instance statement is separate: only if an archive-bearing
submission supplies ordinary digest-bound bytes and printed hashes for
\(\Schema,\Ledger,\InterfaceMap,\ClaimInventory,\ReviewManifest,\CaseManifest,
\Dispatch,\Digest\), supplies the corresponding source byte string
\(\MainBytes\) and finite snapshot table \(\Snapshot\), provides root normalization,
ordinary-file checks, JSON decoding, and digest equality for each of those
records, and also includes a separate proof of this
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) contract, may an
executable file such as \artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py}
be cited as accepting exactly the schema relation on those supplied bytes.
Without those archive and script-conformance premises, a script run, path name,
JSON filename, or PDF rendering of a row is not executable evidence for this
PDF-only specification.
In particular, the case-row clauses C1--C5 range over
\(\operatorname{RowsCase}(\CaseManifest)\), while the interface clauses I1--I2
range over \(\operatorname{RowsIface}(\InterfaceMap)\); no clause in this
contract constrains case rows by reading interface rows.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Unfold the assumed \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) contract.  Its forward half says
that a checker acceptance can occur only after the deterministic parser has
produced the named tuple, each implemented branch has returned the corresponding
displayed clause, E7 has computed the gate-row bijection of
Lemma~\ref{lem:exact-gate-row-bijection}, R4 has computed the typed-pointer
closure of Lemma~\ref{lem:typed-pointer-closure-compact}, and the final return
value has no extra accepting branch.  Hence acceptance implies the displayed
conjunction, which is \(\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}\) by definition.  Its
reverse half says that when that displayed conjunction is true, the checker has
no additional rejection condition and therefore returns \(\mathsf{accept}\).
The theorem proves only this implication from the named conformance contract; a
program-specific result would require separate lemmas for parser determinism,
each implemented branch, and the final-return branch of the actual checker.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Archive contract predicate and Horn projection]
\label{def:archive-contract-predicate}
At the theorem site below, \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\) denotes exactly clauses 1--7 in
the displayed archive contract.  These clauses instantiate the decidable
predicates of Definition~\ref{def:decidable-replay-premises-minimal}: clause 6
and the finite archive header give \(\archivepresent(A)\); clauses 1--3 give
\(\ordinaryfilebyteeq(A,M)\), \(\digestok(M)\), and \(\noselfdigest(M)\);
clause 6 gives \(\immutablelocator(A)\); the run-acceptance hypotheses give
\(\transcriptsok(A)\); and \(\ScriptOKstageA\) together with
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) gives \(\scriptsconform(A)\).  Let \(\Facts(Z)\) be the full finite fact set
obtained from the parsed archive records after archive normalization, byte
equality, digest equality, parser conformance, and script conformance have been
established.  It may include archive-format facts, run-acceptance facts,
scanner facts, digest facts, finite-checker facts, and script-conformance facts,
so it is not claimed to be equal to \(A^{+}\).

Let \(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\) be the projection of this full fact set onto
exactly the Horn atom alphabet printed in
Proposition~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}.  The archive parsers may
contribute non-coordinate support atoms, but no script-conformance predicate
adds its Horn atom to \(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\) unless the theorem
hypotheses below state that the corresponding conformance premise has been
attached.  The theorem never identifies this projection with \(A^{+}\); it uses
the following separate frame condition to control possible extra facts.  This
frame condition is an external closed-world hypothesis at the theorem site; it
is not derived from \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\) or from digest/manifest domain checks.

Define \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) to be the conjunction of the following finite clauses.
F1: \(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\cap Q=\varnothing\).
F2: the only Horn rules with conclusions in \(Q\) are exactly
\(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\).
F3: the atoms \(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\) are not in
\(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\setminus Q\) unless a separate named upgrade
derivation is supplied.
F4: no non-coordinate atom of \(\ReplayBase(Z)\) is treated as one of
\(\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\).
F5: coordinate truth is evaluated only by
\(\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(\ReplayBase(Z))\) using the displayed
rule set.
In particular, \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) includes the pre-closure coordinate condition
\[
\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\cap Q=\varnothing .
\]
Equivalently, write
\[
\ReplayBase(Z)=\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\setminus Q,
\]
and compute all coordinate bits only after Horn closure.  For \(q_i\in Q\),
define
\[
\ReplayVector_i(Z)=
\mathbf{1}_{q_i\in\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(\ReplayBase(Z))}.
\]
The ordered tuple \(\ReplayVector(Z)\) is indexed as
\((\qlex,\qrgs,\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven)\).

Clause 7 of \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\), written \(\AtomMapOKA(Z)\), is the exact finite
path-to-atom compiler used by the archive theorem.  Its authoritative
machine-readable rows are the digest-bound records in
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json} and
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}.  The compact
Table~\ref{tab:path-atom-map-a} is only the human digest of those rows: it gives
the role classes, row counts, and emitted atom names, while the exact path,
byte-length, and SHA--256 strings remain in the machine-readable manifest
records so they are not line-broken or visually rewrapped in the main text.
Compiling those rows emits exactly the displayed Horn atoms and no coordinate
atom.  Predicate rows such as \texttt{scanOK}, \texttt{RecordGateOK},
\texttt{CertDAGOK}, and \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a} are not emitted by path
existence; they enter only through the separate premises named in the theorem.
Finally, abbreviate
\[
\ArchiveInstantiatesAplus(Z)\Longleftrightarrow A^{+}\subseteq\ReplayBase(Z).
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{table}
\caption{Compact digest of the \(\PathAtom(p,a)\) map for the archive-instantiated Horn base.}
\label{tab:path-atom-map-a}
\footnotesize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.20\linewidth}L{0.12\linewidth}Y@{}}
\toprule
Role class & Rows & Horn atoms emitted from digest-bound rows \\ \midrule
Surface and source & 3 &
\texttt{pubAbs}, \texttt{suppMain}, \texttt{mainTex}.\\
\addlinespace
Inventory and digest & 4 &
\texttt{invJson}, \texttt{invMd}, \texttt{finalDigest},
\texttt{DigestOKstage\_a}.\\
\addlinespace
Replay kernel & 4 &
\texttt{stageAHornSchema}, \texttt{stageAManifest},
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate}, \texttt{stageAReplayReport}.\\
\addlinespace
Support rows & 3 &
\texttt{srcInterface}, \texttt{caseArtifacts}, \texttt{venueReadiness}.\\
\addlinespace
Upgrade marker & 1 &
\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}.  This marker is a formal atom only; it does not
emit \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}.\\

\end{tabularx}
\vspace{0.5ex}
\footnotesize
Exact relative paths and emitted atom names for these 15 rows are normative in
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json}; SHA--256 values and byte
equalities are supplied by \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}
and the archive byte-length clauses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}.  This table
deliberately does not reprint long path or digest strings.
\end{table}

\begin{lemma}[Archive instantiates the \(A^{+}\) Horn base]
\label{lem:archive-instantiates-aplus}
Assume \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\), \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\), \(\RecordGateOK\),
\(\CertDAGOK\), \(\DigestOKstageA\), \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\),
\(\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}\), \(\ScriptOKstageA\), and
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\).  Then \(\ArchiveInstantiatesAplus(Z)\), equivalently
\[
A^{+}\subseteq\ReplayBase(Z).
\]
More explicitly,
\[
A_{\mathrm{rec}}\cup\{\texttt{scanOK},\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\}
\subseteq\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z)),
\]
and the closed-world coordinate condition in
Definition~\ref{def:archive-contract-predicate} places the same atoms in
\(\ReplayBase(Z)\).
Moreover the frame clauses assert that the initial archive projection has no
public coordinate atom, that the only \(Q\)-concluding Horn rules are
\(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\), and that the four non-allowed promotion premises
\(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\) are absent from \(\ReplayBase(Z)\)
unless they are explicitly derived by separate upgrade records.
The lemma proves inclusion of the allowed replay base only; it does not replace
\(\ReplayBase(Z)\) by \(A^{+}\) and does not infer absence of extra support
facts except through the stated \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) clauses.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Clause 1 of \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\) supplies the archive-format guard before any path
or digest row is used; clauses 2--3 supply the ordinary-file byte equalities for
the printed outer rows, the parsed digest-table anchor, and the inner no-self
digest rows; clause 4 supplies the parsed certificate tuple used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}; clauses 5--6 supply the
environment descriptor and stable replay locator; clause 7 is exactly
\(\AtomMapOKA(Z)\).  By the machine-readable manifest rows just named, grouped
in Table~\ref{tab:path-atom-map-a}, this atom map emits all path-derived atoms
in \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\):
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{pubAbs},\texttt{suppMain},\texttt{invJson},\texttt{invMd},
\texttt{finalDigest},\texttt{mainTex},\\
\texttt{stageAHornSchema},\texttt{stageAManifest},
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate},\texttt{stageAReplayReport},\\
\texttt{DigestOKstage\_a},\texttt{srcInterface},\texttt{caseArtifacts},
\texttt{venueReadiness},\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}.
\end{gathered}
\]
The named finite-checker premises add \(\texttt{RecordGateOK}\) and
\(\texttt{CertDAGOK}\).  Together with the stated \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) premise,
which contributes exactly \(\texttt{scanOK}\), these facts contribute the lexical
premises of rule \(R1\):
\[
\{\texttt{mainTex},\texttt{invJson},\texttt{invMd},
\texttt{finalDigest},\texttt{scanOK}\}.
\]
The same atom map contributes the non-coordinate support atoms
\[
\{\texttt{pubAbs},\texttt{suppMain},\texttt{srcInterface},
\texttt{caseArtifacts},\texttt{venueReadiness}\},
\]
which are retained as finite support rows in \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\) but do not
conclude any public coordinate by themselves.  The map rows and named checker
hypotheses also contribute exactly the Stage--A atoms used by rule \(R3\):
\[
\begin{gathered}
\texttt{stageAHornSchema},\texttt{stageAManifest},
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate},\texttt{stageAReplayReport},
\texttt{finalDigest},\\
\texttt{RecordGateOK},\texttt{CertDAGOK},\texttt{DigestOKstage\_a},
\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}.
\end{gathered}
\]
The separate \(\ScriptOKstageA\) hypothesis contributes the remaining
\(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\) atom.  The certificate-script conformance
hypothesis justifies using the accepted parsed certificate relation; it does not
add a public coordinate or a stronger upgrade atom.

Therefore every atom of \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\) is in \(\ReplayBase(Z)\), and after
adjoining the one external \(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\) atom every atom of
\(A^{+}\) is in \(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\).  None of these atoms lies in
\(Q\), so the closed-world subtraction defining \(\ReplayBase(Z)\) removes none
of them.  Hence \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\cup\{\texttt{scanOK},
\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\}\subseteq\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\) and
\(A^{+}\subseteq\ReplayBase(Z)\).  The last assertion is not an equality claim:
\(\ReplayBase(Z)\) may contain additional non-coordinate support atoms.  The
needed negative information is supplied separately by \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\): F1 removes
pre-existing coordinate atoms, F2 forbids extra \(Q\)-concluding rules, and F3
excludes the four stronger upgrade premises unless a named upgrade derivation is
present.  In particular \(\LocatorOKstageA\) is not
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Frame-conditional archive contract overview]
\label{thm:archive-contract-overview}
\label{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}
Let \(Z\) be a candidate non-PDF replay archive with unpacked root \(R\), digest
table \(T\), environment descriptor \(E\), \(\LocatorRow\), and supplied raw
byte records for the certificate tuple \(\CertTuple\) used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  Let
\(\operatorname{InnerDigestOK}(T,R)\) mean the mathematical predicate that the
parsed inner digest table has exact finite domain, no duplicate path row, no row
for \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}, and for every parsed
inner path \(p\), \(R\) contains \(p\) as a unique ordinary non-symlink file
whose raw bytes have the digest printed in \(T\).  This predicate is not checked
by definition by any script; a script may implement it only under the separate
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise.  Define
\(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\) to be the conjunction of the following finite facts:
\begin{enumerate}
\item archive-format normalization and alias rejection for the union of the
outer printed paths and the parsed inner digest-table paths: byte-preserving
extraction, exact POSIX relative names, case-sensitive comparison, no Unicode
normalization, no duplicate required entries, no symlink or hard-link entries,
ordinary non-symlink files after extraction, distinct post-unpack identities,
and an immutable replay root;
\item outer byte equality: every outer path printed by the paper's digest
contract is an ordinary file under \(R\) and its raw bytes have the printed
SHA--256 value;
\item inner no-self digest equality: the parsed
\ledgerpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json} has no row for itself,
has exact finite domain, has no duplicate path row, and
\(\operatorname{InnerDigestOK}(T,R)\) holds after the same archive-format and
no-alias guard;
\item record-byte equality: the supplied bytes for the schema, ledger,
submission-interface map, primary-claim inventory, review-bundle manifest,
case-row manifest, dispatch table, digest table, and snapshot table parse to the
ten objects of \(\CertTuple\) used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}; the archive digest table
\(T\) is this parsed \(\Digest\) when read as the Stage--A digest-table object;
  \item environment identity: the replay-environment descriptor pins the JSON,
SHA--256, path-normalization, ordinary-file, and working-directory conventions
used by the finite contract; and
\item locator identity: a DOI-bearing supplement, Software Heritage object,
venue-controlled supplement identifier, or equivalent immutable byte locator
names the exact archive bytes and is paired with the byte-equality rows above;
the PDF-only submission supplies no such locator.
\item atom-map exactness: \(\AtomMapOKA(Z)\) holds for the finite
\(\PathAtom(p,a)\) rows in
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json}, with SHA--256 and
byte-equality checks supplied by the digest and archive clauses; the compact
Table~\ref{tab:path-atom-map-a} groups their emitted atoms.  No other path row
emits an atom of \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\), and no row emits any coordinate atom in
\(Q\).
 \end{enumerate}
 Equivalently, the dependency roles are: clause 1 supplies archive format and
 alias rejection; clauses 2--3 supply outer, digest-table, and inner byte
 equality; clause 4 supplies the parsed certificate tuple; clause 5 supplies the
 environment descriptor; clause 6 supplies the stable locator identity; and
 clause 7 supplies the exact path-to-Horn-atom compiler.
The locator identity premise is
\[
\LocatorOKstageA,
\]
not \(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and therefore is not a \(\qext\)
upgrade row.  The acceptance/run-success hypotheses are separate: the parsed
certificate tuple must satisfy
\(\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(\CertTuple)\), the certificate
script run must accept that tuple, the Stage--A replay script run must accept the
replay report, and the archive scanner premise \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) must hold.
Under these hypotheses
Lemma~\ref{lem:archive-instantiates-aplus} embeds the \(A^{+}\) base of
Proposition~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}.
If
\begin{align*}
&\ArchiveOKA(Z)\land\FrameOKZ(Z)\land
\ScriptOKstageA\land\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\\
&{}\land\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(\CertTuple)\\
&{}\land\operatorname{CertRunAccepts}(Z)
\land\operatorname{StageARunAccepts}(Z)\\
&{}\land\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\land\RecordGateOK\land\CertDAGOK\\
&{}\land\DigestOKstageA\land\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade},
\end{align*}
clause 7 of \(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\), namely \(\AtomMapOKA(Z)\), emits the
path-derived atoms of \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\) from the exact manifest rows,
summarized in Table~\ref{tab:path-atom-map-a}.  The external frame hypothesis
\(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) says that
\(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\cap Q=\varnothing\) initially, that no
\(Q\)-concluding Horn rule is available except \(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\), and that
the non-allowed promotion premises for \(\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\) are absent
unless a named upgrade derivation supplies them.  Therefore
\(\ReplayVector(Z)\) is computed from
\(\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(\ReplayBase(Z))\), not from raw coordinate
atoms or hidden \(Q\)-rules.  Under these framed hypotheses,
\begin{align*}
\ReplayVector(Z)
&=(\mathsf{true},\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false}).
\end{align*}
Without the \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) premise, the first coordinate is not derivable
from the displayed archive facts.  Without \(\ScriptOKstageA\), the second
coordinate is not derivable from the displayed archive facts.
The theorem is a sufficient-condition overview conditional on the external
frame hypothesis; the archive predicate it uses is
Definition~\ref{def:archive-contract-predicate}.  A PDF-only article or an
unlocated local source tree does not instantiate a run-based replay claim; it
supplies only the printed finite specification and this conditional mathematical
archive predicate.  Thus this theorem is non-operative for the current
submission as a software artifact.  A mathematical-software submission of the
executable reading would also have to supply the immutable locator, archive
bytes, exact digest table, checker scripts, run transcripts, and
branch-by-branch \(\ScriptOKstageA\) and \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\)
conformance lemmas named above, and the closed-world frame
\(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) would still have to be supplied rather than inferred from the
archive digest rows alone.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The listed clauses of \(\ArchiveOKA\) summarize the byte and identity premises
missing from the PDF-only relation.  The archive-format clause fixes the name
language and ordinary-file semantics before any digest is read; the outer digest
clause binds the printed anchor; and
\(\operatorname{InnerDigestOK}(T,R)\) is the mathematical inner-row predicate
that binds every nested support record without appealing to a script.  The
record-byte clause supplies the
parsed tuple for Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}; the
environment clause pins the trusted byte-reading and JSON conventions; and the
locator clause identifies the archive as the submitted replay surface.  The
atom-map clause supplies exactly the path-derived Horn atoms from the
manifest-row compiler, with Table~\ref{tab:path-atom-map-a} serving as a compact
digest of those atoms.  The frame condition supplies the facts that the
pre-closure archive projection contains no coordinate atom, that every
\(Q\)-concluding rule is among \(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\), and that the four
non-allowed promotion premises are absent unless a named upgrade derivation is
present.  The
certificate acceptance hypothesis supplies the finite relation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}; \(\operatorname{CertRunAccepts}(Z)\)
and \(\operatorname{StageARunAccepts}(Z)\) supply the accepted finite runs, and
the explicit \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) premise supplies the scanner atom.  Hence
Lemma~\ref{lem:archive-instantiates-aplus} gives \(A^{+}\subseteq\ReplayBase(Z)\).
Rules \(R1\) and \(R3\) therefore derive \(\qlex\) and \(\qrgs\), respectively.

For the negative coordinates, do not replace \(\ReplayBase(Z)\) by the smaller
set \(A^{+}\).  Instead use \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\).  By F2 the only rules capable of
concluding \(\qsrc,\qart,\qext,\qven\) are \(R5,R6,R7b,R8\).  Rule \(R5\)
requires \(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\), rule \(R6\) requires
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\), rule \(R7b\) requires
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\) in addition to any locator atom, and
rule \(R8\) requires \(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\).  By F3 these
four premises are absent from \(\ReplayBase(Z)\) unless separately derived, and
the theorem hypotheses supply no such named upgrade derivation.  Extra archive
facts outside \(A^{+}\) are therefore harmless: by F4 they are not coordinate
atoms, and by F5 they can affect coordinates only through the displayed rules.
The locator clause supplies only \(\LocatorOKstageA\); because
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\) is still absent, \(\qext\) does not
fire.  Thus the four remaining coordinates are false and
\(\ReplayVector(Z)=(\mathsf{true},\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})\).

If \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) is removed, rule \(R1\)'s \(\texttt{scanOK}\) premise is
missing from \(\ReplayBase(Z)\); because F1 gives
\(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\cap Q=\varnothing\), no base coordinate fact can
supply \(\qlex\), and because F2 lists all \(Q\)-concluding rules, no other rule
concludes \(\qlex\).  If
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) is removed, rule \(R3\)'s
\(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\) premise is missing from \(\ReplayBase(Z)\); F1
forbids a pre-existing \(\qrgs\) coordinate fact, and F2 supplies no other rule
concluding \(\qrgs\).  If any archive clause is absent, the byte-bound replay
surface is not identified, so only the printed conditional specification remains.
\end{proof}

\section{Related work by interface}
\label{sec:related-work}
This paper is closest to proof-carrying and certificate-checking traditions:
proof-carrying code and foundational proof certificates separate a compact
certificate object from the trusted checker that validates it
\cite{Necula1997PCC,Appel2001FoundationalPCC,AppelMcAllester2001FPCCRecursiveTypes,ChihaniMillerRenaud2013FPC}.
Our contribution uses that separation at the publication boundary rather than
inside a programming-language or logical-kernel safety theorem.  The submitted
certificate proves only a finite Horn-coordinate closure, and only for the
coordinates whose premises are present.  It is not a proof of source-theory
truth, proof-assistant reconstruction, artifact semantics, or general
implementation soundness.

The work is also adjacent to formal-mathematics systems and LLM-assisted
formalization.  Lean 4, Lean, Isabelle/AFP, LeanDojo, Lean Copilot,
Draft--Sketch--Prove, DeepSeek-Prover, and Llemma provide theorem-prover
substrates, libraries, corpora, and proposal mechanisms for formalization
\cite{MouraUllrich2021Lean4,deMouraKADR2015Lean,BlanchetteHMN2015AFP,Wenzel2019IsabelleAFP,YangEtAl2023LeanDojo,SongYangAnandkumar2024LeanCopilot,JiangEtAl2023DraftSketchProve,XinEtAl2024DeepSeekProver,AzerbayevEtAl2023Llemma}.
Here such tools motivate the advisory-agent gate: generated suggestions remain
non-load-bearing until deterministic finite rows and a human-promotion boundary
place them in an accepted coordinate.

Artifact and reproducibility systems give stronger evidence units than the one
claimed here.  ReproZip, CODECHECK, RO-Crate, BagIt/RFC 8493, PROV-O, in-toto,
SLSA, DataLad, Nix/NixOS, Guix, CWL, Snakemake, Sumatra, Software Heritage
identifiers, the Software Citation Principles, and FAIR4RS can support archived
research objects, execution certificates, provenance graphs, supply-chain
attestations, rebuild environments, workflow descriptions, or software-identity
claims
\cite{ChirigatiRampinShashaFreire2016ReproZip,EglenEtAl2021CODECHECK,SoilandReyesEtAl2022ROCrate,KunzeEtAl2018BagItRFC8493,LeboEtAl2013PROVO,TorresAriasEtAl2019InToto,OpenSSF2023SLSA10,HalchenkoEtAl2021DataLad,DolstraDeJongeVisser2004Nix,DolstraLohPierron2010NixOS,ValletMichonneauTournier2022Guix,CWLCommunity2016,MoelderEtAl2021Snakemake,Davison2012SumatraContext,DavisonEtAl2014Sumatra,DiCosmoGruenpeterZacchiroli2020SWHID,SmithEtAl2016SoftwareCitationPrinciples,BarkerEtAl2022FAIR4RS}.

Signed and transparency-log artifact-identity systems are especially close to
the archive half of this paper.  TUF specifies repository metadata and threshold
signatures for update authenticity; Sigstore combines signing with transparency
log inclusion; SPDX gives a standardized software bill of materials; the NIST
SSDF names secure-software-development practices; and Citation File Format gives
machine-readable software citation metadata
\cite{SamuelEtAl2010SurvivableKeyCompromise,TUFSpecification,SigstoreDocs,SPDXSpec,NISTSP800218,CitationFileFormat}.
Those systems can establish who signed an artifact, which bytes or package
components are named, what dependency metadata is declared, or how software
should be cited.  They do not by themselves supply this paper's finite Horn
coordinate closure, scanner-substring theorem, typed-pointer certificate schema,
or branch-by-branch \(\ScriptOKstageA\) and \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\)
conformance proofs.  Conversely, the coordinate system here does not replace
signatures, transparency logs, SBOMs, secure-development controls, or citation
metadata; it only states which such records would be allowed to promote which
publication coordinates.
The archive contract in Theorem~\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}
is deliberately narrower: it asks only for ordinary-file byte equality, the
no-self digest table, the environment descriptor, locator identity, and the
explicit script-conformance premises needed by the two positive replay
coordinates.

For venue positioning, Table~\ref{tab:main-cicm-theorem-comparison} gives a
reproducible, corpus-defined comparison rather than a narrow keyword-only sample.
The screened corpus is the official CICM event-year proceedings for 2021--2025
and the citable CICM workshop records in that window
\cite{CICM2021Proceedings,CICM2021Workshops,CICM2022Proceedings,CICM2023Proceedings,CICM2024Proceedings,CICM2025Proceedings}.  The paper-local
transcript \artifactpath{certificates/cicm_search_transcript.json} contains the
row-level screen used for the table: 74 screened rows, one per retained
title-level hit in the stated corpus, with fields
\auditcode{year}, \auditcode{venue}, \auditcode{title},
\auditcode{doi_or_arxiv}, \auditcode{included_excluded},
\auditcode{keyword_hit}, and \auditcode{rationale}.  Running
\artifactcmd{python}{certificates/emit_cicm_screen_csv.py}
\artifactpath{certificates/cicm_search_transcript.json} emits exactly those
columns as CSV.  The screen retained (i) title or abstract hits for
\auditcode{certificate}, \auditcode{certified}, \auditcode{solver},
\auditcode{artifact}, \auditcode{replay}, \auditcode{library},
\auditcode{maintenance}, \auditcode{blockchain}, \auditcode{database},
\auditcode{similarity}, \auditcode{LLM}, or \auditcode{autoformalization}, and
(ii) any proceeding item whose contribution is a checker, certificate,
maintenance mechanism, archive/ledger mechanism, or formal-library data
interface even if those exact words do not appear in the title.  The
machine-readable rows give inclusion or exclusion labels and rationales; the PDF
table below is only the theorem-by-theorem comparison distilled from that
finite screen.  For CICM 2025, rows are named by event year; the bibliography
records Springer publication and copyright metadata.  This is a reproducible
finite bibliographic screen, not a claim about unqueried databases or later
metadata changes.

 \section{Conclusion}
 The useful abstraction is not an assertion about who or what produced a theorem
 candidate.  It is the finite publication-coordinate route that assigns each
 artifact to the strongest claim it is entitled to support.  For the current
 PDF-level article the unconditional claim is the printed six-coordinate finite
 specification and Horn calculation
 \[
 (\qlex,q_{\mathrm{rgs}},q_{\mathrm{src}},q_{\mathrm{art}},q_{\mathrm{ext}},
 q_{\mathrm{ven}})=(\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
 \mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})
 \]
 for the base displayed relation.  Adjoining the explicit scanner-success
 premise gives the intermediate finite relation with \(\qlex\) positive only as a
 decoded begin-token substring witness.
 Adjoining the external branch-by-branch \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise after that
 gives the conditional mathematical relation with \(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) positive.
 The executable reading is outside this PDF-only specification.  The conditional
 archive theorem states the needed archive, scanner, digest, run, script, and
 frame premises; Table~\ref{tab:scope-register} is the single main-text register
 for stronger coordinates that remain outside the public claim.

\paragraph{Supplement boundary.}
The source package retains
\artifactpath{appendix_full_technical_ledger.tex} and the material below this
file's first \verb|\end{document}| as non-load-bearing supplement text.  Those
sources collect Zeckendorf transport, Richardson variants, factorial-spike
stress tests, long ledger closures, and generated audit wrappers.  They are not
compiled into the main article and do not add public coordinates beyond
Theorems~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy},
\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}, and
\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}.
