\section{Replay artifact boundary}
\label{sec:replay-artifact-boundary}
This section states the archive preconditions that would be needed to turn the
printed finite specification into a non-operative conditional replay predicate.
The preconditions are part of the specification; this PDF-only article does not
assert that they have been satisfied by a supplied archive.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The PDF article prints predicates, digests, and the archive contract.
\item The certificate files named below are intended replay bytes for a later
archive-instantiated submission.
\item An executable claim would require a non-PDF artifact submission that
supplies those bytes as ordinary non-symlink files, matches the byte-equality
ledger, pins the replay environment, adds a stable public or venue-controlled
locator, and includes the required script-conformance proof.
\end{enumerate}
The conditional replay claim is instantiated precisely when a review package
satisfies those three bullets using
Tables~\ref{tab:replay-artifact-manifest}--\ref{tab:artifact-submission-checklist},
Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger}, Table~\ref{tab:artifact-locator-status}, and
Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}.  The observed local
\texttt{python -VV}/Windows/NTFS run is provenance for the descriptor, not an
alternative theorem premise.

The conditional review commands are displayed separately so their path tokens do
not carry mathematical content.
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{0.93\linewidth}{Y}
\artifactcmd{python}{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py}\\
\artifactcmd{python}{certificates/replay_case_rows.py}
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
Under the separate \(\ScriptOKstageA\) branch-by-branch conformance premise, the
first command may be cited as implementing the finite relation that checks the
rows in the inner no-self digest table, rejects an attempted row for
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}, compiles manifest bytes
to typed atoms, forward-chains the fixed Horn schema, validates certificate DAGs,
and compares the recomputed six-coordinate vector with the replay report.  The
script path alone is not a premise of the mathematical archive predicate.  The
command does not compute the outer printed digest-table anchor in
Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger}; that row is an archive-review condition on
the submitted digest-table bytes.  Under its own conformance premise, the second
command may be cited as implementing the finite reconstruction of the four
printed historical case rows from the review bundle and the expected-row JSON
file in Table~\ref{tab:replay-artifact-manifest}.  The eligible case universe is
closed by the finite compiler case set:
Newmath intake isolation, upper-fibers overlap blocking, fake-extension
blocking, and Rule110 limitation disclosure.  A manifest row is eligible only if
its case name is in that set, its local provenance list and submitted snapshot
list have the same length, every snapshot path passes the relative-root,
\texttt{resolve(strict=True)}, \texttt{is\_relative\_to}, ordinary-file,
\texttt{lstat()} non-symlink, and SHA--256 checks of
Tables~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}--\ref{tab:replay-byte-binding}, and its boundary
declares historical or path-verified scope.  The same command also runs a
negative control: a false fifth row claiming Rule110 dynamic semantic validation
from the limitation snapshot is rejected by the same case-set verifier.  These
commands check finite records only; Table~\ref{tab:scope-register} records the
coordinates that would require additional rows.

\subsection{Trusted computing base for replay}
\label{subsec:replay-tcb}
The replay theorem is conditional on the trusted computing base in
Tables~\ref{tab:replay-tcb} and~\ref{tab:replay-byte-binding}.  The normative
predicate is short.  For an archive root \(R\) and descriptor bytes \(E\), write
\(\operatorname{EnvOK}(R,E)\) when the byte-bound descriptor pins: ordinary-file
byte reads; relative-path normalization rejecting absolute paths, \texttt{..},
backslashes, directories, and symlinks; UTF--8-sig JSON decoding with the object
semantics in Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}; SHA--256 over raw bytes; and a command
working directory/root field resolving exactly to \(R\).  A container, Nix, Guix,
ReproZip, or venue-controlled descriptor is equivalent only if it entails this
same predicate and is itself byte-bound by the archive ledger.  The CPython
3.10.11/Windows/NTFS transcript is only the observed descriptor example; it is
not an additional theorem premise and not a portability claim for every host
with a similar version string.
\begin{table}
\caption{Trusted computing base for parser and filesystem semantics.}
\label{tab:replay-tcb}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{L{0.22\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Component & Assumed semantics and rejection policy \\ \midrule
Digest-bound environment descriptor & A replay-instantiated archive must include
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_replay_environment.json} as an ordinary
non-symlink file, and that file must be byte-bound by the inner digest table and
the outer printed ledger.  This JSON descriptor is the operative environment
premise for the replay theorem.  It has the status of an equivalent descriptor:
in a final artifact it may be replaced by, or accompanied by, a container recipe,
Nix flake/derivation, Guix manifest, ReproZip package, or venue-controlled
descriptor only if the replacement pins at least the same interpreter,
standard-library, filesystem, path-normalization, JSON-decoding, digest, and
working-directory semantics.\\
\addlinespace
Non-normative observed descriptor example & The submitted descriptor records
CPython \(3.10.11\),
the exact \texttt{python -VV} transcript
\(\texttt{Python 3.10.11}\),
\(\texttt{tags/v3.10.11:7d4cc5a}\),
\(\texttt{Apr 5 2023 00:38:17}\), and
\(\texttt{MSC v.1929 64 bit (AMD64)}\), and the standard-library modules
\texttt{json}, \texttt{pathlib}, \texttt{hashlib}, \texttt{subprocess},
\texttt{stat}, \texttt{sys}, and \texttt{os}.  The observed run used
\texttt{Windows-10-10.0.26200-SP0} on a local NTFS worktree.  That host string
records provenance only; it is not a permission to instantiate the replay claim
from an unarchived local host.  No theorem below proves Python, the host kernel,
process scheduling, NTFS, descriptor tooling, or hardware correctness.\\
\addlinespace
JSON decoding & Each JSON input is read as raw bytes, decoded as
\texttt{utf-8-sig}, and parsed by \texttt{json.loads}.  Arrays retain file
order; objects are the finite dictionaries returned by the parser; strings are
Unicode strings; \texttt{null} maps to \texttt{None}; and if an object repeats a
key, the retained value is the parser's last value for that key.  Missing files
and parse failures reject the artifact-instantiated claim.\\
\addlinespace
Filesystem root and normalization & All artifact paths are interpreted relative
to the submitted paper root \(R\).  The root is first fixed as
\texttt{Path(root).resolve(strict=True)} after the archive-format check in
Lemma~\ref{lem:archive-format-normalization}.  A candidate manifest string \(s\)
is admissible only when it is an ASCII relative POSIX path printed in the digest
ledger, its components are nonempty, its parsed parts contain neither
\texttt{.}, \texttt{..}, an absolute-root component, drive syntax, nor
backslashes, and byte-for-byte comparison with the canonical path list is exact.
The accepted path language is therefore case-sensitive and Unicode-normalization
free: no lowercasing, uppercasing, NFC/NFD conversion, percent decoding, locale
folding, or operating-system display-name normalization is applied.  After
byte-preserving unpacking, the candidate \texttt{p=(R / s).resolve(strict=True)}
must satisfy \texttt{p.is\_relative\_to(R)} in the Python \(3.10.11\)
\texttt{pathlib} sense.  The finite atom compiler uses the original POSIX
relative byte string after these checks, not a machine-specific absolute path.\\
\addlinespace
Links, aliases, and mutable roots & Archive members with symlink, hard-link,
device, FIFO, socket, directory-as-file, or platform-specific reparse semantics
are rejected before extraction.  For every accepted file path after extraction
the parent chain is resolved under \(R\), the final directory entry is checked by
\texttt{Path.lstat()}, and the mode must be a regular file and must not be a
symbolic link.  Distinct manifest strings must also have distinct post-unpack
file identities: on systems exposing \texttt{st\_dev}/\texttt{st\_ino}, the pairs
must differ; on systems without reliable inode identity, the archive descriptor
must certify that no two required names alias the same mutable storage object.
Case-folding aliases, Unicode-normalization aliases, short-name aliases,
network mounts, live archive mounts, and mutable virtual filesystems therefore
reject the archive instantiation instead of becoming host-dependent cases.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

\begin{lemma}[Archive format normalization and alias rejection]
\label{lem:archive-format-normalization}
Let \(Z\) be a submitted replay archive.  Let \(P_{\mathrm{outer}}\) be the
finite relative-path set printed in
Tables~\ref{tab:replay-artifact-manifest}--\ref{tab:artifact-submission-checklist}
and let \(P_{\mathrm{inner}}\) be the finite path domain parsed from
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}.  The required set is
\(P_{\mathrm{req}}=P_{\mathrm{outer}}\cup P_{\mathrm{inner}}\), so review-bundle and
certificate-support entries reached only through the inner digest table receive
the same archive-format and no-alias guard before they are read.  The predicate
\(\ArchiveFormatOK(Z,R,P_{\mathrm{req}})\) is the following finite archive-format
condition.
\begin{enumerate}[label={\(\mathrm{F}_{\arabic*}\):}]
\item The archive format is byte-preserving for ordinary file payloads.  Extraction
copies the member payload byte string verbatim to the target file and performs no
newline conversion, character-set transcoding, executable filtering, decompressor
post-processing, or TeX interpretation.
\item Each required member name is exactly one ASCII POSIX relative path in
\(P_{\mathrm{req}}\).  Its separator is \texttt{/}; its components are nonempty;
no component is \texttt{.} or \texttt{..}; it has no leading slash, drive prefix,
backslash, NUL byte, percent-decoding convention, or platform extension.  Name
comparison is byte-for-byte, case-sensitive equality.  Unicode normalization is
not a hidden equivalence: because required names are ASCII, any member requiring
NFC/NFD, locale, or case-folding normalization is not the required member.
\item The archive has exactly one ordinary-file entry for each required name and
no required entry is represented as a symlink, hard link, directory, device, FIFO,
socket, sparse redirect, external-file reference, or implementation-specific
reparse point.  Duplicate entries for a required name reject the archive rather
than being resolved by last-writer or first-writer convention.
\item Extraction is deterministic: entries are processed in lexicographic order of
their POSIX byte names into a fresh empty root \(R\).  Before writing a required
file, all parent directories must already be directories created for this
extraction, and no extraction step may overwrite a previously written required
file or replace a directory by a file.
\item After extraction, every \(p\in P_{\mathrm{req}}\) satisfies the ordinary
non-symlink relative-root test of Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}, and distinct
required names have distinct post-unpack file identities.  If the host exposes
\texttt{st\_dev}/\texttt{st\_ino}, the identity test is inequality of those
pairs; otherwise the descriptor must supply an equivalent no-hard-link/no-alias
certificate for the required entries.
\item The extraction root is immutable during replay: no required file, parent
directory, mount binding, case-folding rule, Unicode-normalization rule, or
archive view may change between the post-unpack identity check and the byte reads
used for digest comparison.
\end{enumerate}
Then, under \(\ArchiveFormatOK(Z,R,P_{\mathrm{req}})\), every required archive
path denotes a unique ordinary non-symlink file under \(R\), the path key used by
the finite relation is the printed POSIX byte string, and
\(\operatorname{readBytes}_{R}(p)\) is exactly the payload byte string of the
unique archive member named \(p\).  No conclusion of the replay theorem may be
instantiated from a host tree or archive unpacker that omits any one of
\(\mathrm{F}_{1}\)--\(\mathrm{F}_{6}\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Clause \(\mathrm{F}_{2}\) fixes the name language before the operating system is
consulted, so absolute paths, parent traversal, drive syntax, case folding,
Unicode normalization, and separator conversion never become admissible aliases.
Clause \(\mathrm{F}_{3}\) removes all archive-level link and special-file forms,
including hard links; hence an accepted member contributes only an ordinary-file
payload under its own printed name.  Clauses \(\mathrm{F}_{4}\) and
\(\mathrm{F}_{5}\) make extraction order and post-unpack identity finite checks:
there is one entry for each required name, no overwrite convention is used, and
two required names cannot denote the same post-unpack storage object.  Clause
\(\mathrm{F}_{1}\) identifies the bytes written for that object with the archive
payload bytes, and \(\mathrm{F}_{6}\) keeps that object stable until the digest
read.  Combining these six checks gives a total function from
\(P_{\mathrm{req}}\) to unique ordinary files under \(R\), with byte value equal
to the corresponding archive payload.  Therefore later SHA--256 and JSON
parsing premises depend only on the finite archive entries and not on CPython,
\texttt{pathlib}, local case-folding behavior, hard-link provenance, or mutable
archive views beyond the explicitly named TCB predicates.
\end{proof}

\begin{table}
\caption{Byte binding, digest semantics, and command transcript convention.}
\label{tab:replay-byte-binding}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{L{0.22\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Component & Assumed semantics and rejection policy \\ \midrule
\addlinespace
Existence and bytes & File existence means successful
\texttt{resolve(strict=True)} after the relative-root check, followed by the
\texttt{is\_relative\_to(R)} containment test and the \texttt{lstat()} ordinary
non-symlink file test above.  Bare \texttt{Path.exists()} is not an evidence
predicate.  The bytes hashed or parsed are exactly the bytes returned by
\texttt{Path.read\_bytes()} for that checked path; there is no newline
conversion, macro expansion, archive unpacking, or \TeX{} interpretation.\\
\addlinespace
Digest algorithm & Every digest row means
\texttt{hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()} on those raw bytes: SHA--256 over the
byte string, printed as a lower-case \(64\)-hex-character string.  Digest
comparison is exact string equality after the JSON or manifest field has been
parsed.\\
\addlinespace
Command transcript & A command-run row is trusted only as the finite transcript
fields named by the row: \texttt{command}, \texttt{working\_directory},
\texttt{source\_commit}, \texttt{environment}, \texttt{stdout},
\texttt{stderr}, \texttt{exit\_code}, and \texttt{log\_path}, or the
corresponding subset actually present in the schema.  The key
\texttt{source\_commit} is the schema's canonical immutable source field.  Exit code
\(0\) means the operating system reported successful process termination for
that command; nonzero exit, timeout, missing transcript field, or missing log
rejects the command-run coordinate.  The transcript is evidence only for the
printed finite predicate named by that command row; it is not a proof that the
command's source implements any unstated relation.\\
\addlinespace
PDF binding & The PDF is not used as an executable byte oracle.  The theorem
statements use a two-level manifest convention.  The inner support manifest is
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}; it contains no row for
itself and is the digest table used by the finite byte-binding relation.  The
outer anchor is the printed ledger row in Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger}
giving the SHA--256 of that digest-table file.  A current-byte theorem claim is
accepted only when every required replay-support file named by the inner no-self
digest table is hash-bound by the relation and all inner rows match.  The outer
printed row lets a reviewer identify the digest-table bytes supplied with the
paper, but it is not an executable self-check and is not an additional accepted
input atom.  Recompiling the PDF after any artifact-byte change does not preserve
the claim unless a new digest/replay tuple is supplied.
\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

These operational assumptions fix the byte and path model used by the finite
predicates; the stronger coordinates remain governed by
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}.

\subsection{Record-gate adequacy schema without circular soundness}
The record-gate theorem used here is a finite schema theorem, not a proof that a
pipeline implementation is sound.  Let
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Kind=\{&\textsf{formal\mbox{-}source},\textsf{traceability},\textsf{route},
\textsf{submission},\\
&\textsf{artifact},\textsf{discovery},\textsf{work\mbox{-}selection}\}
\end{aligned}
\]
be the seven-element claim-kind set.  Let \(\Gate\) be a finite gate set and let
\(\Gamma:\Kind\to\mathcal P(\Gate)\) be a deterministic dispatch table.  A
certificate for a ledger claim \(c\) is a tuple
\[
C=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B),
\]
where \(K\subseteq\Kind\) is nonempty, \(\ell\) is an evidence level,
\(E=(E_{\mathrm{atom}},E_{\mathrm{path}})\) is a typed finite evidence surface,
\(H\) is the human-promotion coordinate, \(R\) is the finite list of pass rows,
\(D\) is the discovery-interface coordinate, and \(B\) is the command-boundary
coordinate.  Certificate records use exactly the evidence-level set
\[
\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}=
\{\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified},\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\}.
\]
This two-element set is a theorem hypothesis about the certificate-record schema
row, not a convention inferred from the artifact-pattern list.  In particular,
strings used elsewhere as pointer-boundary patterns, including manifest tags,
table tags, snapshot names, and historical-boundary tags, are outside
\(\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}\).
For this theorem the certificate schema row \(\Sigma\) is part of the submitted
checker input and is itself locked: the parsed value
\(\Sigma[\texttt{evidence\_levels}]\) is exactly
\(\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}\).  This is separate from the schema's
artifact-pattern vocabulary.  Schema vocabulary patterns such as
\texttt{explicit-historical-boundary:*} are not closed evidence atoms; only
whole-string \texttt{historical-boundary:}\(\alpha\) tokens can be admitted as
pointer-boundary atoms after the checks below.  They are not evidence levels and
are not read by \(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}\).  Define \(\Acc_{\Gamma}(c,C)\) by the six
finite predicates:
\begin{enumerate}[label={\(\mathrm{P}_{\arabic*}\):}]
\item \(c\) has a ledger row with all required record fields and
\(K\subseteq\Kind,\ K\ne\varnothing\).
\item \(\ell\in\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}\), and
\(E=(E_{\mathrm{atom}},E_{\mathrm{path}})\) is a submitted typed evidence
surface with finite atom and path components,
\(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\cup E_{\mathrm{path}}\ne\varnothing\), every member of
\(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\) satisfying the non-path evidence-atom grammar, and every
member of \(E_{\mathrm{path}}\) satisfying the relative support-path grammar.
The two grammars are disjoint, and a string with local support-path syntax is
rejected from \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\).  Thus there is no untyped membership clause
\(u\in E\) in this theorem: evidence membership means membership in one of the
two typed components displayed here.
\item Let \(G_C=\bigcup_{k\in K}\Gamma(k)\).  The pass rows form an exact
finite gate graph over \(G_C\): \(R\) is a finite list; every row has a
\texttt{gate\_name} field; the map \(r\mapsto r[\texttt{gate\_name}]\) is a
bijection from the rows of \(R\) onto \(G_C\).  Thus every required gate has one
and only one pass row, and no duplicate, stale, irrelevant, or contradictory
extra pass row is accepted.
\item Each row of \(R\) has the required pass fields, the same level \(\ell\), an
allowed decision, and an evidence pointer in the finite admissible-pointer set
declared for the row.  Prefix matches, wildcard expansion, and bare filesystem
existence are not admissible evidence.
\item \(D\) is present exactly when \(\textsf{discovery}\in K\).
\item \(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}(\ell,B)\) holds in the following typed
sense.  In this schema the canonical command-run source field is
\texttt{source\_commit}; there is no separate \texttt{source\_state} key in
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}\).
\end{enumerate}
Write \(F_{\mathrm{cmd}}\) for the five required command-run field names
\[
\{\texttt{command},\texttt{source\_commit},\texttt{environment},
\texttt{exit\_code},\texttt{log\_path}\}.
\]
Relative to the same locked schema row \(\Sigma\), the command-boundary
predicate is the conjunction of the following three typed clauses:
\begin{enumerate}[label={\(\mathrm{B}_{\arabic*}\):}]
\item The locked certificate schema has
\(\Sigma[\texttt{evidence\_levels}]=\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}\), and the
entry level satisfies \(\ell\in\Sigma[\texttt{evidence\_levels}]\).  Thus the
two command-boundary levels are exactly
\(\{\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified},\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\}\).  Historical
case boundaries, manifest tags, wildcard-looking schema documentation strings,
and table labels are pointer-boundary atoms only; they are never members of this
level set, even if they occur in a schema artifact-pattern field.
\item If \(\ell=\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified}\), then \(B\) contains
\texttt{boundary.no\_command\_run\_assertion}.
\item If \(\ell=\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\), then
\texttt{boundary.command\_run\_fields} is a finite dictionary and its key set
contains \(F_{\mathrm{cmd}}\).
\end{enumerate}
For mnemonic clause labels retained from the record specification, let
\(\operatorname{NoE9}(e)\) mean that entry \(e\) satisfies clause E9 and let
\(\operatorname{NoE10}(e)\) mean that entry \(e\) satisfies clause E10.
Thus
\[
\Acc_{\Gamma}(c,C)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{6}P_i(c,C).
\]
Record-gate adequacy is the following separate finite input predicate
\(\RGA(P,L,\Gamma)\) on a submitted package \(P\), finite acceptance ledger
\(L\), and dispatch table \(\Gamma\):
\[
\RGA(P,L,\Gamma)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\forall c\in L_{\mathrm{acc}}\ \exists C\ 
\bigl(C=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\land \Acc_{\Gamma}(c,C)\bigr),
\]
where \(L_{\mathrm{acc}}\) is the finite set of ledger rows marked accepted.
This predicate is adequacy only for the finite record schema above.  It is not
derived from \(\Acc_{\Gamma}\) for a single row, and it is not a theorem about
executable checker soundness, implementation soundness, proof-assistant
correctness, source completeness, semantic truth, archive availability, or audit
completeness.

\begin{lemma}[Finite schema consequence of record-gate adequacy]
\label{lem:rga-schema-consequence}
If \(\RGA(P,L,\Gamma)\) holds, then for every accepted claim \(c\in L_{\mathrm{acc}}\) there
exists a certificate \(C\) such that \(\Acc_{\Gamma}(c,C)\).  If the submitted
ledger also imposes a total order on candidate certificates, the
lexicographically least such \(C\) defines a function
\(\mathsf{Cert}_{\Gamma}(c)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
This is only unpacking of the input predicate \(\RGA(P,L,\Gamma)\).  By the
displayed definition, for each \(c\in L_{\mathrm{acc}}\) the predicate supplies
one finite certificate satisfying exactly the six predicates \(P_1,\ldots,P_6\)
inside \(\Acc_{\Gamma}\).  A total order on the finite candidate-certificate set
gives a unique least satisfying certificate whenever the set is nonempty.  The
conclusion is therefore only about finite record predicates and finite
certificate selection; no implementation soundness of a gate program is
inferred.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact pass-row graph has no conflict resolution]
\label{lem:exact-pass-row-graph-no-conflicts}
Fix a certificate \(C=(K,\ell,E,H,R,D,B)\) and let
\(G_C=\bigcup_{k\in K}\Gamma(k)\).  Predicate \(P_3\) in
\(\Acc_{\Gamma}(c,C)\) is equivalent to the conjunction
\[
\begin{aligned}
\{r[\texttt{gate\_name}]:r\in R\}&=G_C,\\
\forall g\in G_C,\quad
|\{r\in R:r[\texttt{gate\_name}]=g\}|&=1,
\end{aligned}
\]
with every row of \(R\) carrying a \texttt{gate\_name} field.  Therefore
acceptance uses no first-row, last-row, priority, or majority conflict
resolution.  Adding any extra row to an already accepted certificate destroys
\(P_3\): if its gate name is in \(G_C\) it creates multiplicity \(2\) for that
required gate, and if its gate name is outside \(G_C\) it creates a row outside
the required gate set.  Thus this lemma is the local uniqueness repair for
\(P_3\), not a coverage-only condition.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The displayed definition of \(P_3\) says that the projection
\(r\mapsto r[\texttt{gate\_name}]\) is a bijection from the finite row list
\(R\) onto \(G_C\).  A finite-map bijection is exactly surjectivity onto
\(G_C\), injectivity on rows, and absence of undefined gate-name values.
Surjectivity gives at least one row for each required gate; injectivity gives at
most one row for each required gate; and the codomain condition excludes every
row whose gate name is not required.  The displayed cardinality condition is
the same statement written gate-by-gate.  If a new row is appended, the new
projection cannot remain bijective: its name either duplicates an existing
required image or lies outside \(G_C\).  Thus irrelevant, stale, duplicate, or
contradictory rows cannot be ignored while preserving acceptance.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[E10 is exactly the command-run boundary clause]
\label{lem:e10-command-boundary-b3}
Let \(e\) be a parsed ledger entry whose evidence level is
\(\ell=\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\), and let \(B=e[\texttt{boundary}]\).  Use
the canonical field name \texttt{source\_commit}; a clause using
\texttt{source\_state} would not be this lemma's E10 clause.
Then
\[
\operatorname{NoE10}(e)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\mathrm{B}_3\text{ of }\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}(\ell,B).
\]
Equivalently, clause E10 rejects exactly when the command-run boundary clause
fails:
\[
\mathrm{E10}(e)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\neg \mathrm{B}_3.
\]
This is only a finite record-schema test.  A command line or script name passing
E10 is not executable evidence unless the corresponding \(\ScriptOK\) premise is
separately supplied.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For a command-run entry the displayed E10 clause inspects only the dictionary
path \texttt{boundary.command\_run\_fields}.  It rejects precisely when at least
one member of
\[
F_{\mathrm{cmd}}=
\{\texttt{command},\texttt{source\_commit},\texttt{environment},
\texttt{exit\_code},\texttt{log\_path}\}
\]
is absent from that dictionary.  Clause \(\mathrm{B}_2\) of
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}\) handles the different path-verified case and is
not evaluated for a command-run entry.  Clause \(\mathrm{B}_3\) says precisely
that the same dictionary exists as
a finite dictionary and that its key set contains this same finite set
\(F_{\mathrm{cmd}}\).  Therefore satisfaction of E10 is equivalent to
\(\mathrm{B}_3\), and rejection by E10 is its negation.  No other displayed
clause inspects these five command-run keys.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 2a (schema): pure finite certificate-record acceptance]
\label{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec-supplement}
This theorem gives only the finite mathematical relation for certificate-record
acceptance after parsing.  Its inputs are an abstract finite parsed tuple
\[
(B,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D,S)
\]
consisting of a parsed certificate schema, ledger, submission-interface map,
primary-claim inventory, review-bundle manifest, case-row manifest, dispatch
table, digest table, and snapshot-boundary table.  The theorem does not assert that the current PDF
contains, supplies, or reconstructs any operative JSON bytes for those objects.
Byte decoding, root normalization, symlink rejection, and SHA--256 equality are
preconditions of the separate artifact-instantiated theorem
\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}, not conclusions of this pure
schema theorem.  In the present theorem, let \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D(p)\)
mean the abstract predicate that \(p\) is an admissible ordinary relative path
under the selected root and is bound by an exact SHA--256 row in the supplied
digest table \(D\).  Python-script conformance is likewise outside this theorem
and is the separate premise of
Lemma~\ref{lem:certificate-record-scriptok-separate}.

The typed string grammars used below are the normative grammar for the finite
route specification and are whole-string matched.  Let
\(\alpha\) range over nonempty strings matching
\texttt{[A-Za-z0-9\_:.-]+}, let \(\beta\) range over nonempty strings matching
\texttt{[A-Za-z0-9\_./-]+}, and require every
\(\beta\)-path to contain no empty segment, no \texttt{.} segment, no
\texttt{..} segment, no leading slash, and no backslash.  The current-ledger
tokens in Table~\ref{tab:current-ledger-instantiation} use the normalized forms
\texttt{manifest:case\_evidence/0} and
\texttt{historical-boundary:}\(\alpha\), and the top-level support files listed
below are explicit alternatives.  Then
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
T_{\mathrm{table}}&::=&\texttt{table:}\alpha,\\
T_{\mathrm{formal}}&::=&(\texttt{definition:}|\texttt{lemma:}|\texttt{proposition:}|
\texttt{theorem:}|\texttt{corollary:})\alpha,\\
T_{\mathrm{manifest}}&::=&\texttt{manifest:}\beta,\\
T_{\mathrm{hist}}&::=&\texttt{historical-boundary:}\alpha,\\
T_{\mathrm{snapshot}}&::=&\texttt{review\_bundle/case\_snapshots/}\beta,\\
E_{\mathrm{atom}}&::=&T_{\mathrm{table}}\mid T_{\mathrm{formal}}
\mid T_{\mathrm{manifest}}\mid T_{\mathrm{hist}},\\
E_{\mathrm{path}}&::=&\texttt{certificates/}\beta\mid\texttt{review\_bundle/}\beta
\mid\texttt{submission\_abstract.tex}\mid\texttt{main.tex}
\mid\texttt{appendix\_full\_technical\_ledger.tex}
\mid\texttt{source\_interface\_record.json}
\mid\texttt{PIPELINE.md}\mid\texttt{SOURCE\_MAP.md}
\mid\texttt{ARTIFACT\_INVENTORY.md}
\mid\texttt{theorem\_inventory.json}.
\end{array}
\]
A manifest tag is accepted only when the \(\beta\) string is a slash-separated
JSON-pointer path present in \(M\), using literal object keys and decimal list
indices without wildcard or escape syntax; a historical-boundary tag only when an
eligible case row names the exact tag; and a snapshot path only when it is a
\(T_{\mathrm{snapshot}}\) string in an eligible submitted case row.  These
productions are pairwise disjoint: every atom token
contains a colon before any slash-bearing path position, while every path token
starts with one of the displayed path prefixes or one of the displayed exact
file names and contains no colon.  Strings with wildcards, prefixes, absolute-path
syntax, malformed \texttt{..} segments, or unlisted table/manifest/historical
names fail parsing before \(\PointerOK\) is evaluated.

Let \(\Gamma\) be the finite schema dispatch table parsed from the same
schema object as \(\Sigma\).  Define
\(\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(B,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D,S)\) to mean that the
parsed certificate schema, ledger, submission-interface map, primary-claim
inventory, review-bundle manifest, case-row manifest, dispatch table, digest
table, and snapshot-boundary table satisfy all displayed clauses M1--M3,
C1--C5, L1--E10, R1--R4, I1--I2, and P1--P5 below.  The
theorem's logical form is therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(B,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D,S)\\
&\qquad{}\Longleftrightarrow
\text{all displayed clauses pass},
\end{aligned}
\]
for every already-parsed tuple satisfying the typed grammars just stated.  No
byte-parsing, root-normalization, or current-record byte premise is hidden in
this theorem; such premises belong only to an archive or byte-supplied
instantiation that actually provides the raw records.

For a parsed ledger entry with typed evidence surface
\(E=(E_{\mathrm{atom}},E_{\mathrm{path}})\), schema row \(\Sigma\),
review-bundle manifest dictionary \(M\), and snapshot-boundary table \(S\).
\(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\) contains only closed evidence atoms: whole-string table or
formal labels, manifest-row tags whose slash-separated path is present in \(M\), and exact
historical-boundary tags named by an eligible submitted case row.  It does not
contain wildcard patterns, prefixes, raw absolute paths, or raw relative support
paths.  \(E_{\mathrm{path}}\) contains local support paths and is admissible only
through \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\).  These two parsed lists are typed by
disjoint grammars, so a string with relative support-path syntax cannot be
accepted by first placing it in \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\).  Define the digest-bound
boundary predicates used by evidence pointers as follows.
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(M)=
\{\,p :\;& p \text{ is a relative path named in }M,\\
& D[p]=M[p].\operatorname{sha256}\,\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \(D[p]\) is an abstract digest-table field in the parsed tuple, not a PDF
instruction to read a file.  A concrete archive may instantiate \(D[p]\) by raw
byte equality only through Theorem~\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}.
Let the submitted table/formal-label boundary be byte-bound rather than
PDF-visual.  For submitted \path{main.tex} byte string \(B\) and digest table
\(D\), define
\[
\TableBoundaryScan(B,D)=T
\]
as follows.  First require that \(B\) is the ordinary digest-bound row
\(\texttt{main.tex}\) in \(D\) and that \(B\) decodes as \texttt{utf-8-sig};
otherwise the scan is undefined.  Split the decoded string by
\path{str.splitlines()} and process the resulting finite line list in order.  A
display boundary token is emitted only by one of the following whole-source
regular-expression events:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item a table event is the literal token \auditcode{\\begin}, a left brace, the
literal word \auditcode{table}, a right brace, the shortest following substring,
then the literal token \auditcode{\\label}, a left brace, a captured string
\(x\) with no right brace, and a right brace;
\item a formal event is the literal token \auditcode{\\begin}, a left brace, one
of the literal words \auditcode{proposition}, \auditcode{lemma},
\auditcode{theorem}, or \auditcode{corollary}, a right brace, an optional
single bracketed title with no right bracket, the shortest following substring,
then the same literal \auditcode{\\label} capture of \(x\).
\end{enumerate}
where \(\texttt{.*?}\) is evaluated on the finite decoded source with newline
matching enabled and with the leftmost-first semantics of Python
\auditcode{re.finditer}.  A table event emits \(\texttt{table:}x\) for its label
\(x\).  A formal event emits \(e\texttt{:}x\), where \(e\) is the environment
name and \(x\) is its label.  The scan rejects if an emitted token is duplicated
or if \(x\) is empty, contains a right brace, or contains a newline.  Otherwise
\(\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}\) is exactly this finite emitted token set.
It contains no atom inferred from the rendered PDF, from an auxiliary
\path{.aux} file, from TeX macro expansion, from \path{\textbackslash input},
or from a \(\rawBeginTokenWitness\) scanner row.  We write
\(\operatorname{TableBoundaryOK}(B,D,T)\) exactly when the preceding scan is
defined and returns \(T=\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}\).  Let
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(S)=
\{\,p :\;& p \text{ occurs in an eligible submitted snapshot list}\\
& \text{and its submitted digest field agrees with }D[p]\,\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
A submitted snapshot list is eligible only through
Table~\ref{tab:case-row-eligibility}: its case name is in the fixed case set, its
local-path list and reviewable-snapshot list have the same length, every
snapshot path is relative under the submitted root grammar, its digest field
equals the supplied digest-table row \(D[p]\), and the row boundary declares
historical or path-verified scope.  Ordinary-file and non-symlink facts for those
rows are archive-instantiation facts, not PDF-only schema facts.

The admissible-pointer equivalence used by R4 is exactly the digest-bound finite
promotion predicate
\[
\begin{aligned}
\PointerOK(u,E,M,S)\Longleftrightarrow{}&
u\in E_{\mathrm{atom}}
\vee (u\in E_{\mathrm{path}}\wedge \operatorname{BoundPath}_D(u))\\
&{}\vee u\in \operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(M)\\
&{}\vee u\in \operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}
\vee u\in \operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(S).
\end{aligned}
\]
This displayed typed predicate is the entire R4 predicate.  There is no further
well-formedness, reachability, or filesystem-existence disjunct hidden outside
this equivalence.  The untyped
shortcut \(u\in E\) is not available in this theorem: a path-looking string that
is not in \(E_{\mathrm{path}}\) and digest-bound by
\(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\) fails R4 even if the same bytes could be written as
an atom string in some malformed record.
The evidence-surface membership clauses are literal membership in the typed
finite certificate evidence lists, not filesystem queries.  A non-path evidence
atom is admitted only through \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\) after the closed atom grammar
above has rejected wildcard patterns, prefixes, raw paths, and historical tags
not named by an eligible case row.  A local support path is admitted through
\(E_{\mathrm{path}}\) only when the same string also satisfies
\(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\).  A file's existence under the submitted root never
promotes it into evidence by itself.

For checker bookkeeping, the displayed relation realizes these four sets by
closed, digest-bound finite enumerations:
\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{0.96\linewidth}{@{}L{0.27\linewidth}Y@{}}
\toprule
Boundary & Finite realization \\ \midrule
\(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\) &
The literal non-path evidence atoms after typed schema parsing: whole-string
table/formal labels, manifest-row tags with JSON-pointer paths in \(M\), and exact historical
boundary tags named by eligible case rows.  These are not path prefixes,
wildcard patterns, evidence levels, or raw local paths.\\
\(E_{\mathrm{path}}\) &
The literal local-support-path evidence list after typed schema parsing; a
member contributes to \(\PointerOK\) only if it also satisfies
    \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\).\\
\(\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(M)\) &
The relative paths \(p\) named by manifest rows whose recorded
\texttt{sha256} field equals the SHA--256 of \(\operatorname{readBytes}(p)\).\\
\(\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}\) &
Whole-string atoms of the forms \texttt{table:}\(x\),
\texttt{proposition:}\(x\), \texttt{lemma:}\(x\), \texttt{theorem:}\(x\), and
\texttt{corollary:}\(x\) emitted by the deterministic
\(\TableBoundaryScan(B,D)\) pass over the digest-bound submitted
\path{main.tex} bytes.\\
\(\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(S)\) &
Relative snapshot paths occurring in eligible submitted case rows and passing
the same root, ordinary-file, symlink-rejection, and SHA--256 checks.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
This is a finite membership test.  The grammar admits only digest-bound manifest
paths, manifest-row tags whose slash-separated paths occur in \(M\), table or formal-label atoms
emitted by the digest-bound submitted \path{main.tex} table scan, exact historical-boundary tags
declared by eligible case rows, evidence paths whose row carries the required
digest-bound support, and submitted snapshot paths that pass the case-row
boundary checks.  A path-looking string placed in the atom component fails typed
parsing before R4 unless it is such an exact historical-boundary token; even then
it is a historical pointer boundary, not a command-boundary level.  A schema
value ending in \(\texttt{*}\), a tag prefix, a nonlisted table label, arbitrary
root-relative file existence, or a path that merely exists on disk does not
satisfy \(\PointerOK\).

The following displayed relation is the operative finite mathematical
design-pattern specification for any finite parsed certificate-record inputs
\artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json},
\artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json},
\artifactpath{review_bundle/submission_interface_map.json},
\artifactpath{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}, and
\artifactpath{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}.  It is not an archive
checker over ``the paths in Tables'' and does not claim that a prose checklist is
a deterministic manifest.  Such a software-checker claim would require a
digest-bound \artifactpath{archive_manifest.json} enumerating every required
relative path, SHA--256 digest, role, and command obligation, together with an
outer archive digest.  The words ``branch'', ``block'', and ``error'' below are
mnemonic names for Boolean clauses in this finite relation.  They do not assert
control flow, source-code line numbers, Python evaluation order, or executable
conformance.  Thus this theorem is not a machine-checkable pseudocode checker:
it is a non-executable mathematical design pattern until the separate
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) premise of
Lemma~\ref{lem:certificate-record-scriptok-separate} is discharged.  For E7 the
relation uses, for each parsed entry,
\[
G_C=\bigcup_{k\in K}\Gamma(k),\qquad
n_R(g)=|\{r\in R:r[\texttt{gate\_name}]=g\}|,
\]
on the subdomain where R1 holds for the relevant row.  E7 fails exactly when
some \(g\in G_C\) has \(n_R(g)\ne1\), or when some parsed row has
\(r[\texttt{gate\_name}]\notin G_C\).  The Python file
\artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py} is mentioned here
only as a submitted support artifact for a possible replay package.  It is not a
premise of this iff claim, and this theorem does not say that the Python file
implements the displayed relation.
\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.38\linewidth}L{0.14\linewidth}Y@{}}
\toprule
Mnemonic block name & Clause family & Mathematical obligation discharged here \\
\midrule
\texttt{check\_manifest\_required\_files} & M1--M3 &
Same required keys, same schema-prescribed relative path equality, and same
digest-table path predicate \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\).\\
\texttt{check\_case\_rows} & C1--C5 &
Same fixed case-row manifest, same accepted/rejected split, same eligible
snapshot paths, same digest-table equality for those paths, and same exact
historical-boundary atom set.\\
\texttt{check\_ledger\_entries} & L1--E10 &
Same seven-row cardinality lock, same entry-name set, same required fields, same
claim-kind predicate, same locked two-element certificate evidence-level set,
same typed evidence-surface predicates, same exact gate-graph cardinalities,
same discovery-interface parity, and same command-boundary clauses.\\
\texttt{check\_pass\_records} & R1--R4 &
Same pass-row required fields, same evidence-level equality, same allowed
decision set, and same finite pointer predicate \(\PointerOK(u,E,M,S)\).\\
\texttt{check\_submission\_interface} & I1--I2 &
Same four-row cardinality and same fixed submission-interface id set.\\
\texttt{check\_primary\_claim\_inventory} & P1--P5 &
Same minimum-row bound, same nonempty claim/status fields, and same certificate
and evidence-level requirements for rows not marked non-load-bearing.\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{L{0.14\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Clause & Rejecting condition in the mathematical specification \\ \midrule
M1 & A key \(k\) of \(\texttt{schema.manifest\_required\_files}\) is absent
from the manifest.\\
M2 & The manifest value at \(k\) is not the schema-prescribed relative path.\\
M3 & The schema-prescribed manifest path is not a digest-bound admissible
relative path under the paper root.\\
C1 & The case-row manifest does not have exactly the five case names of
Table~\ref{tab:case-row-eligibility}, with exactly the four accepted rows and
the Rule110 dynamic semantic validation negative-control row rejected.\\
C2 & A case row lacks its case name, skeleton, verifier result, boundary kind,
snapshot list, or finite digest map for listed snapshots.\\
C3 & The eligible-row set is not exactly the accepted-row subset, or an eligible
row has a boundary kind other than historical or path-verified, or a listed
snapshot path is not a normalized POSIX relative path below
\texttt{review\_bundle/case\_snapshots/}.\\
C4 & The finite snapshot-boundary table \(S\) differs from the snapshot paths
listed by eligible rows, or an eligible-row snapshot digest disagrees with the
corresponding digest-table row \(D[p]\), or a rejected row contributes to \(S\).\\
C5 & The historical-boundary atom set differs from the exact tags printed by
eligible rows, or admits a case name, skeleton name, rejected-row tag, wildcard,
or path prefix as a boundary atom.\\
L1 & \(\texttt{ledger.entries}\) does not have length \(7\).\\
L2 & The set of entry names is not exactly
\(\{\texttt{a1},\ldots,\texttt{a7}\}\).\\
E1 & A ledger entry lacks one of
\(\texttt{schema.record\_required\_fields}\).\\
E2 & Its \(\texttt{claim\_kind\_set}\) is not a nonempty list.\\
E3 & Its claim-kind set is not a subset of
\(\texttt{schema.claim\_kind\_domain}\).\\
E4 & The byte-bound schema row does not have
\(\texttt{schema.evidence\_levels}=\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}\), or the
entry's \(\texttt{evidence\_level}\) is not in that locked two-element set.\\
E5 & Its \(\texttt{evidence\_surface}\) does not parse as a nonempty typed
surface \(E=(E_{\mathrm{atom}},E_{\mathrm{path}})\) with finite components,
closed non-path atoms in \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\), relative support-path strings in
\(E_{\mathrm{path}}\), and disjoint atom/path grammars.\\
E6 & Its \(\texttt{pass\_records}\) field is not a nonempty list.\\
E7 & The pass rows are not an exact finite gate graph over
\(\bigcup_{k\in K}\Gamma(k)\): after rows lacking \texttt{gate\_name} are
rejected by R1, some required gate has zero rows, some required gate has more
than one row, or some row names a gate outside the assigned set.\\
E8 & The discovery-interface field is present for exactly one side of the
condition \(\textsf{discovery}\in K\).\\
E9 & A path-verified entry lacks
\(\texttt{boundary.no\_command\_run\_assertion}\), equivalently
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_2\) fails for that entry.\\
E10 & A command-run entry lacks one of
\(\texttt{command}\), \(\texttt{source\_commit}\),
\(\texttt{environment}\), \(\texttt{exit\_code}\), or
\(\texttt{log\_path}\) under \(\texttt{boundary.command\_run\_fields}\),
equivalently \(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_3\) fails for that entry
by Lemma~\ref{lem:e10-command-boundary-b3}.\\
R1 & A pass row lacks one of
\(\texttt{schema.pass\_record\_required\_fields}\).\\
R2 & A pass row's evidence level is not the parent entry's level.\\
R3 & A pass row's decision is not in \(\texttt{schema.pass\_decisions}\).\\
R4 & A pass row's evidence pointer \(u\) fails
\(\PointerOK(u,E,M,S)\) for the parsed typed entry evidence surface,
review-bundle manifest, table boundary, and submitted snapshot list.\\
I1 & The submission-interface map does not contain exactly four rows.\\
I2 & The submission-interface ids are not exactly
\(\{\texttt{sim1},\texttt{sim2},\texttt{sim3},\texttt{sim4}\}\).\\
P1 & The primary-claim inventory has fewer than \(12\) rows.\\
P2 & A primary-claim row lacks a nonempty \(\texttt{claim}\).\\
P3 & A primary-claim row lacks a nonempty \(\texttt{status}\).\\
P4 & A row whose status does not start with
\(\texttt{non-load-bearing}\) lacks a nonempty \(\texttt{certificate}\).\\
P5 & Such a load-bearing row lacks a nonempty \(\texttt{evidence\_level}\).\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
Thus E4 is a certificate-level vocabulary lock, not a pointer-boundary test:
historical case-row strings and artifact-pattern strings are available only to
E5/R4 after typed pointer parsing, and cannot enlarge the two command-boundary
levels used by \(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}\).
The displayed relation accepts exactly when none of the listed rejecting
conditions holds.  Consequently,
\(\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(B,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D,S)\) iff all rows satisfy
\(P_1,\ldots,P_6\) for \(\Acc_{\Gamma}\), the manifest bindings M1--M3 hold,
the case-row-manifest locks C1--C5 hold, the submission-interface locks I1--I2 hold, and the primary-claim locks P1--P5
hold.  Equivalently, for any finite parsed records satisfying the displayed
grammars,
\[
\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(B,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D,S)
\Longleftrightarrow
\text{clauses M1--M3, C1--C5, L1--E10, R1--R4, I1--I2, P1--P5 pass}.
\]
This schema theorem is not a free-standing assertion that the current records
have been supplied or parsed correctly, nor that
\artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py} implements the
relation.  The current seven-row instantiation is the separate byte-bound
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-ledger-bytes-to-acc}, and executable script conformance
is the separate Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-current-instance}.  The
relation rejects iff at least one displayed clause is violated.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
After the parsing precondition succeeds, every loop is over a finite JSON array
or over a finite dictionary supplied by the schema.  The displayed relation is
the conjunction of the complements of the listed rejecting clauses.  Each clause
is a Boolean predicate over the parsed records, schema domains, and dispatch
table; no clause mutates those data.  Hence a rejecting clause holds exactly on
violation of its displayed predicate.

The ledger-entry branches are precisely the acceptance predicates.  E1 together
with E2 and E3 is \(P_1\): the row has all required fields and a nonempty
\(K\subseteq\Kind\).  E4 and E5 are \(P_2\): the byte-bound schema row locks the
evidence-level vocabulary to \(\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}\), the entry level is
in that exact set, and the evidence surface is nonempty, finite, and typed.
The historical-boundary atoms admitted by E5 are pointer tokens, not additional
evidence levels.  E6, R1 restricted to the
\texttt{gate\_name} field, and E7 are \(P_3\): there is a finite pass-row list,
every row has a gate name, and the gate-name projection is a bijection onto the
assigned gate set \(\bigcup_{k\in K}\Gamma(k)\).  Consequently a missing row,
duplicate row for the same assigned gate, contradictory pair of rows for the
same gate, stale row for an unassigned gate, or irrelevant extra row all violate
the displayed relation and cannot be hidden behind mere coverage.  R1--R4
are \(P_4\): every pass row has the remaining required fields, agrees with the
parent evidence level, has an allowed decision, and has a permitted evidence
pointer according to the displayed finite relation
\(\PointerOK(u,E,M,S)\).  Here \(E\) is always the ordered typed pair
\((E_{\mathrm{atom}},E_{\mathrm{path}})\); the proof never uses an untyped
premise \(u\in E\).  The R4 branch is therefore an exact typed membership test in
the finite union
\[
\begin{aligned}
E_{\mathrm{atom}}
&{}\cup \{u\in E_{\mathrm{path}}:\operatorname{BoundPath}_D(u)\}\\
&{}\cup \operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(M)\cup
\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}\cup \operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(S).
\end{aligned}
\]
R4 fails iff the pointer is outside that union.  Both directions of this
statement are by the same five typed disjuncts: membership in
\(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\), digest-bound membership in \(E_{\mathrm{path}}\),
manifest-boundary membership, table-boundary membership, or snapshot-boundary
membership.  No inclusion into a larger evidence set is used.  Consequently the
equivalence
\[
\text{R4 passes}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\PointerOK(u,E,M,S)
\]
is literal after expanding \(E\) as the typed pair above.  If \(u\) is a local
support path, membership through the evidence surface requires exact listing in
\(E_{\mathrm{path}}\) and \(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D(u)\).  The typed parse
precondition forbids listing that path-looking string in \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\),
so atom membership cannot bypass byte binding.  Manifest membership requires a
named relative manifest path and equality between the printed SHA--256 field and
the hash of the bytes read at that path.  Snapshot membership requires
occurrence in an eligible submitted snapshot list and the same relative-root and
digest checks.
If \(u\) is a table or formal-label atom, the whole string must be in the
digest-bound submitted \(\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}\).  Thus the proof uses no rule of the form
``pointer denotes an existing file under the root'': clause R4 fails
exactly when
\[
\neg\PointerOK(u,E,M,S).
\]
Prefix matching, filesystem existence, schema wildcards, and nonlisted table or
snapshot names all fail R4.
E8 is \(P_5\), the
exact discovery-interface discipline.  Because E4 checks the byte-bound schema
row \(\texttt{schema.evidence\_levels}\) for equality with the certificate set
\[
\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}=
\{\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified},\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\},
\]
and then checks \(\ell\) against that same parsed set, E4 is exactly the
\(\mathrm{B}_1\) clause of \(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}\).  It does not consult
the schema's artifact-pattern list and does not treat historical case-boundary
strings as levels.  Those strings are checked only through \(\PointerOK\) as
closed pointer atoms or snapshot boundaries, so they cannot make E4 pass and
cannot create a third command-boundary level.  For
\(P_6\),
clause E9 is exactly the path-verified implication
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_2\): on a path-verified row it fails
precisely when the no-command-run assertion is absent, and on any other level it
is inactive.  Clause E10 is exactly the command-run implication
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_3\) by
Lemma~\ref{lem:e10-command-boundary-b3}: on a command-run row it fails precisely
when the five required command-run fields are not all present under
\texttt{boundary.command\_run\_fields}, and on any other level it is inactive.
Thus E9 and E10 together are not an additional predicate; they are
the two level-specific branches of \(P_6\).  L1 and L2 are the current-package
row lock selecting the seven submitted entries; they do not strengthen
\(\Acc_{\Gamma}\) for an individual row, but they fix the finite ledger to which
the theorem is applied.  M1--M3, C1--C5, I1--I2, and P1--P5 are likewise the
manifest, case-row-manifest, submission-interface, and primary-claim row locks
explicitly named in the statement.

If the displayed relation accepts, no listed rejecting clause holds; therefore
each selected ledger entry satisfies all six predicates defining
\(\Acc_{\Gamma}\), and the three families of row locks also hold.  Conversely,
if all six acceptance predicates and all row locks hold, then every listed
rejecting condition is false, so the displayed relation accepts.  This proves
exact equivalence between the printed finite predicate and the displayed
mathematical relation.  Script conformance and the stronger coordinates are
governed by Lemma~\ref{lem:certificate-record-scriptok-separate} and
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Digest-bound current-ledger bytes]
\label{def:current-ledger-bytes-ok}
\(\operatorname{CurrentLedgerBytesOK}(R)\) means the following byte-level
predicate.  Let
\[
P_{\mathrm{cert}}=
\left\{
\begin{array}{l}
\artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/submission_interface_map.json},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}
\end{array}
\right\}.
\]
The root \(R\) contains each \(p\in P_{\mathrm{cert}}\) as an ordinary
non-symlink file, the digest table
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json} contains the rows
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
\artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json}
&\mapsto&\texttt{47e3f2751b0177dc48e1607bac1d8c55b46cbc668dd081c71a20360e09069684},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}
&\mapsto&\texttt{65193d0eb722914e2573abf5f04858a8c2033f92d484ab11a5707d3b8b3fa066},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/submission_interface_map.json}
&\mapsto&\texttt{bcf46b25076ea6abafc88fd55768b1590e5b5305e6cea247be20219d833f3d1a},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}
&\mapsto&\texttt{e5c31cc92b92b50e3c736b717340d0808aa8eb16f4e23a913654a66fec0bf591},\\
\artifactpath{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}
&\mapsto&\texttt{4feadd22e96bd29b2af2c6da1e3533b48ecd14ec044efaf6193bb5809d11bec4},
\end{array}
\]
and for every such row \(p\mapsto d_p\),
\[
\operatorname{sha256}(\operatorname{readBytes}_{R}(p))=d_p.
\]
Decoding those five files as \texttt{utf-8-sig} JSON yields the parsed objects
\((\Sigma,L,I,Q,M)\), the case-row manifest \(C\) is the submitted finite
case-row manifest used by the review bundle, and the dispatch table
\(\Gamma=\Sigma.\texttt{gate\_dispatch}\).  Finally
\(\operatorname{JSONOK}(\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C)\) holds: \(L\) has ledger name
\texttt{current\_package\_pass\_records}, evidence level
\texttt{path-verified}, schema pointer
\artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json}, and exactly the seven
entries \(\texttt{a1},\ldots,\texttt{a7}\) with the JSON fields
\texttt{claim\_kind\_set}, \texttt{evidence\_level},
\texttt{evidence\_surface}, \texttt{pass\_records},
\texttt{discovery\_interface}, \texttt{human\_promotion}, and \texttt{boundary}
parsed from \artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}.
The canonicalization allowed inside \(\operatorname{JSONOK}\) is finite and
listed: \(\texttt{manifest:case\_evidence[}i\texttt{]}\) is rewritten to
\(\texttt{manifest:case\_evidence/}i\) for \(i=0,1,2,3\), and the two
\texttt{explicit-historical-boundary:} path strings in entry \(\texttt{a3}\)
are rewritten to
\(\texttt{historical-boundary:newmath-finite-parts-dynamical-zeta-shifts}\)
and
\(\texttt{historical-boundary:auditable-theory-to-paper-pipeline-root}\).
No prefix rule, wildcard rule, path existence rule, or other alias is available.
Table~\ref{tab:current-ledger-instantiation} and
\artifactpath{certificates/current_ledger_instantiation.json} are explanatory
normalized renderings of those bytes; neither is the evidence object for the
current-ledger theorem.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[RowParseOK for the current ledger]
\label{lem:current-ledger-row-parse-ok}
Let \(L_{\mathrm{cur}}\) be the seven entries obtained by decoding and
canonicalizing \artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}
under Definition~\ref{def:current-ledger-bytes-ok}, and assume
\[
\operatorname{CurrentLedgerBytesOK}(R).
\]
Let \(\operatorname{RowParseOK}(L_{\mathrm{cur}})\) mean that every canonicalized
non-path evidence string parsed from the JSON byte record is a whole-string
member of \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\), and every canonicalized local support path
parsed from the same byte record is a whole-string member of \(E_{\mathrm{path}}\),
for the grammars in
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  Then
\(\operatorname{RowParseOK}(L_{\mathrm{cur}})\) holds.  This lemma is the place
where the raw JSON strings in
\artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}, after only the
finite canonicalizations listed in Definition~\ref{def:current-ledger-bytes-ok},
are converted into typed tokens of
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}; the printed table supplies no
membership shortcut.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The premise supplies the byte-level list of strings by reading
\artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}.  The bracketed
manifest aliases and two explicit historical-boundary aliases are first replaced
by the finite canonical strings listed in
Definition~\ref{def:current-ledger-bytes-ok}; Table~\ref{tab:current-ledger-instantiation}
is only a readable projection of the resulting parsed tuple.  All cases are
finite whole-string checks.  The canonical atom rows are:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\texttt{a1}:&
\texttt{table:tab:source-interface-record},
\texttt{manifest:pinned\_source\_snapshot},
\texttt{lemma:lem:source-interface-extraction-correctness},\\
&\texttt{proposition:prop:reviewable-source-snapshot-interface},
\texttt{proposition:prop:source-interface-record-file-consistency};\\
\texttt{a2}:&
\texttt{table:tab:submission-interface-map},
\texttt{table:tab:primary-claim-inventory},
\texttt{proposition:prop:machine-readable-certificate-records};\\
\texttt{a3}:&
\texttt{manifest:case\_evidence/0},
\texttt{historical-boundary:newmath-finite-parts-dynamical-zeta-shifts},\\
&\texttt{historical-boundary:auditable-theory-to-paper-pipeline-root},
\texttt{table:tab:case-witnesses},
\texttt{proposition:prop:path-complete-case-witnesses};\\
\texttt{a4}:&

\texttt{table:tab:case-witnesses},
\texttt{proposition:prop:path-complete-case-witnesses};\\
\texttt{a5}:&
\texttt{manifest:case\_evidence/2},
\texttt{table:tab:source-interface-record},
\texttt{table:tab:case-witnesses},\\

\texttt{a6}:&

\texttt{table:tab:case-witnesses},
\texttt{proposition:prop:path-complete-case-witnesses};\\
\texttt{a7}:&
\texttt{table:tab:submitted-theorem-obligation-ledger},
\texttt{proposition:prop:manual-theorem-inventory-boundary},\\
&\texttt{theorem:thm:theorem-obligation-ledger-boundary},
\texttt{theorem:thm:deterministic-theorem-environment-extractor-boundary}.
\end{array}
\]
This finite display is the normalized token list computed from the JSON byte
record, not a pretty-printed path abbreviation: brackets, absolute paths, raw
prefixes such as \texttt{explicit-historical-boundary:}, and unlisted top-level
paths are not being silently accepted outside the two listed canonicalizations.
The table and formal-label strings have a displayed atom prefix followed by an
\(\alpha\)-string.  The manifest strings have prefix \texttt{manifest:} followed
by \(\beta\)-strings whose JSON-pointer paths are present in \(M\).  The two
historical strings have prefix \texttt{historical-boundary:} followed by
\(\alpha\)-strings.  Hence each listed atom is in \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\).

The canonical path rows parsed from the same JSON byte record are:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}

The exact top-level names are displayed alternatives of \(E_{\mathrm{path}}\).
Every remaining path starts with \texttt{review\_bundle/} followed by a nonempty

\begin{theorem}[Current-ledger byte instantiation]
\label{thm:current-ledger-bytes-to-acc}
\label{lem:current-ledger-ok}
Let \(R\) be a submitted root satisfying
\(\operatorname{CurrentLedgerBytesOK}(R)\).  Let
\((\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D)\) be the parsed tuple obtained from the byte files
named in Definition~\ref{def:current-ledger-bytes-ok} and the digest table, with
\(\Gamma=\Sigma.\texttt{gate\_dispatch}\).  Let
\[
C_e=(K_e,\ell_e,E_e,H_e,R_e,D_e,B_e)
\]
be the certificate tuple computed for entry \(e\) by parsing
\artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}: \(K_e\) is
\texttt{claim\_kind\_set}, \(\ell_e\) is \texttt{evidence\_level}, \(E_e\) is
the canonicalized typed evidence surface, \(H_e\) is \texttt{human\_promotion},
\(R_e\) is the finite \texttt{pass\_records} list, \(D_e\) is
\texttt{discovery\_interface}, and \(B_e\) is \texttt{boundary}.  Assume the
finite boundary predicates, where \(S\) is the submitted snapshot-list boundary
extracted from the case-row manifest \(C\),
\[
\operatorname{AllBoundPaths}(L,D),\quad
\operatorname{ManifestBoundaryOK}(M,D),
\]
\[
\operatorname{SnapshotBoundaryOK}(S,D),
\quad
\operatorname{TableBoundaryOK}(B,D,T).
\]
These four finite hypotheses mean that every evidence-path pointer has its
\(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\) digest row, every manifest pointer is in
\(\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(M)\), every snapshot pointer is in
\(\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(S)\), and every table/formal-label pointer is in
the deterministic \(\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}=T\) scan of submitted
\path{main.tex}; they are hypotheses of
this theorem, not consequences of merely printing pointer strings.  Then
\[
\operatorname{CurrentLedgerBytesOK}(R)
\Rightarrow
\bigwedge_{e\in\{\texttt{a1},\ldots,\texttt{a7}\}}
\Acc_{\Gamma}(e,C_e).
\]
Equivalently, the byte-supplied current ledger satisfies L1--L2 and every row
satisfies E1--E10 and R1--R4 of
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  The displayed
Table~\ref{tab:current-ledger-instantiation} may remain as an explanatory
rendering, but it is not the evidence object for this theorem.  In particular,
the theorem is specification-only with respect to named scripts and command
lines: executable force is deferred to \(\ScriptOKstageA\) and
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\), not obtained from path strings in the ledger.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Parse \artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json} from the
bytes named by \(\operatorname{CurrentLedgerBytesOK}(R)\), not from
Table~\ref{tab:current-ledger-instantiation}.  The parsed object has ledger name
\texttt{current\_package\_pass\_records}, schema pointer
\artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json}, top-level evidence level
\texttt{path-verified}, and exactly the seven entries
\(\texttt{a1},\ldots,\texttt{a7}\); hence L1 and L2 hold for the parsed current
ledger.  For each entry \(e\), compute
\[
K_e,\ell_e,E_e,H_e,R_e,D_e,B_e
\]
from the corresponding JSON fields as stated in the theorem.  Every computed
\(K_e\) is nonempty and is a subset of the seven-kind domain \(\Kind\), and every
computed level is
\(\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified}\in\mathsf{Lev}_{\mathrm{cert}}\).  The evidence
surface is then split into \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\) and \(E_{\mathrm{path}}\) by the
typed grammar after the finite canonicalizations of
Definition~\ref{def:current-ledger-bytes-ok}.  By
Lemma~\ref{lem:current-ledger-row-parse-ok}, every resulting evidence atom and
path is a whole-string member of the corresponding typed grammar.  No broader
identity is used: pretty wrapping, glyph choices, raw absolute-path syntax,
bracketed manifest indexes outside the four listed aliases, and nonlisted
top-level names would still fail
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  Thus E1--E6 hold for every
row once the byte-bound parse preconditions of the theorem hold.

It remains only to check the exact gate graph, discovery parity, pointers, and
level boundary for each parsed row.  Parse the dispatch table
\(\Gamma=\Sigma.\texttt{gate\_dispatch}\) from
\artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json}.  It assigns traceability
to \texttt{inventory} and \texttt{marker-existence}; route to
\texttt{promotion}, \texttt{overlap}, and \texttt{submission-state}; submission
to \texttt{submission-pack}; discovery to \texttt{pinned-discovery} and
\texttt{theorem-content-delta}; and artifact to \texttt{status} and
\texttt{limitation-ledger}.  Computing
\(G_e=\bigcup_{k\in K_e}\Gamma(k)\) and comparing it with the
\texttt{gate\_name} projection of \(R_e\) gives:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
e & K_e & \texttt{gate\_name}(R_e)\\ \hline
\texttt{a1} & \{\textsf{traceability}\} &
\{\texttt{inventory},\texttt{marker-existence}\}\\
\texttt{a2} & \{\textsf{route},\textsf{submission}\} &
\{\texttt{promotion},\texttt{overlap},\texttt{submission-state},\texttt{submission-pack}\}\\
\texttt{a3} & \{\textsf{route}\} &
\{\texttt{promotion},\texttt{overlap},\texttt{submission-state}\}\\
\texttt{a4} & \{\textsf{route},\textsf{submission}\} &
\{\texttt{promotion},\texttt{overlap},\texttt{submission-state},\texttt{submission-pack}\}\\
\texttt{a5} & \{\textsf{discovery}\} &
\{\texttt{pinned-discovery},\texttt{theorem-content-delta}\}\\
\texttt{a6} & \{\textsf{artifact}\} &
\{\texttt{status},\texttt{limitation-ledger}\}\\
\texttt{a7} & \{\textsf{traceability},\textsf{submission}\} &
\{\texttt{inventory},\texttt{marker-existence},\texttt{submission-pack}\}.
\end{array}
\]
The right column is an exact set equality with no duplicate rows for any listed
gate and no row whose gate lies outside \(G_e\).  Hence E7 holds row by row.

The \texttt{discovery\_interface} JSON field is non-null only for \(\texttt{a5}\),
the unique row with \(\textsf{discovery}\in K_e\), and null for the other six
rows, so E8 holds.  Each evidence pointer parsed from \(R_e\) is literally either
a member of that row's \(E_{\mathrm{atom}}\), a member of that row's
\(E_{\mathrm{path}}\), or a manifest/table/snapshot boundary token.  The four
explicit boundary hypotheses supply the load-bearing R4 facts for the latter
cases: \(\operatorname{AllBoundPaths}(L,D)\) supplies
\(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\) for path pointers,
\(\operatorname{ManifestBoundaryOK}(M,D)\) supplies membership in
\(\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(M)\),
\(\operatorname{SnapshotBoundaryOK}(S,D)\) supplies membership in
\(\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(S)\), and
\(\operatorname{TableBoundaryOK}(B,D,T)\) supplies membership in the
digest-bound submitted \(\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}\).  Therefore R1--R4 hold for every pass
row.  Finally, all seven rows have level \(\textsf{path\mbox{-}verified}\)
and their boundary object contains
\texttt{boundary.no\_command\_run\_assertion}; this is exactly
\(\mathsf{CommandBoundaryOK}.\mathrm{B}_2\), while E10 is inactive because no
row has level \(\textsf{command\mbox{-}run}\).  Thus every row satisfies
\(P_1,\ldots,P_6\), hence
\(\bigwedge_e\Acc_\Gamma(e,C_e)\).
Applying Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec} to the same parsed
tuple also gives the equivalent clause statement for C1--C5, L1--E10, and R1--R4.  This
byte-instantiation theorem is still not a script-conformance theorem: it does
not assert that \artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py}
accepts exactly these rows unless the separate \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\)
premise is supplied by Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-current-instance}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Certificate-record script conformance is a separate premise]
\label{lem:certificate-record-scriptok-separate}
Let
\[
\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}(P,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma)
\]
mean the following externally supplied branch-by-branch conformance theorem for
\artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py}: under the TCB and
byte-binding convention of Tables~\ref{tab:replay-tcb} and
\ref{tab:replay-byte-binding}, the script's accept/reject set on the supplied
byte records for certificate schema \(\Sigma\), ledger \(L\),
submission-interface map \(I\), primary-claim inventory \(Q\), review-bundle
manifest \(M\), case-row manifest \(C\), dispatch table \(\Gamma\), and digest
table \(D\) is exactly the displayed mathematical relation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  The theorem must prove
branch-by-branch equivalence for M1--M3, C1--C5, L1--E10, R1--R4, I1--I2, and P1--P5,
including the typed \(\PointerOK\) rule and the archive-instantiated
\(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\) rule, with \(C\) and \(\Gamma\) either separately
supplied byte records or uniquely derived from supplied byte records.  Then
digest-bound inputs plus
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) justify
citing that script run as an implementation of the certificate-record
specification.  Without \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\),
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec} remains only the
non-executable mathematical specification.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
This is the definition of \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\).  The premise states that
the script and the displayed finite relation have identical accepting and
rejecting sets on the supplied byte records after parsing, with equality checked
per listed branch rather than inferred from file digests.  Therefore a run of the
script may be cited only under that premise.  If the premise is absent, the
theorem supplies only the relation and its mathematical equivalence with
\(\Acc_{\Gamma}\) and the listed row locks; it supplies no fact about the Python
source.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 2b (current instance): certificate-script instance]
\label{thm:certificate-record-current-instance}
Define
\[
\operatorname{ArchiveOK}_{\mathrm{cert}}(R)
\]
to mean that \(R\) satisfies \(\operatorname{CurrentLedgerBytesOK}(R)\), the
digest-bound ordinary-file and no-symlink conditions for the five certificate
inputs in \(P_{\mathrm{cert}}\), the parsed digest-table bindings used by
\(\operatorname{BoundPath}_D\), and the finite boundary predicates
\(\operatorname{AllBoundPaths}(L,D)\),
\(\operatorname{ManifestBoundaryOK}(M,D)\),
\(\operatorname{SnapshotBoundaryOK}(S,D)\), and
\(\operatorname{TableBoundaryOK}(B,D,T)\), where \(B\) is the submitted
\path{main.tex} byte row.  If
\[
\operatorname{ArchiveOK}_{\mathrm{cert}}(R)\wedge
\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}(P,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma)
\]
holds for the submitted script path
\artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py}, then that script
accepts exactly the Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec} relation on
the current parsed tuple:
\[
\artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py}
\text{ accepts }
\Longleftrightarrow
\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(B,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D,S).
\]
Consequently the script accepts the current seven-row ledger exactly when
M1--M3, C1--C5, L1--E10, R1--R4, I1--I2, and P1--P5 pass, and in that case
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-ledger-bytes-to-acc} supplies
\(\bigwedge_{e\in\{\texttt{a1},\ldots,\texttt{a7}\}}\Acc_\Gamma(e,C_e)\).
Without the \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) premise this theorem has no executable
conclusion; only the schema relation and the byte-instantiated current-ledger
mathematics remain.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\(\operatorname{ArchiveOK}_{\mathrm{cert}}(R)\) supplies the ordinary-file,
SHA--256, JSON parsing, digest-table, and finite boundary facts needed to obtain
the parsed tuple and to apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-ledger-bytes-to-acc}.  The premise
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\) is, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:certificate-record-scriptok-separate}, the branch-by-branch
equality between the submitted Python script's accept/reject set and the
displayed finite relation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  Combining those two facts
gives the displayed iff.  The final current-ledger consequence is then exactly
Theorem~\ref{thm:current-ledger-bytes-to-acc}; no inspection of
Table~\ref{tab:current-ledger-instantiation} and no inference from script digests
is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Admissible pointers are exactly finite promoted atoms or bytes]
\label{lem:pointerok-exactness}
In Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}, for fixed parsed
\((E_{\mathrm{atom}},E_{\mathrm{path}},M,S)\), a pointer \(u\) is accepted by R4
if and only if \(u\) belongs to the finite union displayed in the definition of
\(\PointerOK(u,E,M,S)\).  In particular, none of the following facts is
sufficient for R4: \(u\) merely exists as a file under the root, \(u\) shares a
prefix with a manifest key, \(u\) matches a schema wildcard-looking pattern, or
\(u\) is a table, snapshot, or historical-boundary string not named by the
corresponding finite boundary set.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Expand the definition:
\begin{align*}
\PointerOK(u,E,M,S)\Longleftrightarrow{}&
u\in E_{\mathrm{atom}}\\
&{}\vee (u\in E_{\mathrm{path}}\wedge \operatorname{BoundPath}_D(u))\\
&{}\vee u\in\operatorname{ManifestBoundary}(M)\\
&{}\vee u\in\operatorname{TableBoundary}_{B,D}\\
&{}\vee u\in\operatorname{SnapshotBoundary}(S).
\end{align*}
Each disjunct is finite and closed.  The atom component is literal membership
after the closed atom grammar; the path component is literal evidence-list
membership plus the digest-bound relative-root predicate; the manifest and
snapshot components require named submitted rows and SHA--256 equality; and the
table component is the finite whole-string label set emitted by
\(\TableBoundaryScan(B,D)\) from digest-bound \path{main.tex} bytes.  No disjunct mentions bare
filesystem existence, prefix closure, or wildcard expansion.  Therefore R4
passes exactly on that finite promoted set and rejects every pointer outside it.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Conditional Horn replay kernel}
The Stage--A replay kernel is the finite Horn system in
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_horn_schema.json}.  The rules are reproduced
in Table~\ref{tab:horn-kernel-rules} so that the replay claim is a finite
byte-bound specification rather than an appeal to prose.  Executing that
specification requires the archive and locator conditions in
Section~\ref{sec:replay-artifact-boundary}.  The table applies the public
renaming \(\rho(\texttt{qinv})=\qlex\), where \(\qlex\) is the raw public
coordinate atom; the evidence predicate \(\rawBeginTokenWitness(r)\) remains a
separate scanner-row predicate.  The submitted JSON still contains the legacy
atom string \texttt{qinv}, and older support prose may still print
\(q_{\mathrm{lex}}\).
