\begin{lemma}[Digest-bound parser and manifest compilation]
\label{lem:stage-a-parser-compilation}
Under the TCB of Tables~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}--\ref{tab:replay-byte-binding} and
the digest table in
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}, the parser of
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_horn_schema.json} returns exactly the clauses
\(R0\)--\(R12\) displayed in Table~\ref{tab:horn-kernel-rules}, with \(R7\)
split into \(R7a\) and \(R7b\) for the external-support coordinate, and the
digest-bound manifest and finite checker projections determine exactly the
file-derived and checker-verified atom component
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{0.94\linewidth}{@{}L{0.12\linewidth}Y@{}}
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}=\) &
\texttt{pubAbs}, \texttt{suppMain}, \texttt{invJson}, \texttt{invMd},
\texttt{finalDigest}, \texttt{mainTex},
\texttt{stageAHornSchema}, \texttt{stageAManifest},
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate}, \texttt{stageAReplayReport},
\texttt{RecordGateOK}, \texttt{CertDAGOK}, \texttt{DigestOKstage\_a},
\texttt{srcInterface},
\texttt{caseArtifacts}, \texttt{venueReadiness},
\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}.
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
This compiled set deliberately omits \texttt{scanOK}.  Adjoining the separately
proved scanner premise \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) gives \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\), and
adjoining the meta-level conformance assumption \(\ScriptOKstageA\) after that
gives the conditional set \(A^{+}\) used for the second closure in
Theorem~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}; without those assumptions the
compiler output and the unconditional closure use only the displayed
file-derived and checker-verified atoms.
Here ``parser'' and ``compiler'' denote the two displayed mathematical
projections on digest-bound JSON objects.  This lemma does not assert that the
bytes of \artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py} implement those
projections; that implementation claim is the separate hypothesis
\(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}\) of
Lemma~\ref{lem:stage-a-scriptok-implies-replayok}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The digest hypotheses bind the two parser inputs to the archive raw bytes
supplied under the contract at the two named relative paths; no absolute path,
symlink, archive expansion, or \TeX{} interpretation is used.  Under the JSON
convention in
Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}, \(\mathsf{parseHorn}\) is the finite projection that
requires a top-level \texttt{rules} array and maps each object
\((\texttt{premises},\texttt{conclusion})\), in file order, to the Horn clause
\(\rho(\texttt{conclusion})\leftarrow\rho[\texttt{premises}]\), where
\(\rho(\texttt{qinv})=\qlex\) and \(\rho\) fixes every other displayed atom.
The witness predicate \(\rawBeginTokenWitness(r)\) is not a coordinate atom.
The renaming is only a public notation convention for the legacy artifact byte
string; it does not add a premise, change the replay file, or promote the raw
scanner to theorem-inventory coverage.  The digest-bound
schema bytes contain exactly the fourteen rule objects with identifiers
\texttt{R0\_single\_route\_surface} through
\texttt{R12\_route\_surface\_quotient}; their premise lists and conclusions are
the clauses printed as \(R0\)--\(R12\) in Table~\ref{tab:horn-kernel-rules}, and
the parser reads no other field as a rule.  Thus the parsed Horn system is the
displayed table.

Similarly, \(\mathsf{compileManifest}\) is the finite projection that requires
the manifest's \texttt{compiler\_rows} array and emits exactly the value of each
row's \texttt{atom} key, in file order, after the same digest-bound relative-path
checks.  The digest-bound manifest bytes and the associated finite record tests
determine exactly the file-derived and checker-verified atoms displayed
above, including \texttt{mainTex}, \texttt{RecordGateOK},
\texttt{CertDAGOK}, \texttt{DigestOKstage\_a}, and \texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}.
The scanner atom \texttt{scanOK} is not emitted by \texttt{compiler\_rows}; it
enters only if the separate \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\) premise is adjoined.  The meta-level atom
\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a} is not emitted by \texttt{compiler\_rows}; when it is
supplied as the separate conformance premise of
Lemma~\ref{lem:stage-a-scriptok-implies-replayok}, the resulting set is the
conditional \(A^{+}\) set used for the second closure in
Theorem~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}.  The
four upgrade
names \texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade},
\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade},
\texttt{stableLocator}, \texttt{archiveByteEquality},
\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}, and
\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade} do not occur as \texttt{atom} values
in \texttt{compiler\_rows}; appearances in negative-boundary prose or coordinate
metadata are not compiler rows.  Hence the compiled atom set is exactly the
displayed file-derived set \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\), not \(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\) or \(A^{+}\);
the raw \(\qlex\) closure is obtained only after adjoining \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\),
and the conditional \(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) closure is obtained only after also
adjoining the separate \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise.  The
proof has inspected
only the bound JSON records and the displayed finite projections, so it supplies
no branch-by-branch Python conformance lemma.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Byte-bound conditional Horn relation]
\label{cor:byte-bound-stage-a-horn-replay}
Use the same TCB and digest table as
Lemma~\ref{lem:stage-a-parser-compilation}.  The byte-bound mathematical
relation for \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_horn_schema.json} against
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json} has the three closures stated
in Theorem~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}: the unconditional
\(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\) closure is empty on public coordinates, the scanner-conditional
\(A^{\mathrm{scan}}\) closure derives only \(\qlex\), and the \(A^{+}\) closure
derives \(\qlex\) and \(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\).  This corollary depends on those
digest-bound JSON bytes and on the displayed finite parser/manifest projections,
not on extracting rules or atoms from the PDF and not on the behavior of
\artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py}.  Consequently the
unconditional byte-bound manifest branch has all six public coordinates false.
The
scanner-conditional finite-object branch has
\[
\qlex=\mathsf{true},\qquad
q_{\mathrm{rgs}}=q_{\mathrm{src}}=q_{\mathrm{art}}=q_{\mathrm{ext}}=
q_{\mathrm{ven}}=\mathsf{false},
\]
where \(\qlex\) has only the byte-indexed decoded-source meaning of
Lemma~\ref{thm:raw-begin-token-witness-soundness} and
Table~\ref{tab:scope-register};
and any archive-located artifact instantiation satisfying
Section~\ref{sec:replay-artifact-boundary} for the finite records and the
separate \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise has the conditional mathematical vector
\[
\qlex=q_{\mathrm{rgs}}=\mathsf{true},\qquad
q_{\mathrm{src}}=q_{\mathrm{art}}=q_{\mathrm{ext}}=q_{\mathrm{ven}}=
\mathsf{false}.
\]
Without that branch-by-branch premise, the scanner-conditional finite objects
supply only \(\qlex\) from the raw \(\ScanOK\) atom and the file-derived and
checker-verified atoms; they supply neither a
positive \(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) coordinate nor a replay assertion by the script.
Thus this corollary preserves the base, scanner-conditional, and archive-conditional
split of Theorem~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy} rather than promoting
either conditional branch into the unconditional one.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Lemma~\ref{lem:stage-a-parser-compilation} identifies the digest-bound parsed
schema with the displayed Horn table and the digest-bound compiled manifest with
the displayed atom set \(A_{\mathrm{rec}}\).  Theorem
\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy} first computes the closure of exactly
those finite objects after the explicit \(\ScanOK\) premise is adjoined, deriving
only \(\qlex\) among public coordinates, with the byte-indexed decoded-source meaning
supplied by Lemma~\ref{thm:raw-begin-token-witness-soundness}, and then computes the separate closure after
adjoining the \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise.  A replay instantiation satisfying the archive,
locator, digest, \(\ScanOK\), \(\RecordGateOK\), \(\CertDAGOK\), and
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) premises therefore has the conditional displayed
mathematical coordinate vector and no additional source,
artifact-semantic, archive, venue, or whole-implementation-conformance
consequence.  Without \(\ScriptOKstageA\), the corollary records only the
scanner-conditional file component, derives only \(\qlex\) in the raw-substring
sense, and cannot fire rule \(R3\).
\end{proof}

This corollary has no source-code conformance branch hidden in it.  It is a
statement about the digest-bound JSON bytes and the displayed finite
parser/manifest projections; the optional replay by
\artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py} begins only after the
separate \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise is attached.

The finite relation, not an unproved source-code branch, computes two least fixed
points.  Over the atoms compiled or verified from
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json} together with the explicit
\(\ScanOK\) premise, it derives only \(\qlex\) among public coordinates, in the
raw-substring sense.  Over those atoms plus the separate
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise and the certificate DAG objects in
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_horn_audit_certificate.json}, the specified
input atoms include \(\texttt{mainTex}\), \(\texttt{invJson}\),
\(\texttt{invMd}\), \(\texttt{finalDigest}\), \(\texttt{scanOK}\), the four
Stage--A file atoms, \(\texttt{RecordGateOK}\), \(\texttt{CertDAGOK}\),
\(\texttt{DigestOKstage\_a}\), the separate \(\texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a}\)
premise, and \(\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}\), so \(\qlex\) (legacy artifact atom
\(\texttt{qinv}\), public raw-coordinate atom) and
\(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) are derived in the conditional
mathematical closure.  Neither base contains
\(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{stableLocator}\), \(\texttt{archiveByteEquality}\),
\(\texttt{locatorOKstageA}\), \(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), or
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\), so
\(q_{\mathrm{src}},q_{\mathrm{art}},q_{\mathrm{ext}},q_{\mathrm{ven}}\) are not
in either least fixed point.

The source-level label and endpoint-boundary audit is a separate static scan,
not a PDF inference.  The command
\artifactcmd{python}{certificates/scan_tex_source_labels.py --write certificates/static_source_label_scan.json}
decodes the source bytes and scans the decoded lines of \artifactpath{main.tex},
\artifactpath{submission_abstract.tex}, and
\artifactpath{appendix_full_technical_ledger.tex}.  It does not evaluate TeX
conditionals, comments, includes, or macros; consequently any label in an
inactive branch would still be counted.  The current scan records \(21\), \(3\),
and \(354\) labels in those three sources, respectively, with zero duplicate
labels in each source and zero duplicate labels across the three-source package.
The same scan records zero occurrences of the stale endpoint-certificate tokens
listed in \artifactpath{certificates/static_source_label_scan.json}: the three
legacy rational constants, the legacy boundary equation using the larger
endpoint, and the two obsolete root-inequality phrases.  Thus the current source
package contains no printed central lower-bound certificate at the former shared
endpoint, no overlapping closed endpoint/central convention, and no hidden
inactive duplicate label.  The claim remains syntactic source hygiene only; it
is not proof checking.

\begin{table}
\caption{\(\rawBeginTokenSubset\) manifest for public coordinate
\(\qlex\) (legacy artifact atom \texttt{qinv}; row predicate
\(\rawBeginTokenWitness(r)\)).}
\label{tab:inventory-name-manifest}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{L{0.34\linewidth}Y}
\toprule
Scanner item & Meaning in the checker \\
\midrule
\artifactpath{definition} & Accepted only as the exact environment-name branch
inside \path{\textbackslash begin\{definition\}}. \\
\artifactpath{lemma} & Accepted only as the exact environment-name branch inside
\path{\textbackslash begin\{lemma\}}. \\
\artifactpath{proposition} & Accepted only as the exact environment-name branch
inside \path{\textbackslash begin\{proposition\}}. \\
\artifactpath{theorem} & Accepted only as the exact environment-name branch
inside \path{\textbackslash begin\{theorem\}}. \\
\artifactpath{corollary} & Accepted only as the exact environment-name branch
inside \path{\textbackslash begin\{corollary\}}. \\
\addlinespace
Begin pattern & After \texttt{utf-8-sig} decoding, each source line is searched
for at most its first match of
\path{\textbackslash begin\{(definition|lemma|proposition|theorem|corollary)\}}
with an optional immediate bracket title. \\
\addlinespace
End and label convention & For a begin record with name \(e\), the body extends
to the first later line containing \path{\textbackslash end\{e\}} or to end of
file; the label is the first \path{\textbackslash label\{...\}} in that slice. \\
\addlinespace
Comment and conditional convention & Comments, macro definitions, skipped
conditionals, and included files are not interpreted.  A matching decoded substring
inside a percent comment or inactive branch is counted; a token hidden behind a
macro or \path{\textbackslash input} is not discovered. \\
\addlinespace
Examples & Positive:
\path{\textbackslash begin\{lemma\}[T]\textbackslash label\{lem:x\}\textbackslash end\{lemma\}};
also counted if preceded by \path{\%}.  Negative:
\path{\textbackslash begin \{lemma\}},
\path{\textbackslash begin\{lemma*\}},
\path{\textbackslash begin\{remark\}}. \\
\addlinespace
Regression cases & Counted even though \TeX{} would not execute them: matching
substrings in percent comments and inactive conditional branches.  Not counted
even though a full \TeX{} expansion or parser might expose them:
macro-generated begins, \path{\textbackslash input}-included begins, nested begins
inside an already recorded body, starred environments, and second begin tokens
on the same source line. \\
\addlinespace
Excluded reading & No displayed-\TeX{}-content, theorem-inventory,
proof-checking, semantic-completeness, scope-adequacy, or
audit-record-completeness claim follows from this coordinate.
\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{table}

Required replay instantiation &

archive or unpacked support tree.  Acceptable forms include a DOI-bearing
supplement, a Software Heritage identifier for the archived source/support
object, a venue-controlled supplement identifier, or another public immutable
locator with an accompanying SHA--256 equality row for the archive or complete
file list.  This row is the non-coordinate replay identity premise

promotion row for \(q_{\mathrm{ext}}\), \(q_{\mathrm{ven}}\), or the Horn atom
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\).\\

paths and SHA--256 values as Tables~\ref{tab:replay-artifact-manifest}--%
\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger}, including the complete inner digest-table file
whose own digest is supplied only by the outer printed ledger.  A repository URL,
local path, generated PDF, or manifest row without this byte-equality check is
not a replay locator.\\

Table~\ref{tab:artifact-submission-checklist} is an archive-packaging condition,
not an additional mathematical theorem and not an assertion that such an archive
is already part of the PDF-only submission.  The complete archive condition is
that the replay artifact archive, when supplied, contains exactly the ordinary
files
named in Tables~\ref{tab:replay-artifact-manifest} and
\ref{tab:artifact-submission-checklist}, at those relative paths, together with
the review-bundle directory entries reached by
\artifactpath{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}; that it contains the
digest-bound replay-environment descriptor
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_replay_environment.json}; and that the same
archive is named by a stable locator row of
Table~\ref{tab:artifact-locator-status}.  The byte binding then has two levels:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the submitted JSON file
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json} is the inner support
manifest for the finite Stage--A relation; its rows bind every replay-support
file used by the relation, including the environment descriptor and optional
script bytes, except the digest-table file itself; and
\item Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger} is the outer printed ledger; its row for
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json} binds the bytes of that
inner manifest for review and archive comparison, but is not parsed as a
self-digest row by the finite relation.
\end{enumerate}
Thus the outermost digest anchor is explicit rather than informal, while the
inner table still avoids the impossible requirement of validating the bytes of
the file that contains the row.  If any listed file is omitted, if the
environment descriptor or stable locator row is absent, or if the digest-bound
bytes differ from the listed rows at the appropriate level, the archive-bound
finite-record claim is not instantiated; the surviving PDF-level claim is the
finite specification in Table~\ref{tab:scope-register}.  A script path or
command transcript adds no evidential force unless its own branch-by-branch
\(\ScriptOK\) premise is also supplied.

\begin{lemma}[Two-level byte binding for conditional replay records]
\label{lem:byte-binding-submitted-records}
Use the TCB in Tables~\ref{tab:replay-tcb} and
\ref{tab:replay-byte-binding}.  Suppose a replay artifact archive \(Z\) satisfies
\(\ArchiveFormatOK(Z,R,P_{\mathrm{req}})\) as defined in
Lemma~\ref{lem:archive-format-normalization}, where \(P_{\mathrm{req}}\) is the
required path set for this replay package.  Suppose further that \(Z\) contains the
relative paths in Tables~\ref{tab:replay-artifact-manifest} and
\ref{tab:artifact-submission-checklist} as ordinary non-symlink files, and
suppose their raw bytes have the SHA--256 values printed in the outer ledger,
Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger}.  In particular, the archive contains an
ordinary file at \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json} whose
SHA--256 is the outer printed digest-table row, and the parsed JSON rows inside
that file contain no path equal to
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}.  Also suppose the archive
is identified by the \(\LocatorOKstageA\) stable locator row satisfying
Table~\ref{tab:artifact-locator-status}, and that the archive contains the
digest-bound replay-environment descriptor
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_replay_environment.json} required by
Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}.  Write
\[
\DigestOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}(P,H,A,C,R)
\]
for exactly this finite digest predicate, available only under the non-PDF
archive hypotheses just listed: the archive-format lemma fixes POSIX
normalization, case-sensitive byte-name comparison, hard-link rejection, Unicode
normalization rejection, deterministic extraction order, byte-preserving unpacking,
distinct post-unpack identities, and immutable roots; the paths are admissible
ordinary relative paths; absolute paths, \texttt{..}, backslashes, symlinks,
directories, missing rows, self-rows for the digest table, and SHA--256 mismatches reject;
the digest-bound manifest, Horn schema, audit certificate, replay report, and
environment descriptor then parse under the JSON convention in
Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}.  Digest equality is only byte identity for these
finite inputs.  It is not a proof that any Python function implements the
mathematical replay predicate, and it is not available in the PDF-only article
unless the non-PDF archive, stable locator, replay-environment descriptor, and
matching byte-equality manifest hypotheses stated above are all supplied.

This lemma is only the byte-binding step missing from a PDF-only reading: when
the archive hypotheses are supplied, it links the archive JSON bytes, not just
displayed path names, to the finite atoms, rules, and record rows used by
Theorems~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}
and~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  Under
\(\DigestOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}(P,H,A,C,R)\), the submitted bytes parse to the
following finite JSON objects.
\begin{enumerate}[label={\(\mathrm{B}_{\arabic*}\):}]
\item The bytes of \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_manifest.json} parse to a
manifest whose \texttt{compiler\_rows}, together with the finite checker
predicates verified from the digest-bound rows, have exactly the displayed
file-derived and checker-verified input atoms
\[
\begin{array}{@{}lll@{}}
\texttt{pubAbs} & \texttt{suppMain} & \texttt{invJson}\\
\texttt{invMd} & \texttt{finalDigest} & \texttt{mainTex}\\
\texttt{scanOK} & \texttt{stageAHornSchema} & \texttt{stageAManifest}\\
\texttt{stageAHornCertificate} &
\texttt{stageAReplayReport}\\
\texttt{RecordGateOK} & \texttt{CertDAGOK} & \texttt{DigestOKstage\_a}\\
\texttt{srcInterface} & \texttt{caseArtifacts} & \texttt{venueReadiness}\\
\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade} & &
\end{array}
\]
Adjoining the separate meta-level \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise gives the
conditional \(A^{+}\) set used by the second closures in
Theorem~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:byte-bound-stage-a-horn-replay}; it is not emitted by the
manifest compiler rows.
\item The bytes of \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_horn_schema.json} parse to
the Horn clauses \(R0\)--\(R12\) printed in
Table~\ref{tab:horn-kernel-rules}, with coordinate set
\(\{\qlex,q_{\mathrm{rgs}},q_{\mathrm{src}},q_{\mathrm{art}},
q_{\mathrm{ext}},q_{\mathrm{ven}}\}\).
\item The bytes of
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_horn_audit_certificate.json} parse to a
derivation DAG for \(\qlex\), represented in the JSON as \texttt{qinv}, by
\(R1\), and to a conditional derivation DAG for \(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) by \(R3\)
over the adjoined \(A^{+}\) premise set, plus minimal missing-premise
obstructions
\(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\) for
\(q_{\mathrm{src}},q_{\mathrm{art}},q_{\mathrm{ext}},q_{\mathrm{ven}}\),
respectively.
\item The bytes of \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_replay_report.json} parse
to the displayed conditional accepted-coordinate vector over \(A^{+}\)
\[
\qlex=q_{\mathrm{rgs}}=\mathsf{true},\qquad
q_{\mathrm{src}}=q_{\mathrm{art}}=q_{\mathrm{ext}}=q_{\mathrm{ven}}=
\mathsf{false}.
\]
\item The bytes of
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_replay_environment.json} parse to the
descriptor whose pinned fields are the CPython \(3.10.11\) \texttt{python -VV}
transcript, the required standard-library module list, the
\texttt{utf-8-sig}/\texttt{json.loads} convention, the SHA--256 digest
convention, the relative-root/\texttt{resolve(strict=True)}/
\texttt{is\_relative\_to}/\texttt{lstat()} filesystem policy, and the submitted
paper-root working directory for the two replay commands.  Its Windows/NTFS
entry is provenance only.
\item The five \artifactpath{review_bundle/} JSON records in
Table~\ref{tab:artifact-submission-checklist} are byte-bound review-bundle inputs
for Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}; they are not used as
implicit evidence for source rebuild, artifact semantics, upload, venue, or
whole-pipeline implementation soundness, and they do not prove that any Python
branch implements the displayed certificate-record predicate.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The digest hypothesis is a finite byte-equality assertion: by
Table~\ref{tab:replay-byte-binding}, a row in
Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger} means SHA--256 of exactly the bytes returned
by \texttt{Path.read\_bytes()} at the named relative path.  Collision resistance
is not needed for the conditional statement: the lemma speaks about the
submitted bytes whose digests are printed, not about adversarially finding other
bytes with the same digest.

Under the JSON-decoding convention in Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}, the five
Stage--A JSON files parse as finite dictionaries.  Inspecting their displayed
fields gives \(\mathrm{B}_1\)--\(\mathrm{B}_5\): the manifest's
\texttt{compiler\_rows} fields, together with the separately checked finite
record predicates, are the file-derived and checker-verified atoms in
\(\mathrm{B}_1\); the schema's \texttt{rules} array is exactly the rule table
\(R0\)--\(R12\); the certificate's
\texttt{derivation\_dags}, \texttt{accepted\_coordinates}, and
\texttt{obstruction\_basis} fields are the DAG and obstruction objects in
\(\mathrm{B}_3\); and the report's \texttt{accepted\_coordinates} field is the
vector in \(\mathrm{B}_4\).  The descriptor's pinned fields are exactly those
listed in \(\mathrm{B}_5\), and its host-provenance field is not used as a
separate acceptance route.

Finally, the review-bundle records are listed and digest-bound but no Horn rule
or checker branch promotes them to the excluded coordinates; hence
\(\mathrm{B}_6\) follows from the rule table and the negative-coordinate
argument in Theorem~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}.  This last step is
only a record-surface exclusion: it is not a proof by inspection of
\artifactpath{review_bundle/verify_certificate_records.py},
\artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py}, or any other submitted
script.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Sufficient archive contract for conditional replay]
\label{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract-supplement}
Let \(Z=(R,T,E,L,B_{\mathrm{cert}})\) be a candidate replay archive, where \(R\)
is the unpacked paper root, \(T\) is the parsed JSON value obtained from the bytes
at \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}, \(E\) is the replay
environment descriptor, \(L=\LocatorRow\) is the locator row only in this
archive theorem, and
\[
B_{\mathrm{cert}}=(b_{\Sigma},b_L,b_I,b_Q,b_M,b_C,b_{\Gamma})
\]
is the tuple of supplied raw byte records for, respectively,
\(\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma\).  These are not read from the PDF: they are ordinary
archive files whose decoded JSON values must be the parsed objects used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.

Define two finite maps.  The outer printed map \(D_{\mathrm{outer}}\) has exactly
the relative paths printed in Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger} as its domain;
its value at \(p\) is the SHA--256 digest printed in that row.  The inner map
\(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) is the path-to-digest map encoded by the parsed rows of
\(T\).  Let \(dt=\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json}\), let
\(P_{\mathrm{outer}}=\operatorname{dom}(D_{\mathrm{outer}})\), and let
\(P_{\mathrm{inner}}=\operatorname{dom}(D_{\mathrm{inner}})\).  After the digest-table
bytes are parsed, the required archive path set is
\[
P_{\mathrm{req}}=P_{\mathrm{outer}}\cup P_{\mathrm{inner}}.
\]
Thus outer manifest files and every nested review-bundle or certificate-support
path reached through the parsed digest table are all subject to the same
archive-format normalization, ordinary-file, symlink/hard-link rejection,
no-alias, and root-immutability guard before any inner support file is accepted.
Every path quantified below has a specified digest in either
\(D_{\mathrm{outer}}\) or \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\), and no partial function
\(p\mapsto d_p\) is used.

Write \(\mathrm{OrdinaryFile}_{R}(p)\) and \(\mathrm{readBytes}_{R}(p)\) for the
ordinary non-symlink file predicate and raw byte read under the TCB of
Tables~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}--\ref{tab:replay-byte-binding}.  Write
\(\mathrm{DigestRows}(T)=D_{\mathrm{inner}}\), and write
\(\operatorname{parseJSON}_{\mathrm{utf8sig}}\) for the JSON-decoding convention of
Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}.  Write \(\mathcal R_{\mathrm{bytes}}\) for
\(\mathrm{RecordBytesOK}_{T}(B_{\mathrm{cert}},\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma)\).
Write \(\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for the finite support-row relation
``path \(p\) is an ordinary file under \(R\) and
\(\mathrm{sha256}(\mathrm{readBytes}_{R}(p))=D_{\mathrm{inner}}(p)\).''  Define
the camera-ready archive predicate by the wrapped display
\begin{align*}
\ArchiveOKA(Z)\Longleftrightarrow{}&
\ArchiveFormatOK(Z,R,P_{\mathrm{req}})\\
&{}\land \operatorname{dom}(D_{\mathrm{outer}})=P_{\mathrm{outer}}\\
&{}\land dt\in P_{\mathrm{outer}}\\
&{}\land
   \bigl(\forall p\in P_{\mathrm{req}}:
   \mathrm{OrdinaryFile}_{R}(p)\bigr)\\
&{}\land
   \bigl(\forall p\in P_{\mathrm{outer}}:
   \mathrm{sha256}(\mathrm{readBytes}_{R}(p))\\
&\hspace{8em}=D_{\mathrm{outer}}(p)\bigr)\\
&{}\land
   \bigl(T=\operatorname{parseJSON}_{\mathrm{utf8sig}}\\
&\hspace{8em}(\mathrm{readBytes}_{R}(dt))\bigr)\\
&{}\land
   \bigl(\mathrm{DigestRows}(T)=D_{\mathrm{inner}}
   \land dt\notin P_{\mathrm{inner}}\bigr)\\
&{}\land \mathrm{InnerDigestOK}(T,\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}})\\
&{}\land \mathcal R_{\mathrm{bytes}}\\
&{}\land \LocatorOKstageA(Z,T)\\
&{}\land \mathrm{EnvOK}(R,E).
\end{align*}
Here \(\mathrm{InnerDigestOK}(T,\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}})\) is the mathematical
predicate saying exact domain equality between the parsed inner rows and
\(P_{\mathrm{inner}}\), one SHA--256 field per path, no duplicate path row, no row
for \(dt\), equality between every row's digest string and
\(D_{\mathrm{inner}}(p)\), and the support-file fact
\(\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}}(p)\) for every parsed inner path \(p\).  Since
\(P_{\mathrm{inner}}\subseteq P_{\mathrm{req}}\), each such path has already passed
the same archive-format and no-alias guard as the outer paths.  It is not
defined by the replay script.  The optional executable layer may cite
\(\artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py}\) only under a separate
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) theorem proving that the script implements this
\(\mathrm{InnerDigestOK}\) predicate branch by branch.  The displayed
equality
\[
T=\operatorname{parseJSON}_{\mathrm{utf8sig}}(\mathrm{readBytes}_{R}(dt))
\]
is load-bearing: the tuple component \(T\) is not merely a no-self-row witness but
the parsed bytes at the digest-table path.  \(\mathrm{RecordBytesOK}_{T}\) means
that the seven supplied byte records decode as \texttt{utf-8-sig} JSON, parse to
exactly \((\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma)\), and have SHA--256 values equal to their
named rows in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\); if \(\Gamma\) is extracted from the schema
file rather than stored as a separate file, the archive must still supply the
canonical dispatch-table byte slice and its SHA--256 row.  \(\LocatorOKstageA(Z,T)\)
means that \(L\) is a stable public or venue-controlled locator naming the exact
archive bytes and satisfying the Table~\ref{tab:artifact-locator-status}
byte-equality condition; and \(\mathrm{EnvOK}(R,E)\) is the root-dependent
environment predicate of Table~\ref{tab:replay-tcb}, including the requirement
that the descriptor's working-directory/root field resolves to the archive root
\(R\).  The initial \(\ArchiveFormatOK\) conjunct is the finite
format-level guard of Lemma~\ref{lem:archive-format-normalization}; it fixes
POSIX path names, case-sensitive comparison, hard-link rejection, Unicode
normalization rejection, deterministic extraction, byte-preserving unpacking,
post-unpack identity separation, and root immutability before any digest row is
read.  Equivalently,
\(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\) expands to the following finite submission facts:
\begin{enumerate}[label={\(\mathrm{A}_{\arabic*}\):}]
\item after the digest-table bytes are parsed, the archive satisfies
\(\ArchiveFormatOK(Z,R,P_{\mathrm{req}})\) for
\(P_{\mathrm{req}}=P_{\mathrm{outer}}\cup P_{\mathrm{inner}}\): required
names are exact POSIX byte strings, extraction is byte-preserving and
deterministic, symlink and hard-link entries are rejected, distinct required
paths cannot alias the same file object, and the unpacked root is immutable
during replay;
\item every relative path in \(P_{\mathrm{outer}}=\operatorname{dom}(D_{\mathrm{outer}})\)
is supplied outside the PDF as an ordinary non-symlink file under the submitted
paper root, and every such file has the SHA--256 value printed for that path in
Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger};
\item the supplied archive contains an ordinary file at \(dt\), and the tuple
component \(T\) is exactly the \texttt{utf-8-sig} JSON parse of
\(\operatorname{readBytes}_{R}(dt)\);
\item the parsed inner digest table has exact domain \(P_{\mathrm{inner}}\), has
no row for \(dt\), has no duplicate path row, and has one digest string per path
equal to \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}(p)\);
\item the mathematical predicate
\(\mathrm{InnerDigestOK}(T,\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}})\) holds: for every
\(p\in P_{\mathrm{inner}}\), the row syntax from \(\mathrm{A}_4\) is accompanied
by the ordinary-file and raw-byte equality fact
\(\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}}(p)\).  Thus review-bundle and certificate records
reached through the inner digest table are format-guarded and byte-bound without
assuming that Table~\ref{tab:printed-sha-ledger} lists those inner support rows
again;
\item the supplied byte records \(b_{\Sigma},b_L,b_I,b_Q,b_M,b_C,b_{\Gamma}\)
decode as \texttt{utf-8-sig} JSON and parse, after the stated root
normalization, to exactly the tuple
\((\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma)\) used by
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}, with their byte digests equal
to the named rows of \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\);
\item \artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_replay_environment.json} is supplied as
a digest-bound machine-readable descriptor pinning the interpreter build,
standard-library modules, JSON-decoding convention, digest convention,
operating-system interface, filesystem assumptions, path-normalization policy,
and working directory for the replay commands, and that descriptor resolves its
working-directory/root field to \(R\); and
\item a DOI-bearing supplement, Software Heritage identifier,
venue-controlled supplement identifier, or equivalent immutable byte locator
names the exact archive or unpacked support tree and is accompanied by the
byte-equality condition in Table~\ref{tab:artifact-locator-status}; this is the
\(\LocatorOKstageA\) replay-identity premise, not the external-equivalence row
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and it is not a
\(q_{\mathrm{ext}}\) upgrade row.
\end{enumerate}
\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.12\linewidth}Y Y@{}}
\toprule
Symbol & Archive byte role & Required SHA--256 binding \\ \midrule
\(\Sigma\) & \artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json} &
Row in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for \artifactpath{review_bundle/certificate_schema.json}.\\
\(L\) & \artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json} &
Row in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for \artifactpath{review_bundle/current_package_pass_records.json}.\\
\(I\) & \artifactpath{review_bundle/submission_interface_map.json} &
Row in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for \artifactpath{review_bundle/submission_interface_map.json}.\\
\(Q\) & \artifactpath{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json} &
Row in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for \artifactpath{review_bundle/primary_claim_inventory.json}.\\
\(M\) & \artifactpath{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json} &
Row in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for \artifactpath{review_bundle/REVIEW_BUNDLE_MANIFEST.json}.\\
\(C\) & \artifactpath{certificates/case_rows_expected.json} &
Row in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for the case-row byte record.\\
\(\Gamma\) & Canonical dispatch-table bytes derived from \(\Sigma\). &
Named row in \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\) for the canonical dispatch-table extraction.\\
\bottomrule

\end{center}
\begin{center}
\scriptsize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{@{}L{0.15\linewidth}Y Y@{}}
\toprule
Clause & Supplies & Consumed by replay argument \\ \midrule
\(\mathrm{A}_1\) & Archive format, POSIX-name normalization, ordinary-entry
type, symlink/hard-link rejection, no-alias checks, and root immutability for
\(P_{\mathrm{outer}}\cup P_{\mathrm{inner}}\). & Makes every outer and inner path a
unique ordinary byte source before digest comparison.\\
\(\mathrm{A}_2\) & Outer byte equality against \(D_{\mathrm{outer}}\), including
the digest-table file \(dt\). & Binds the printed ledger and the digest-table
bytes.\\
\(\mathrm{A}_3\)--\(\mathrm{A}_5\) & Parsed tuple equality
\(T=\operatorname{parseJSON}_{\mathrm{utf8sig}}(\operatorname{readBytes}_{R}(dt))\),
inner row domain \(P_{\mathrm{inner}}\), and inner SHA--256 equality against
\(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\). & Binds review-bundle and certificate-support records
without reading any unguarded inner path.\\
\(\mathrm{A}_6\) & Parsed certificate-record tuple
\((\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma)\) and its byte records. & Supplies the pure finite
certificate checker inputs.\\
\(\mathrm{A}_7\) & Root-dependent environment descriptor \(\mathrm{EnvOK}(R,E)\). &
Pins JSON, digest, path, filesystem, interpreter, and working-directory
semantics.\\
\(\mathrm{A}_8\) & Stable locator and complete byte-equality identity row. &
Supplies \(\LocatorOKstageA\), not \(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\).\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
The displayed definition is an exact archive-predicate expansion, not a
one-way checklist.  More explicitly, after \(T\) is fixed as the parsed
\texttt{utf-8-sig} JSON value of the digest-table bytes at \(dt\), the following
biconditional holds by definition:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\ArchiveOKA(Z)\Longleftrightarrow{}&
\Bigl[
  \forall p\in P_{\mathrm{req}}:
  \mathrm{OrdinaryFile}_{R}(p)\land
  \mathrm{sha256}(\mathrm{readBytes}_{R}(p))=d_p
\Bigr]\\
&{}\land \ArchiveFormatOK(Z,R,P_{\mathrm{req}})\\
&{}\land \mathrm{InnerDigestOK}(T,\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}})\\
&{}\land \mathrm{RecordBytesOK}_{T}(B_{\mathrm{cert}},
      \Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma)\\
&{}\land \mathrm{EnvOK}(R,E)\land \LocatorOKstageA(Z,T),
\end{aligned}
\]
where \(d_p\) means all applicable declared digest rows for \(p\): outer paths
must match \(D_{\mathrm{outer}}(p)\), inner paths must match
\(D_{\mathrm{inner}}(p)\), and a path present in both maps is accepted only if
the two declared digests agree with the same bytes.  For the digest-table path
\(dt\) itself, the digest is the outer printed digest-table anchor; the no-self
condition \(dt\notin P_{\mathrm{inner}}\) prevents the inner table from
certifying its own bytes.  The \(\mathrm{InnerDigestOK}\) conjunct contains the
exact parsed inner-domain equality, no duplicate row, no-self row, and inner
digest equality conditions.  The script
\artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py} is not part of this
biconditional except as an ordinary digest-bound path when it is listed in
\(P_{\mathrm{req}}\); executable force requires the separate
\(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise.
The final row is a finite canonical byte slice of the dispatch table, not a
license to infer \(\Gamma\) from prose: the archive must either supply that
slice as a named byte record or give an equivalent digest-bound extraction row.
The present PDF-only article does not assert \(\mathrm{A}_1\)--\(\mathrm{A}_8\);
it defines them as the finite contract for a conditional archive-instantiated replay.
This theorem also assumes the closed-world frame \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\);
\(\ArchiveOKA(Z)\) by itself is only a byte and locator predicate and does not
rule out pre-existing coordinate facts, hidden \(Q\)-concluding rules, or
stronger promotion premises.
For \(q_i\in Q\), the replay vector is the Horn-closure projection
\[
\ReplayVector_i(Z)\Longleftrightarrow
q_i\in\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\setminus Q).
\]
This projection is used together with the frame predicate \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\) of
Definition~\ref{def:archive-contract-predicate}: pre-closure coordinate atoms are
absent, every \(Q\)-concluding rule is one of \(R1,R3,R5,R6,R7b,R8\), and the
non-allowed promotion premises are absent unless a named upgrade derivation
supplies them.  Thus no coordinate can be true merely because it was already
present as an input fact or because an unprinted \(Q\)-rule was silently added.
The archive-instantiation predicate
\[
\ArchiveInstantiatesAplus(Z)\Longleftrightarrow A^{+}\subseteq\ReplayBase(Z)
\]
is obtained only after the archive clauses are combined with the explicit
\(\ScanOK\), \(\RecordGateOK\), \(\CertDAGOK\), \(\DigestOKstageA\),
\(\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}\), the four Stage--A file atoms, accepted-run, and
script-conformance hypotheses.  The stronger upgrade atoms
\(\texttt{freshFormalSourceUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{dynamicArtifactSemanticUpgrade}\),
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), and
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\) are absent only under this frame
condition unless a separate upgrade record explicitly derives them.  If those
hypotheses are supplied, then
the camera-ready replay theorem is
\begin{align*}
&\ArchiveOKA(Z)\land\FrameOKZ(Z)\land\ScriptOKstageA\land\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{cert}}\\
&\qquad{}\land\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\land\RecordGateOK\land\CertDAGOK\\
&\qquad{}\land\DigestOKstageA\land\texttt{qrgsReplayUpgrade}\land
\operatorname{AcceptsCertSpec}(B,\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma,D,S)\\
&\qquad{}\land\operatorname{CertRunAccepts}(Z)
\land\operatorname{StageARunAccepts}(Z)\\
&\qquad\Longrightarrow
\ReplayVector(Z)=
(\mathsf{true},\mathsf{true},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false}).
\end{align*}
Without \(\ScanOK(B,I,J,D)\), the first coordinate is not derivable; without
\(\ScriptOKstageA\), the second coordinate is not derivable.
The implication is exactly the replay precondition package used by
Lemma~\ref{lem:byte-binding-submitted-records},
Corollary~\ref{cor:byte-bound-stage-a-horn-replay}, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}.  Thus an
archive-instantiated replay would have positive coordinates \(\qlex\) and
\(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) and negative coordinates
\(q_{\mathrm{src}},q_{\mathrm{art}},q_{\mathrm{ext}},q_{\mathrm{ven}}\).  The
stable locator in \(\mathrm{A}_8\) is exactly \(\texttt{locatorOKstageA}\), the
identity premise for the supplied archive.  It is not the separate
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\) row and therefore cannot by itself fire
\(\texttt{qext}\).  A positive \(q_{\mathrm{ext}}\) coordinate would require
both \(R7a\)'s locator premise and \(R7b\)'s external-archive equivalence atom.
Conversely, the PDF-only article, or a local source tree without the supplied
record bytes, locator, and byte-equality facts
\(\mathrm{A}_1\)--\(\mathrm{A}_8\), does not instantiate a
executable artifact claim.  In that case the article retains only the printed
finite specification, the mathematical Horn calculation, and the conditional
statement of what an archive satisfying
\(\ArchiveOKA\) would replay.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The forward implication is by expansion of the eight clauses.  Clause
\(\mathrm{A}_1\) supplies the archive-format guard: POSIX byte-name
normalization, case-sensitive comparison, hard-link and symlink-entry rejection,
Unicode-normalization rejection, deterministic byte-preserving extraction,
post-unpack no-alias identity checks, and root immutability.  Clause
\(\mathrm{A}_2\) supplies the outer ordinary-file, relative-root,
normalization, containment, final-entry symlink rejection, and printed SHA--256
hypotheses.  Clause \(\mathrm{A}_3\) supplies the missing definitional equality
\(T=\operatorname{parseJSON}_{\mathrm{utf8sig}}(\operatorname{readBytes}_{R}(dt))\)
between the tuple component and the bytes at the digest-table path.  Clause
\(\mathrm{A}_4\) supplies the parsed inner-row domain and no-self-row facts
needed to form \(P_{\mathrm{inner}}\) and \(D_{\mathrm{inner}}\).  Clause
\(\mathrm{A}_5\) is exactly
\(\mathrm{InnerDigestOK}(T,\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}})\): exact inner-domain
equality, no duplicate path row, no row for \(dt\), digest equality for every
path named by the parsed inner table, and the ordinary-file/raw-byte equality
fact \(\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}}(p)\) for each such path.  This is a
mathematical archive predicate; no step in this implication invokes
\artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py} or treats a script run as
evidence for the predicate.  Clause \(\mathrm{A}_6\) supplies raw byte records for
\(\Sigma,L,I,Q,M,C,\Gamma\), their JSON parsing into the pure schema tuple, and
their inner-table digest bindings.  Clause \(\mathrm{A}_7\) supplies the pinned
root-dependent execution descriptor assumed by the replay commands, including
the requirement that the descriptor root resolves to \(R\).  The observed
\texttt{python -VV}/Windows/NTFS transcript in that descriptor is provenance, not
an independent host-specific theorem premise.
Clause \(\mathrm{A}_8\) supplies \(\texttt{locatorOKstageA}\): the stable locator
and complete byte-equality condition that turns a local support tree into an
operative submitted artifact.  This is a replay-identity fact only; it is not
the human-promoted external-archive equivalence atom used by \(R7b\) for
\(q_{\mathrm{ext}}\).
Therefore the digest-bound JSON objects, replay scripts, expected case rows,
review-bundle inputs, and environment assumptions are exactly the preconditions
cited by the byte-binding lemma and the conditional Horn replay corollary.  The
coordinate vector is computed from
\(\operatorname{Cl}_{H_{\mathrm{Horn}}}(\pi_{\mathrm{Horn}}(\Facts(Z))\setminus Q)\)
under \(\FrameOKZ(Z)\), not by substituting the smaller set \(A^{+}\) for the
whole archive projection.  The displayed rules \(R1\) and \(R3\) fire because
their premises are present; the four stronger coordinates remain false because
F1 removes initial coordinate facts, F2 lists all \(Q\)-concluding rules, and F3
keeps their named promotion atoms absent.  Clause \(\mathrm{A}_1\) supplies the
archive-format guard; clause \(\mathrm{A}_8\) makes
the located archive identical to the submitted replay surface; it does not add
\(\texttt{externalArchiveEquivalence}\), assert external archive equivalence as
a promoted coordinate, or add
\(\texttt{uploadTimeVenueAcceptanceUpgrade}\) to the Horn atom set.

This theorem is intentionally one-way.  The PDF contains printed path names,
digest strings, and mathematical definitions, but it does not contain the raw
ordinary files at those paths, an executable filesystem root, an environment
descriptor, or an immutable locator naming an archive.  A local source tree has
ordinary files but still lacks the public or venue-controlled locator unless
clause \(\mathrm{A}_8\) is separately supplied, and local file existence without
the archive-format guard of \(\mathrm{A}_1\), the digest-table equality of
\(\mathrm{A}_3\), the outer and inner SHA--256 equalities of
\(\mathrm{A}_2\)--\(\mathrm{A}_5\), and the supplied record bytes of
\(\mathrm{A}_6\) is not a byte-equality manifest.  Therefore the paper proves a
sufficient archive contract for the conditional replay vector, not an iff
characterization of all possible replay implementations.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conditional Stage--A replay assumption package]
\label{lem:stage-a-scriptok-implies-replayok}
For the PDF-only article this environment records a conditional assumption, not
an implementation theorem.  Write
\[
\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}(P,H,A,C,R)
\]
for the following externally discharged branch-by-branch conformance theorem
about the submitted path
\artifactpath{certificates/replay_stage_a_audit.py}: when run under the TCB of
Tables~\ref{tab:replay-tcb} and \ref{tab:replay-byte-binding}, the script's
accept/reject set is exactly the finite Stage--A relation whose branches are:
\begin{enumerate}[label={\(\mathrm{S}_{\arabic*}\):}]
\item it implements \(\mathrm{InnerDigestOK}(T,\mathcal R_{\mathrm{inner}})\) for
the no-self digest table, validating every row and rejecting absolute paths,
\texttt{..}, backslashes, symlinks, directories, missing rows, digest
mismatches, and any attempted row for
\artifactpath{certificates/stage_a_digest_table.json};
\item it emits manifest atoms exactly from the digest-checked
\texttt{compiler\_rows}, verifies the separate finite record predicates, and
requires the explicit \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a} trusted-assumption atom as a
meta-level input rather than deriving or emitting it from digest rows;
\item it verifies \(\ScanOK\), \(\RecordGateOK\), \(\CertDAGOK\), and
\(\DigestOKstageA\) by the finite label, rule-row, DAG, and digest-table tests
described above;
\item it forward-chains the finite Horn rule set monotonically over the finite
atom/rule universe until the least fixed point is reached;
\item it validates positive certificate DAGs and negative missing-premise
obstructions against that closure; and
\item it accepts exactly when the recomputed six-coordinate vector equals the
vectors declared in the certificate and replay report.
\end{enumerate}
The paper does not prove any of \(\mathrm{S}_1\)--\(\mathrm{S}_6\) from the
printed PDF, digest strings, or path names; those clauses require either the
submitted source bytes plus a separate conformance proof, or an archive/review
process that treats \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}\) as a trusted hypothesis.
In particular, the explicit \texttt{ScriptOKstage\_a} atom is only a meta-level
input to the displayed Horn closure; it is not a manifest compiler-row atom and
is not established by the digest table, by successful parsing, by positive
certificate DAGs, or by negative missing-premise obstructions.
Under that hypothesis the Stage--A replay contract is the conditional statement
\begin{align*}
&\DigestOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}(P,H,A,C,R)\wedge
\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}(P,H,A,C,R)\\
&\hspace{2em}\Longrightarrow
\ReplayOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}(P,H,A,C,R).
\end{align*}
Thus \(\ScriptOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}\) is not a consequence of
\(\DigestOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}\), of the digest rows, or of
Lemmas~\ref{lem:stage-a-parser-compilation}
and~\ref{lem:byte-binding-submitted-records}; it is precisely the unproved
script-conformance premise that must be attached or assumed before
\(\ReplayOK_{\mathrm{stage\_a}}\) can be invoked.  Without it, all uses of the
Stage--A package in this article are uses of the displayed mathematical relation
only.  This lemma also does not add a public result beyond
Theorem~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy}: it is only the conditional
support premise for an optional executable reading of the two-coordinate replay
surface.
\end{lemma}

\section{Related work by interface}
\label{sec:related-work}
This paper is closest to proof-carrying and certificate-checking traditions:
proof-carrying code and foundational proof certificates separate a compact
certificate object from the trusted checker that validates it
\cite{Necula1997PCC,Appel2001FoundationalPCC,AppelMcAllester2001FPCCRecursiveTypes,ChihaniMillerRenaud2013FPC}.
Our contribution uses that separation at the publication boundary rather than
inside a programming-language or logical-kernel safety theorem.  The submitted
certificate proves only a finite Horn-coordinate closure, and only for the
coordinates whose premises are present.  It is not a proof of source-theory
truth, proof-assistant reconstruction, artifact semantics, or general
implementation soundness.

The work is also adjacent to formal-mathematics systems and LLM-assisted
formalization.  Lean 4, Lean, Isabelle/AFP, LeanDojo, Lean Copilot,
Draft--Sketch--Prove, DeepSeek-Prover, and Llemma provide theorem-prover
substrates, libraries, corpora, and proposal mechanisms for formalization
\cite{MouraUllrich2021Lean4,deMouraKADR2015Lean,BlanchetteHMN2015AFP,Wenzel2019IsabelleAFP,YangEtAl2023LeanDojo,SongYangAnandkumar2024LeanCopilot,JiangEtAl2023DraftSketchProve,XinEtAl2024DeepSeekProver,AzerbayevEtAl2023Llemma}.
Here such tools motivate the advisory-agent gate: generated suggestions remain
non-load-bearing until deterministic finite rows and a human-promotion boundary
place them in an accepted coordinate.

Artifact and reproducibility systems give stronger evidence units than the one
claimed here.  ReproZip, CODECHECK, RO-Crate, BagIt/RFC 8493, PROV-O, in-toto,
SLSA, DataLad, Nix/NixOS, Guix, CWL, Snakemake, Sumatra, Software Heritage
identifiers, the Software Citation Principles, and FAIR4RS can support archived
research objects, execution certificates, provenance graphs, supply-chain
attestations, rebuild environments, workflow descriptions, or software-identity
claims
\cite{ChirigatiRampinShashaFreire2016ReproZip,EglenEtAl2021CODECHECK,SoilandReyesEtAl2022ROCrate,KunzeEtAl2018BagItRFC8493,LeboEtAl2013PROVO,TorresAriasEtAl2019InToto,OpenSSF2023SLSA10,HalchenkoEtAl2021DataLad,DolstraDeJongeVisser2004Nix,DolstraLohPierron2010NixOS,ValletMichonneauTournier2022Guix,CWLCommunity2016,MoelderEtAl2021Snakemake,Davison2012SumatraContext,DavisonEtAl2014Sumatra,DiCosmoGruenpeterZacchiroli2020SWHID,SmithEtAl2016SoftwareCitationPrinciples,BarkerEtAl2022FAIR4RS}.
The archive contract in Theorem~\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}
is deliberately narrower: it asks only for ordinary-file byte equality, the
no-self digest table, the environment descriptor, locator identity, and the
explicit script-conformance premises needed by the two positive replay
coordinates.

For venue positioning we screened the official CICM 2021--2025 Springer
proceedings records and citable CICM workshop records using the terms
\auditcode{formalization}, \auditcode{certificate}, \auditcode{artifact},
\auditcode{LLM}, \auditcode{library}, \auditcode{database}, and
\auditcode{algorithm}
\cite{CICM2021Proceedings,CICM2021Workshops,CICM2022Proceedings,CICM2023Proceedings,CICM2024Proceedings,CICM2025Proceedings}.
Within that stated screen, the closest items were library-maintenance,
proof-assistant database, document-infrastructure, LLM-assistance, and verified
algorithm papers rather than publication-coordinate replay papers
\cite{BaanenEtAl2026GrowingMathlib,YamamichiEtAl2021MizarPlatform,KohlhaseMuellerSchaefer2021STeX,BauerEtAl2024LeanHoG,Karatarakis2024Deligne,HorowitzEtAl2026Herald,BrinichJohnson2024QFT,BartolettiBonzioFerrara2026Semigroups,BentkampAvigad2021VerifiedOptimization}.
The claim is only that no closer item was found by that stated screen.  It is
not an assertion about unqueried databases, later metadata changes, uncited
pages, or differently phrased papers.  For CICM 2025, rows are named by event
year; the bibliography distinguishes the Springer publication and copyright
metadata.

Classical Richardson undecidability, exact-real and symbolic-algebra systems,
Zeckendorf numeration, and finite-automata/rational-series theory provide
background for optional supplement branches
\cite{Richardson1968,Caviness1970,Wang1974,Mueller2001IRRAM,Lambov2007RealLib,Johansson2017Arb,Johansson2021Calcium,Hart2010FLINT,ZimmermannEtAl2018SageMath,MeurerEtAl2017SymPy,Zeckendorf1972,Frougny1999,AhlbachUsatineFrougnyPippenger2013,Idziaszek2020,AlloucheShallit2003,KauersPaule2011}.
Those branches are not main-paper evidence and do not add public coordinates.

\section{Conclusion}
The useful abstraction is not an assertion about who or what produced a theorem
candidate.  It is the finite publication-coordinate route that assigns each
artifact to the strongest claim it is entitled to support.  For the current
PDF-level article the unconditional claim is the printed six-coordinate finite
specification and Horn calculation
\[
(\qlex,q_{\mathrm{rgs}},q_{\mathrm{src}},q_{\mathrm{art}},q_{\mathrm{ext}},
q_{\mathrm{ven}})=(\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})
\]
for the base displayed relation.  Adjoining the explicit scanner-success
premise gives the intermediate finite relation with \(\qlex\) positive only as a
decoded begin-token substring witness.
Adjoining the external branch-by-branch \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise after that
gives the conditional mathematical relation with \(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) positive.
The executable reading is outside the submitted package.  The theorem states
the additional premises that would be required: archive byte equality,
environment and stable-locator clauses, \(\ScanOK\), \(\RecordGateOK\),
\(\CertDAGOK\), \(\DigestOKstageA\), and \(\ScriptOKstageA\).  No
source-rebuild, artifact-semantic, external-archive, venue-status,
executable-script, or whole-program implementation-soundness coordinate is
positive in this paper.

\paragraph{Supplement boundary.}
The source package retains
\artifactpath{appendix_full_technical_ledger.tex} and the material below this
file's first \verb|\end{document}| as non-load-bearing supplement text.  Those
sources collect Zeckendorf transport, Richardson variants, factorial-spike
stress tests, long ledger closures, and generated audit wrappers.  They are not
compiled into the main article and do not add public coordinates beyond
Theorems~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy},
\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}, and
\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}.

\subsection{Richardson-normalizer obstruction}
\label{subsec:richardson-normalizer-obstruction}
The paper uses Richardson undecidability only in the following formal reduction
shape.  This subsection is deliberately separated from the replay theorem above:
it gives a boundary for possible semantic normalizer claims, not another
accepted coordinate of the submitted package.

Fix the Richardson source grammar \(\RE\) in the following imported-theorem
sense.  Its elementary expressions are the single-real-variable expressions
generated from \(x\), rational constants, the constants \(\pi\) and \(\log 2\),
the operations \(+,-,\times\), composition, and the primitives
\(\sin\), \(\exp\), and \(|\cdot|\).  This is the identity-problem fragment
obtained from Richardson's 1968 theorem under its condition that the expression
class contains an absolute-value representative; the later citations are
background, not a different source theorem.  Let
\(\Eq_{\RE}(r,0)\) mean equality to zero on that theorem's own specified domain
convention.  Thus \(\Eq_{\RE}\) is not introduced here as equality on an
unstated or possibly empty domain; it is the identity predicate supplied by the
source theorem itself.

\begin{theorem}[Richardson identity undecidability input]
\label{thm:richardson-source-input}
For the effective elementary-expression grammar \(\RE\) just described, no
algorithm decides \(\Eq_{\RE}(r,0)\) for all \(r\in\RE\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is the cited Richardson undecidability theorem for elementary expression
identity~\cite{Richardson1968}, used with the theorem's own grammar and domain
convention.  The later simplification literature records the same obstruction as
the fundamental barrier to canonical elementary simplification
\cite{Caviness1970,Wang1974}.  The present paper imports only this theorem
statement; it does not derive it from the package's finite artifact rows, from
empty-domain equality, or from a target-language normal-form convention.
\end{proof}

For a binary source comparison write
\[
  \Eq_{\RE}(r,s)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \Eq_{\RE}(r-s,0).
\]
This derived predicate inherits the same domain convention from
Theorem~\ref{thm:richardson-source-input}.  Let \(\TE\) be any proposed target
fragment whose objects are finite strings in a decidable language, and let
\(\Sem_{\TE}\) be its stated semantics.  Equality
\(\Sem_{\TE}(t)=\Sem_{\TE}(u)\) denotes equality of the semantic objects in that
stated target semantics, including whatever domain information the target
semantics assigns.  No separate filesystem, artifact-row, or empty-domain
convention is hidden in this equality.

A translation is a total computable map \(\tau:\RE\to\TE\).  A finite target
normalizer is a total computable map \(\Norm:\TE\to\TE\) whose outputs have
decidable finite-string equality.  The data needed for a Richardson-normalizer
claim are the following three exactness conditions, for all \(r,s\in\RE\):
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Eq_{\RE}(r,s)&\Rightarrow
  \Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))=\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(s)),\\
\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))=\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(s))&\Rightarrow
  \Eq_{\RE}(r,s),\\
\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))=\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(s))&\Longleftrightarrow
  \Norm(\tau(r))=\Norm(\tau(s)).
\end{aligned}
\]
These requirements are the missing data behind any phrase such as
``Richardson normalizer obstruction'': the exact Richardson source theorem, the
total computable translation, the target semantic equality, and the decidable
finite normal forms must all be named.

\begin{lemma}[Richardson reduction against identity-exact finite normalizers]
\label{lem:richardson-normalizer-reduction}
Suppose there are a target fragment \(\TE\), a total computable translation
\(\tau:\RE\to\TE\), and a total computable finite target normalizer \(\Norm\)
such that, for all \(r,s\in\RE\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\Eq_{\RE}(r,s)\Rightarrow
  \Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))=\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(s)),\\
&\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))=\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(s))\Rightarrow
  \Eq_{\RE}(r,s),\\
&\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))=\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(s))\Longleftrightarrow
  \Norm(\tau(r))=\Norm(\tau(s)).
\end{aligned}
\]
Then there is a decision procedure for \(\Eq_{\RE}(r,0)\).  Consequently no
paper-local syntax catalogue, finite witness list, or target normal form can be
used as a semantic Richardson identity oracle unless it supplies these
hypotheses and thereby accepts this decidability consequence.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Given \(r\in\RE\), compute the two finite target objects \(\tau(r)\) and
\(\tau(0)\), normalize both, and compare the resulting finite strings.  This is
an algorithm because \(\tau\) and \(\Norm\) are total computable and the target
normal forms have decidable finite equality.

If
\(\Norm(\tau(r))=\Norm(\tau(0))\), the normalizer exactness hypothesis gives
\(\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))=\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(0))\), and the semantic reflection
hypothesis gives \(\Eq_{\RE}(r,0)\).  If the normal forms are unequal, the
contrapositive of normalizer exactness gives
\(\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(r))\ne\Sem_{\TE}(\tau(0))\).  The semantic preservation
hypothesis would imply this target equality from \(\Eq_{\RE}(r,0)\), so by
contraposition \(\neg\Eq_{\RE}(r,0)\).  Thus the displayed finite-string
comparison decides \(\Eq_{\RE}(r,0)\) for arbitrary \(r\in\RE\), contradicting
Theorem~\ref{thm:richardson-source-input}.  The reduction never uses equality on
an empty common domain: the only source predicate is Richardson's own
\(\Eq_{\RE}\), and target equality is the explicitly supplied equality of
\(\Sem_{\TE}\)-objects.
\end{proof}

\section{Related work by interface}
\label{sec:related-work}
This paper is closest to proof-carrying and certificate-checking traditions:
proof-carrying code and foundational proof certificates separate a compact
certificate object from the trusted checker that validates it
\cite{Necula1997PCC,Appel2001FoundationalPCC,AppelMcAllester2001FPCCRecursiveTypes,ChihaniMillerRenaud2013FPC}.
Our contribution uses that separation at the publication boundary rather than
inside a programming-language or logical-kernel safety theorem.  The submitted
certificate proves only a finite Horn-coordinate closure, and only for the
coordinates whose premises are present.  It is not a proof of source-theory
truth, proof-assistant reconstruction, artifact semantics, or general
implementation soundness.

The work is also adjacent to formal-mathematics systems and LLM-assisted
formalization.  Lean 4, Lean, Isabelle/AFP, LeanDojo, Lean Copilot,
Draft--Sketch--Prove, DeepSeek-Prover, and Llemma provide theorem-prover
substrates, libraries, corpora, and proposal mechanisms for formalization
\cite{MouraUllrich2021Lean4,deMouraKADR2015Lean,BlanchetteHMN2015AFP,Wenzel2019IsabelleAFP,YangEtAl2023LeanDojo,SongYangAnandkumar2024LeanCopilot,JiangEtAl2023DraftSketchProve,XinEtAl2024DeepSeekProver,AzerbayevEtAl2023Llemma}.
Here such tools motivate the advisory-agent gate: generated suggestions remain
non-load-bearing until deterministic finite rows and a human-promotion boundary
place them in an accepted coordinate.

Artifact and reproducibility systems give stronger evidence units than the one
claimed here.  ReproZip, CODECHECK, RO-Crate, BagIt/RFC 8493, PROV-O, in-toto,
SLSA, DataLad, Nix/NixOS, Guix, CWL, Snakemake, Sumatra, Software Heritage
identifiers, the Software Citation Principles, and FAIR4RS can support archived
research objects, execution certificates, provenance graphs, supply-chain
attestations, rebuild environments, workflow descriptions, or software-identity
claims
\cite{ChirigatiRampinShashaFreire2016ReproZip,EglenEtAl2021CODECHECK,SoilandReyesEtAl2022ROCrate,KunzeEtAl2018BagItRFC8493,LeboEtAl2013PROVO,TorresAriasEtAl2019InToto,OpenSSF2023SLSA10,HalchenkoEtAl2021DataLad,DolstraDeJongeVisser2004Nix,DolstraLohPierron2010NixOS,ValletMichonneauTournier2022Guix,CWLCommunity2016,MoelderEtAl2021Snakemake,Davison2012SumatraContext,DavisonEtAl2014Sumatra,DiCosmoGruenpeterZacchiroli2020SWHID,SmithEtAl2016SoftwareCitationPrinciples,BarkerEtAl2022FAIR4RS}.
The archive contract in Theorem~\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}
is deliberately narrower: it asks only for ordinary-file byte equality, the
no-self digest table, the environment descriptor, locator identity, and the
explicit script-conformance premises needed by the two positive replay
coordinates.

For venue positioning we screened the official CICM 2021--2025 Springer
proceedings records and citable CICM workshop records using the terms
\auditcode{formalization}, \auditcode{certificate}, \auditcode{artifact},
\auditcode{LLM}, \auditcode{library}, \auditcode{database}, and
\auditcode{algorithm}
\cite{CICM2021Proceedings,CICM2021Workshops,CICM2022Proceedings,CICM2023Proceedings,CICM2024Proceedings,CICM2025Proceedings}.
Within that stated screen, the closest items were library-maintenance,
proof-assistant database, document-infrastructure, LLM-assistance, and verified
algorithm papers rather than publication-coordinate replay papers
\cite{BaanenEtAl2026GrowingMathlib,YamamichiEtAl2021MizarPlatform,KohlhaseMuellerSchaefer2021STeX,BauerEtAl2024LeanHoG,Karatarakis2024Deligne,HorowitzEtAl2026Herald,BrinichJohnson2024QFT,BartolettiBonzioFerrara2026Semigroups,BentkampAvigad2021VerifiedOptimization}.
The claim is only that no closer item was found by that stated screen.  It is
not an assertion about unqueried databases, later metadata changes, uncited
pages, or differently phrased papers.  For CICM 2025, rows are named by event
year; the bibliography distinguishes the Springer publication and copyright
metadata.

Classical Richardson undecidability, exact-real and symbolic-algebra systems,
Zeckendorf numeration, and finite-automata/rational-series theory provide
background for optional supplement branches
\cite{Richardson1968,Caviness1970,Wang1974,Mueller2001IRRAM,Lambov2007RealLib,Johansson2017Arb,Johansson2021Calcium,Hart2010FLINT,ZimmermannEtAl2018SageMath,MeurerEtAl2017SymPy,Zeckendorf1972,Frougny1999,AhlbachUsatineFrougnyPippenger2013,Idziaszek2020,AlloucheShallit2003,KauersPaule2011}.
Those branches are not main-paper evidence and do not add public coordinates.

\section{Conclusion}
The useful abstraction is not an assertion about who or what produced a theorem
candidate.  It is the finite publication-coordinate route that assigns each
artifact to the strongest claim it is entitled to support.  For the current
PDF-level article the unconditional claim is the printed six-coordinate finite
specification and Horn calculation
\[
(\qlex,q_{\mathrm{rgs}},q_{\mathrm{src}},q_{\mathrm{art}},q_{\mathrm{ext}},
q_{\mathrm{ven}})=(\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},
\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false},\mathsf{false})
\]
for the base displayed relation.  Adjoining the explicit scanner-success
premise gives the intermediate finite relation with \(\qlex\) positive only as a
decoded begin-token substring witness.
Adjoining the external branch-by-branch \(\ScriptOKstageA\) premise after that
gives the conditional mathematical relation with \(q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\) positive.
The executable reading is outside the submitted package.  The theorem states
the additional premises that would be required: archive byte equality,
environment and stable-locator clauses, \(\ScanOK\), \(\RecordGateOK\),
\(\CertDAGOK\), \(\DigestOKstageA\), and \(\ScriptOKstageA\).  No
source-rebuild, artifact-semantic, external-archive, venue-status,
executable-script, or whole-program implementation-soundness coordinate is
positive in this paper.

\paragraph{Supplement boundary.}
The source package retains
\artifactpath{appendix_full_technical_ledger.tex} and the material below this
file's first \verb|\end{document}| as non-load-bearing supplement text.  Those
sources collect Zeckendorf transport, Richardson variants, factorial-spike
stress tests, long ledger closures, and generated audit wrappers.  They are not
compiled into the main article and do not add public coordinates beyond
Theorems~\ref{thm:typed-verified-core-hierarchy},
\ref{thm:certificate-record-checker-spec}, and
\ref{thm:replay-instantiation-archive-contract}.

\bibliographystyle{splncs04}
\bibliography{references}

\end{document}

\section{Related work by interface}
The audit route is adjacent to several literatures but imports only bounded
interfaces from them.  Table~\ref{tab:cicm-theorem-comparison} gives the
theorem-by-theorem comparison with a fixed recent CICM corpus.  The corpus and
screen are part of the method rather than an informal literature sample.  The
source records are the official Springer CICM proceedings volumes for event
years 2021--2025, together with citable CICM workshop volumes in the same window
when an official CEUR or proceedings record is available
\cite{CICM2021Proceedings,CICM2021Workshops,CICM2022Proceedings,CICM2023Proceedings,CICM2024Proceedings,CICM2025Proceedings}.
For CICM 2025 the event year is 2025; the Springer book record for the cited
volume lists 2025 publication dates and 2026 copyright metadata.  The comparison
rows therefore label CICM items by event year, while the bibliography records
the Springer volume metadata.
The search strings, inclusion rules, source-set locators, included rows,
per-query retained outputs, excluded categories, search date, and boundary
statement are printed in
Tables~\ref{tab:cicm-corpus-protocol}--\ref{tab:cicm-query-output-transcript}.
Those PDF tables are the operative bibliographic transcript.  The paper-local
machine-readable file \artifactpath{certificates/cicm_search_transcript.json} is
only a convenience copy of the same finite screen; it is not the sole evidence
for the comparison.  The screen is therefore reproducible as a stated finite
bibliographic procedure from the PDF, but it is not an independent proof that no
differently indexed, later-updated, uncited, or unqueried database record exists.
Table~\ref{tab:cicm-theorem-comparison} then records, for each row, the closest
included CICM item and the reason the row remains weakly related when no closer
item was found by that stated screen.  Because the CICM corpus is narrower than
the artifact and proof-certificate infrastructure relevant to this paper's
route, Table~\ref{tab:outside-infrastructure-theorem-comparison} gives a second
theorem-by-theorem comparison against non-CICM reproducibility, software-identity,
supply-chain, workflow, and proof-certificate systems.  Table
\ref{tab:execution-identity-comparison} then records the same infrastructure at
the level of evidence units and claim boundaries.  The paragraphs after the
tables give the older background streams.

The proof-carrying-code line is the closest conceptual ancestor of the replay
surface: an artifact carries a finite object that a smaller checker can inspect
\cite{Necula1997PCC,Appel2001FoundationalPCC,AppelMcAllester2001FPCCRecursiveTypes}.
Foundational proof certificates refine this separation between certificate
syntax and checking kernel~\cite{ChihaniMillerRenaud2013FPC}.  The Stage--A
theorem uses that lineage only at publication-coordinate level: its submitted
certificate is a finite Horn certificate for
\(\{\qlex,q_{\mathrm{rgs}}\}\), with \(\qlex\) stored under the legacy artifact
atom \texttt{qinv}, not a proof of source-theory
correctness, proof-assistant reconstruction, or artifact semantics.
Reproducibility packaging systems such as ReproZip
\cite{ChirigatiRampinShashaFreire2016ReproZip} motivate the replay-command
interface, but the package here is weaker and more explicit: replay checks
digest-bound finite rows and fixed Horn closure, not clean-machine equivalence.
Lean 4 is the current extensible theorem-prover/programming substrate relevant
to many formalization workflows~\cite{MouraUllrich2021Lean4}, and LLM-in-Lean
tools such as LeanDojo and Lean Copilot
\cite{YangEtAl2023LeanDojo,SongYangAnandkumar2024LeanCopilot} motivate the
advisory-agent boundary: suggestions, repairs, and tactic proposals remain
non-load-bearing until a deterministic gate and human promotion row make a
finite certificate.  This paper does not provide a Lean 4 formalization,
Lean/BEDC rebuild, or axiom-purity audit.

\subsection{Independent execution, packaging, and software identity}
The replay boundary in this paper is deliberately narrower than the major
infrastructure used for computational reproducibility, artifact packaging,
supply-chain integrity, and certificate checking.  RO-Crate packages research
artefacts with machine-readable metadata, identifiers, provenance links, and
relations among data, software, and methods~\cite{SoilandReyesEtAl2022ROCrate}.
BagIt/RFC 8493 specifies a filesystem-oriented package with payload manifests,
tag files, checksums, and fetch metadata for transferring and storing digital
content~\cite{KunzeEtAl2018BagItRFC8493}.  PROV-O supplies a W3C ontology for
agents, activities, entities, and their provenance relations
\cite{LeboEtAl2013PROVO}.  These standards explain two nearby but stronger
interfaces: a bag-level transfer package and a graph-level provenance model.
The submitted Stage--A rows use neither; they use only path, digest, locator,
and boundary rows printed in this article.
In-toto records and verifies signed software-supply-chain layouts and link
metadata across the steps that produce deployable bytes
\cite{TorresAriasEtAl2019InToto}.  SLSA gives a supply-chain framework for
describing and hardening build provenance and artifact integrity across
software-production levels~\cite{OpenSSF2023SLSA10}.  DRAT-trim checks a
proof-object format for SAT refutations and can trim or emit dependency
information for such proofs~\cite{WetzlerHeuleHunt2014DRATTrim}.  Alethe and
Carcara give a modern SMT proof-format/checker route, and Lambdapi/Dedukti-style
work gives a logical-framework route for independently checked proof objects
\cite{AndreottiLachnittBarbosa2023Carcara,ColtellacciEtAl2026SMTLambdapi,Farber2022Kontroli,BoespflugCarbonneauxHermant2012LambdaPiUniversal}.
Guix gives transparent,
verifiable, and long-term reproducible computational environments by recording
the dependency graph and package definitions needed to rebuild software
\cite{ValletMichonneauTournier2022Guix}.  CWL and Snakemake standardize portable
or sustainable workflow descriptions~\cite{CWLCommunity2016,MoelderEtAl2021Snakemake};
Sumatra records experiment context and execution metadata for computational
experiments~\cite{Davison2012SumatraContext,DavisonEtAl2014Sumatra};
DataLad tracks code/data relationships and provenance across distributed
datasets~\cite{HalchenkoEtAl2021DataLad}; and the Software Citation Principles
state how software should be identified and credited~\cite{SmithEtAl2016SoftwareCitationPrinciples}.
These are stronger frameworks than
the checker specified here: they package a research object, attest a supply
chain, check a proof calculus, reconstruct an execution environment, define or
record a workflow, track a provenance graph, or provide a software citation
policy.  The present checker does none of those jobs.

CODECHECK asks an independent executor to run submitted code and issue a
certificate for the observed computation~\cite{EglenEtAl2021CODECHECK}.  Nix and
NixOS make software deployment and system configuration more reproducible by
describing dependencies functionally and isolating build products
\cite{DolstraDeJongeVisser2004Nix,DolstraLohPierron2010NixOS}.  Software
Heritage provides long-term archival source identifiers, including SWHIDs, for
referring to exact source-code objects~\cite{DiCosmoGruenpeterZacchiroli2020SWHID}.
FAIR4RS states findability, accessibility, interoperability, and reusability
principles for research software~\cite{BarkerEtAl2022FAIR4RS}.

This article uses a different evidence unit: a finite row is accepted only for
the predicate printed in the row and only after the raw bytes named by the row
match the submitted digest convention.  That choice is a smaller
publication-coordinate design pattern, not a replacement for the systems above.
Clean-machine reproducibility, archival identity, and full research-software
FAIRness are therefore upgrade coordinates in Table~\ref{tab:scope-register},
with the locator condition further specified in
Table~\ref{tab:artifact-locator-status}.
